# Content plan

The spec the content routine executes. It is also the editorial standard
for anything a human writes here.

## How the loop works

There is no status column to keep in sync. The routine reads this file,
looks at which slugs already exist under
`apps/web/src/content/blog/{es,en,fr,pt,de,it,pl,pt-br,es-mx}/`, and takes the next
unwritten target. What is published *is* the state.

Twenty-eight targets a week, four runs every day, weekends included:
04:00, 08:00 and 11:00 UTC take a comparison each, 16:00 UTC takes a topic
post. **That is about seventy files, not twenty-eight.** A
comparison is one market, a topic is every language the site publishes, so a
normal week is 21 + 7×7. The two regional variants do not multiply that: they
inherit the parent's topic posts and only get a file of their own when the
market changes the answer. The two kinds are written differently — see *Two
queues* and *Topic posts are guides*.

**A target is done when it exists in every directory it was due in.** A
comparison is due in one, a topic post in seven, and a country-locked row in the
one its own row names. Partial coverage is not done: a topic that reached `es`
and `en` but not `de` is still the next target, and the run fills the gaps
instead of starting something new. Without that rule, doubling the cadence just
means the newest markets get skipped faster and nobody notices, because the
target looks written.

Every run, one target:

1. Read the hour (`date -u +%H`) and pick the queue from it: before 12 a
   comparison, from 12 on a topic post. The weekday decides nothing.
2. Take the next target in that queue that is not done.
3. Research it **this run**, from primary sources. Not from memory.
4. Write it in the languages that target's row lists. See *Which
   languages a post gets written in* — it is not always all of them.
5. Verify every factual claim.
6. Publish. Every run ends with the post on main, never parked in a PR.
   If something needs a human (see *Where the routine publishes and
   flags*), publish anyway and flag it in the notification.

## The rule that matters

**Never state a fact about a competitor that does not come from a page
they publish themselves.**

Comparative advertising in Spain is regulated (Ley de Competencia
Desleal, art. 10): comparisons must be objective, verifiable, about
relevant and representative features, and must not denigrate. This site
is published by a real company. A wrong price copied from a blog is a
letter from a lawyer.

In practice:

- **Prices.** Only if the vendor publishes them. Almost none do — say
  exactly that: "no publica precios públicamente (consultado el
  <fecha>)". That sentence is verifiable, it is true of nearly the whole
  market, and it is the strongest thing on the page.
- **Never cite an aggregator blog for a price.** They contradict each
  other and several are written by competitors. One puts Gesden at
  "99 €/mes", another at "licencia perpetua de 2.000-3.000 €". Neither
  is Infomed.
- **Features.** Only from the vendor's own product pages, with the URL
  and the date consulted.
- **No adjectives about competitors.** "Desktop, Windows only" is a fact
  and lands harder than "anticuado" anyway. If a sentence would sting
  read aloud to their sales team, cut it.
- **Anything unverifiable is dropped**, not softened. If dropping it
  guts the post, rebuild the post around what survives verification and
  publish that; tell the user in the notification what was cut and why.

### Which official law sources actually answer

Two of the obvious ones do not, and a run that does not know this burns
most of its budget finding out. Checked again on 12 October 2026:

- **Légifrance returns 403 to everything**, every URL shape, every user
  agent, from both WebFetch and curl. It is the origin blocking
  datacentre traffic, not the egress proxy. **For France, source the
  profession's duties from the ONCD** (`ordre-chirurgiens-dentistes.fr`
  publishes dentist-specific PDFs that quote the code article by number
  and reproduce the déontologie text) **and the practice guidance from
  HAS**. Both are official bodies, neither is a blog, and the ONCD
  document is better aimed at this reader than the code text anyway.
- **Normattiva serves only its navigation tree.** Article bodies load
  by script, and `caricaArticolo`, `caricaAttoIntero`, the `!vig=`
  permalinks and the ELI paths all come back without the text. **For
  Italy, use the FNOMCeO Codice di deontologia medica** (it governs
  "l'esercizio professionale del medico chirurgo e dell'odontoiatra", so
  it binds dentists) **and official regional linee guida**, which quote
  the code articles verbatim in their footnotes. Brocardi, Filodiritto
  and Studiocataldi carry the article texts and are barred: they are
  legal publishers, not official sources.

These do answer, and are worth going to first: `boe.es` (consolidated
texts, fetch with `&tn=0` and grep locally, the pages are ~1 MB),
`gesetze-im-internet.de` (**checked 23 October 2026: the advice here was
backwards that run. curl was reset by the egress proxy on every attempt and
user agent, and WebFetch answered. Try both before giving up on a German
provision. **A later run the same day found neither working**: WebFetch
answered 503 to `__630c.html` on three attempts, curl was reset, and
`recht.bund.de` 404s. So this source is intermittent within a single day,
and a German provision it will not serve is dropped per the rule below,
not sourced from dejure or another legal publisher. For zahnärztliches
Berufsrecht the chamber sources answer reliably: the BZÄK
`BZAEK_MBO-Kommentar.pdf` and the Berufsordnung PDF, both on
`lzk-bw.de`**), `legislation.gov.uk`,
`api.sejm.gov.pl/eli/acts/<publisher>/<year>/<pos>/text.html`
for Poland with `/eli/acts/search?title=` to find the current tekst
jednolity (**the `text.html` endpoint returned 200 with a zero-byte body on
23 October 2026; `text.pdf` served the full consolidated act, so fall back to
the PDF and `pdftotext` it**), and `pgdlisboa.pt` for consolidated Portuguese law, published
by the Procuradoria-Geral Distrital de Lisboa. `diariodarepublica.pt` is
a JavaScript shell that returns 2 KB to every client, so Portugal goes
through pgdlisboa.

Two more that answered on 23 October 2026 and are worth knowing about.
**Italian articles can be read from `gazzettaufficiale.it` even though
Normattiva will not serve them**: open the act's `vediMenuHTML` index, take
the `caricaArticolo` link for the article you want, and fetch that. That is
how the 2023 amendment to art. 2 of legge 409/1985 was sourced. And for
**German case law, `nrwe.justiz.nrw.de` returns 503 to WebFetch and resets
the connection to curl**, but the Landeszahnärztekammer Baden-Württemberg
Urteilsdatenbank on `lzk-bw.de` reproduces the Leitsätze and the citation
chain of the decisions that matter, and it is a chamber of public law rather
than a legal publisher. The Bundeszahnärztekammer's own
`BZAEK_MBO-Kommentar.pdf`, hosted on the same site, is the best German source
on the limits of the Approbation and quotes § 1 Abs. 3 ZHG directly.

**Checked on 27 October 2026, writing the consumo inspection row.**
**`madrid.es` returns 403 to curl on every user agent, and WebFetch reaches it.**
WebFetch cannot read the PDFs there (the text sits in `/FlateDecode` streams and
the summariser sees binary), but it saves the file it fetched and prints the
path, and `pdftotext -layout` on that path reads it in full. So the route to a
madrid.es PDF is WebFetch to download, `pdftotext` to read, not curl. That is
how the Ayuntamiento de Madrid dental inspection protocol and its campaign
evaluation were sourced. Worth trying wherever an origin blocks datacentre
traffic but WebFetch gets through.

**Checked again on 24 October 2026, writing the EU accessibility row.** Four
sources answered and four did not, and the pattern is worth reusing because
three of them are whole-act PDFs rather than article pages.

- **Poland: `eli.gov.pl` serves the full act as a PDF** and is the official
  Dziennik Ustaw portal. `eli.gov.pl/eli/DU/<year>/<pos>/ogl/pol/pdf` returned
  the 29-page consolidated act, and `pdftotext -layout` then greps cleanly.
  Prefer it to the `api.sejm.gov.pl` `text.html` endpoint, which the
  23 October note already records as serving a zero-byte body. The
  `api.sejm.gov.pl/eli/acts/DU/<year>/<pos>/text.pdf` shape did work for
  Prawo przedsiębiorców.
- **Italy: the whole Gazzetta issue PDF is the fastest route**, not the
  `caricaArticolo` loop. `gazzettaufficiale.it/eli/gu/<yyyy>/<mm>/<dd>/<issue>/sg/pdf`
  returned the complete d.lgs. 82/2022. Run `pdftotext` **without**
  `-layout` for GU PDFs: the two-column layout interleaves columns otherwise
  and the article text comes out unreadable.
- **`legislation.gov.uk` answers normally**, per section and per schedule, and
  is the source for anything about the Equality Act 2010.
- **`ada.gov` answers** and is the official US source. Note that its guidance
  pages state they are not legally binding, so cite them as the Department's
  position rather than as the standard.
- **`eur-lex.europa.eu` could not be read at all this run**: the HTML, TXT and
  ELI URL shapes all returned an empty body to WebFetch, and curl got HTTP 202
  with zero bytes. The directive text was therefore never quoted directly; each
  post cites its own national transposition, which is the better source anyway.
- **Légifrance still returns 403**, confirming the note above, so the French
  copy of the accessibility row was not written.
- **`diariodarepublica.pt` is still a JavaScript shell** and returned an empty
  body through both the `data.dre.pt` ELI redirect and the direct detail URL.
  pgdlisboa does not appear to carry this decree. Portugal was dropped for that
  row. Careful with secondary sources here: Portugal's accessibility decree and
  Italy's share the number 82/2022, and at least one vendor accessibility
  statement found this run cites the Italian one as Portuguese law.
- **`gesetze-im-internet.de` failed four times in a row** (503 to WebFetch on
  `/bfsg/`, `/bfsg/BJNR297000021.html` and `/bfsg/__1.html`, connection reset to
  curl), and `recht.bund.de` 404s. Every other copy of the BFSG that surfaced is
  a legal publisher or an interest group, so the German copy of the row was not
  written rather than sourced from one.

**Checked again on 24 October 2026, filling the `fr`, `pt` and `de` copies of
that same accessibility row.** All three had been dropped the run before for
want of an official source, and all three turned out to be reachable by another
route. The pattern worth reusing: when the consolidated statute is blocked, go
to the body that enforces the statute, or to the official gazette PDF.

- **Portugal is not blocked after all: the ELI address with a `/pdf` suffix
  serves the official gazette.** `data.dre.pt/eli/<type>/<no>/<year>/<mm>/<dd>/p/dre/pt/pdf`
  redirects to `files.diariodarepublica.pt` and returned the full Diário da
  República issue (2.2 MB for d.l. 82/2022, 1.3 MB for Portaria 220/2023). The
  `/html` shape on the same ELI path still returns only the 2 KB OutSystems
  shell, which is what the note above recorded. **So Portugal goes through
  `data.dre.pt` with `/pdf`, not through pgdlisboa, and the earlier conclusion
  that Portugal had to be dropped was wrong.** Run `pdftotext -layout` on these:
  the Portaria sets the requirements in a two-column "Requisitos previstos /
  Exemplos indicativos" table that interleaves unreadably without it (the
  opposite of the Italian GU advice above).
- **Germany: `gesetze-im-internet.de` failed a fifth and sixth time** (503 to
  WebFetch, `Recv failure: Connection reset by peer` to curl on three URL
  shapes), and the proxy's own `recentRelayFailures` listed the host. Two
  official routes answered instead. **The Bundestag document server serves
  statute text and its Begründung as PDF**: `dserver.bundestag.de/btd/19/286/1928653.pdf`
  is the government bill for the BFSG, 102 pages, and carries § 2 Nr. 17 and
  Nr. 26 verbatim plus the reasoning on what the e-commerce category covers.
  It is the bill rather than the consolidated text, so **confirm the paragraph
  numbering against the enforcing authority before quoting it**. That authority
  is the second route: the **MLBF** (Marktüberwachungsstelle der Länder, an
  Anstalt öffentlichen Rechts) at `mlbf-barrierefrei.de`, whose FAQ and
  "Informationen für Unternehmen" pages state the closed list of covered
  services, the Kleinstunternehmen thresholds, the § 38 transitional dates and
  the Anlage 3 information duty. `gewerbeaufsicht.bayern.de` corroborates dates
  and competence. `marktueberwachungsstelle.de` does not resolve; the site is
  `mlbf-barrierefrei.de`.
- **France: Légifrance still returns 403**, so that note stands for a third
  run. **`francenum.gouv.fr` is the find**: the State's business-digitisation
  service publishes a detailed page on e-commerce accessibility that gives the
  official definition, the thresholds, the five items of the annex to art.
  D. 412-57, the annex to D. 412-60, and the contravention amounts. **Arcom PDFs
  quote code articles verbatim** with footnote references, and
  `entreprendre.service-public.gouv.fr` answers (note `service-public.fr`
  301-redirects to `service-public.gouv.fr`; WebFetch returns the redirect
  rather than following it). **`economie.gouv.fr` returns 403 to both WebFetch
  and curl**, so the DGCCRF's own fiches are not readable from here even though
  the DGCCRF is the enforcing authority. **`handicap.gouv.fr` PDFs load through
  WebFetch but not curl**, which returns a 6 KB interstitial.
- **EUR-Lex could not be read again, and neither could the Parliament.** The
  HTML, TXT, PDF and ELI shapes on `eur-lex.europa.eu`, plus
  `data.europa.eu/eli/...` and `europarl.europa.eu/doceo/...`, all returned
  **HTTP 202 with a zero-byte body**. `ec.europa.eu` does answer. Treat the
  directive text as unavailable and cite the national transposition, which is
  the better source anyway; where a transposition defers its definitions to the
  directive, as Portugal's art. 3.º does, say so in the post and decline to
  quote the definition.

One correction to this row's own source list, which named the wrong French
instrument: **France transposed the directive into the Code de la consommation,
art. L. 412-13** (by art. 16 of loi n° 2023-171 du 9 mars 2023), with décret
n° 2023-931 and arts. D. 412-57 and D. 412-60. Loi 2005-102 art. 47 is the
public-sector website rule and is not the provision that binds a cabinet. The
row also suggested reading the BFSG "on dejure.org", which this document bars
as a legal publisher; use the two German routes above instead.

**Checked again on 25 October 2026, writing the collaborator/Scheinselbstständigkeit
row.** Four findings, one of them a new French route worth keeping.

- **`gesetze-im-internet.de` answered curl this run, which inverts the note above
  again.** `/sgb_4/__7.html` and `/sgb_4/__7a.html` returned 200 to curl on the
  first attempt; `/sgb_4/__25.html` and `/stgb/__266a.html` were reset on the
  first attempt and both returned 200 on the second. So the 25 October reading is:
  **the host is intermittent per URL, not per run, and the second attempt is
  usually the one that works.** Prefer curl plus local tag-stripping over WebFetch
  here: WebFetch summarises and caps quotes at 125 characters, which is useless
  when the post needs the provision verbatim. The Zahnärzte-ZV lives at
  `zo-zahn_rzte/__32b.html`, not at any `zahnaerzte-zv` or `zahn_rzte-zv` shape.
- **The ONCD publishes its *Guide des contrats* as well as the note d'information,
  and it is the France answer for contract duties.** `ordre-chirurgiens-dentistes.fr/download/55973`
  is a 141-page PDF that yields to `pdftotext -layout` and carries the
  art. L. 4113-9 one-month communication duty, the conseil départemental's
  conformity check, the disciplinary sanction in the last paragraph of L. 4113-9,
  the contrat type versus modèle de contrat distinction, and the conciliation
  clause referring to art. R. 4127-259 as it appears in every model contract.
  Caveat: that edition quotes 2017 VAT thresholds, so date-sensitive figures in it
  are stale even though the statutory duties are not.
- **Légifrance returned 403 for a fourth run**, this time behind a Cloudflare
  interstitial, so the note above stands. For a Cour de cassation decision,
  `courdecassation.fr/recherche-judilibre` is a JavaScript shell that returns an
  empty body, and the fallback used here was **Juricaf** (`juricaf.org`), the
  jurisprudence base of the francophone cassation courts edited by AHJUCAF, which
  served the full arrêt including the Réponse de la Cour. It is an association of
  the supreme courts rather than a commercial legal publisher, so it is not barred
  by the rule above, but say in the post where the text was read.
- **Check the holding, not the moyen, before citing a decision.** This row's own
  note suggested Cass. soc. 21 September 2022, n° 21-14.171 for the requalification
  test. It is nothing of the kind: it is a procedural decision holding that a
  prior-conciliation clause is a fin de non-recevoir which suspends the
  prescription, and the pourvoi was rejected. It was still worth citing, on a
  different point than the one it was filed under.


**Checked again on 26 October 2026, writing the compulsory-insurance row in ES,
DE and PL.** Everything answered, and three mechanics are worth reusing.

- **`eli.gov.pl/eli/DU/<year>/<pos>/ogl/pol/pdf` needs `curl -L`.** Without it
  the response is a 134-byte HTML meta-refresh, which `file` reports as HTML and
  which looks like a block. It redirects to
  `eli.gov.pl/api/acts/DU/<year>/<pos>/text.pdf`, so either follow redirects or
  fetch that address directly. Three acts came back this way, at 1.4 MB, 171 kB
  and 1.1 MB, all clean under `pdftotext -layout`.
- **Find the current Polish tekst jednolity before fetching one.**
  `api.sejm.gov.pl/eli/acts/search?title=<words>` lists the Obwieszczenia with
  their year and pos, newest first. The ustawa o działalności leczniczej is at
  **Dz. U. 2026 poz. 156**, not the 2025 poz. 450 an earlier entry used, and the
  ustawa o zawodach lekarza i lekarza dentysty at **Dz. U. 2026 poz. 37**.
- **`gesetze-im-internet.de` answered on the first attempt**, for
  `/sgb_5/__95e.html` and `/sgb_5/__72.html`, via curl plus local tag-stripping.
  That is now the fourth consecutive entry recommending curl over WebFetch for
  this host, and the retry rule from 26 October still applies to the ZHG paths.
- **The chamber route for German zahnärztliches Berufsrecht has moved.** The
  `BZAEK_MBO-Kommentar.pdf` path this file records under
  `/fileadmin/user_upload/Dokumente/Zahnaerzte/Recht/` now 404s. The working
  address is
  `lzk-bw.de/fileadmin/user_upload/Downloads/Recht/3.Berufsrecht/BZAEK_MBO-Kommentar.pdf`,
  1.4 MB, 5th edition November 2024, and it is the best German source on the
  Berufshaftpflicht because it states what the Berufsordnung does **not** say.
- **Check a statute's chapter before relying on a cross-reference.** § 72 Abs. 1
  Satz 2 SGB V extends «die Vorschriften dieses Kapitels» to Zahnärzte, so the
  bridge only reaches § 95e if both sit in the same chapter. They do: the
  official index puts the Viertes Kapitel at §§ 69 to 140h. Verify that from the
  index rather than assuming it, because the whole German post rests on it.

**Checked again on 26 October 2026, writing the permits row in ES and PL.** Every
source answered, and the two corrections below are to this file's own row rather
than to a host.

- **The ES half of that row had the two authorisations the wrong way round.** The
  row says the authorisation "is **previa**, so it is sought before installing and
  not before opening". Read from the BOE this run, art. 3.2 RD 1277/2003 says the
  opposite about which act is universal: the **funcionamiento** authorisation is the
  one «se exigirá con carácter preceptivo por las comunidades autónomas de modo
  previo al inicio» of the activity, so it is precisely the one sought before
  opening, while the **instalación** one «podrá ser exigida» by each comunidad, as
  may the **cierre** one. So three of the four acts in art. 2.e) are autonomic
  options and only funcionamiento is national. Art. 3.2 also carries two things the
  row did not mention and that are better clinic-side material than the ones it did:
  the funcionamiento authorisation is granted per centro **and per each servicio of
  its oferta asistencial**, renewable with whatever periodicity each comunidad sets,
  and the modificación one covers a change of **titularidad** as well as of structure
  or oferta. Art. 3.3 is the sourced answer to the municipal-licence confusion: a
  non-health administration competent to authorise a centre's puesta en marcha must
  «recabar que aquél cuente previamente con la autorización de funcionamiento».
- **The PL half's 14-day deadline is in the statute, not only in the inspection
  protocols.** The row says to read the Minister of Health rozporządzenie before the
  post states it. No rozporządzenie was needed: **art. 107 ust. 1 ustawy o
  działalności leczniczej** carries it verbatim, with art. 107 ust. 2 setting the
  penalty at up to ten times the minimalne wynagrodzenie (48 060 zł in 2026, from
  Dz. U. 2025 poz. 1242 § 1). What the rozporządzenie under **art. 105 ust. 4**
  actually governs is the *szczegółowy zakres danych objętych wpisem*, which is a
  different question and was left unread and said so in the post. The row's main
  finding, art. 106 ust. 1 pkt 1 and 2 splitting the register keeper by legal form,
  checked out verbatim, and art. 111 ust. 1 closes it: the organ that keeps the
  register is the organ that inspects, so the legal form chosen on day one picks
  both.
- **`boe.es` answered normally for both acts** with `&tn=0` plus local tag
  stripping. RD 1277/2003 came back at 172 kB, not the ~1 MB this file warns about,
  and anexo I and anexo II are both in that single consolidated page, so C.2.5.1 and
  U.44 need no second fetch.
- **`eli.gov.pl` served three acts this run, and the `curl -L` note from the
  insurance row holds.** `eli.gov.pl/eli/DU/<year>/<pos>/ogl/pol/pdf` returned the
  ustawa o działalności leczniczej (Dz. U. 2026 poz. 156, 61 pages), the pomieszczenia
  rozporządzenie (tekst jednolity Dz. U. 2022 poz. 402) and the minimum-wage
  rozporządzenie (Dz. U. 2025 poz. 1242, one page), all clean under
  `pdftotext -layout`. `api.sejm.gov.pl/eli/acts/search?title=` is still the way to
  find which tekst jednolity is current.
- **Two Spanish regional sources answered and one did not, and the dead one matters
  because this file's row named it.** `euskadi.eus` serves the dental authorisation
  trámite in full, with Basque-specific deadlines (two years to apply for
  funcionamiento after the instalación notification, five years' validity, renewal
  three months before, one month for non-substantial modifications, three months'
  notice to close); note the page is ~120 kB of text and WebFetch reads only the
  first 100 kB. The **Andalusian inspection protocol** for C.2.5.1/U.44, version 6
  of August 2022, is on `cdn.juntadeandalucia.es` and is the strongest clinic-side
  document found: it is the inspector's own form, it checks that the premises match
  the scaled plans in the expediente, and it reproduces which art. 15.2 Ley 41/2002
  contents are required outside hospitalisation. WebFetch returns it as binary, so
  take the saved file and `pdftotext` it. **`comunidad.madrid`'s
  `lista_de_verificacion_para_clinicas_dentales.pdf` now 404s**, serving a Drupal
  error page that `file` reports as HTML.
- **Castilla-La Mancha was dropped for want of a primary source.** `jccm.es` serves
  only the August 2025 *borrador* of the orden on requisitos técnico-sanitarios de
  clínicas dentales; the enacted Orden 162/2025 of 28 October (DOCM 11 November 2025)
  is corroborated only by vLex, which this file bars as a legal publisher, and by
  INAP's news page, and `docm.jccm.es`'s search returned HTTP 500. Two regional
  examples were enough for the post, so no third was forced.

**Checked again on 27 October 2026, writing the foreign-qualification row in ES
and DE.** Queue A had no unwritten target left this run, so the morning run took a
Queue B topic, per *Two queues*. Five findings.

- **Queue A is drained as of 27 October 2026.** Every row in A1 to A8 exists in the
  directory its section names, A9 is empty, and the only two Queue A slugs without a
  file are the two retired French rows (`dentalpin-vs-dentra`, removed 6 September
  2026, and `dentalpin-vs-weclever-dental`, folded into `dentalpin-vs-weclever` on
  4 October 2026). A run that counts `dentalpin-vs-*` filenames alone will get the
  rotation wrong, because the head terms (`zahnarztsoftware-kostenlos`,
  `logiciel-dentaire-gratuit` and the rest) are Queue A rows too and belong in the
  per-directory count.
- **`gesetze-im-internet.de` behaved exactly as the 25 October note predicts.**
  `/zhg/__1.html` and `/zhg/__2.html` returned 200 to curl on the first attempt,
  `/zhg/__13.html` was reset on the first and returned 200 on the second. Fifth
  consecutive entry recommending curl plus local tag-stripping over WebFetch for this
  host, and the retry rule holds.
- **The ANECA criteria for Dentista have been reissued and this file's figures were
  incomplete.** The row records "the ANECA Resolución of 31 July 2024: five years of
  study and 540 hours of clinical training". The current document linked from
  `ciencia.gob.es` is `Criterios_ANECA_Dentista_abr2026.pdf`, signed by the Directora
  on **20 April 2026**, and its apartado Primero sets **two** thresholds, not one:
  at least **300 créditos ECTS or 3.480 horas presenciales** where the degree is not
  in ECTS, with a minimum duration of five years, **and** at least 540 hours of
  clinical training. The 3.480-hour figure is the half every secondary summary drops.
  Apartado Tercero adds that the clinical training must be a rotatorio clínico
  odontológico with a final competence assessment, on patients of all ages, under
  supervision.
- **"That page cites no Real Decreto" is right about the dentista page and wrong about
  the procedure.** `homologacion/dentista.html` indeed cites only the ANECA criteria
  and Orden CIN/2136/2008, but the parent page `validate/homologacion.html` cites
  **Real Decreto 889/2022, de 18 de octubre** (BOE-A-2022-17045), and that RD is where
  the two facts a clinic actually needs live: art. 5.1 (effects run from the date the
  credencial is issued, so there is no retroactivity) and art. 18.3 (six months to
  resolve, with negative silence). Read it from `boe.es` before citing it; `&tn=0`
  plus local tag-stripping answered on the first attempt, 147 kB.
- **A search summary reported that Dentista is not susceptible to homologación. It is
  wrong, and the primary page says so.** The note "No son susceptibles de
  homologación" on `validate/homologacion.html` attaches to Abogado/a and Procurador/a
  de los Tribunales; Dentista is the first entry under profesiones sanitarias and
  carries no note. This is the failure mode this file warns about in a new place: not
  an aggregator blog, but a summary of official pages. Open the page.

**Checked again on 27 October 2026, writing the higienista/Delegation row in ES
and DE.** Every source answered on the first attempt, so the findings are about the
row rather than the hosts.

- **`boe.es` answered four acts with `&tn=0` plus local tag-stripping**, all on the
  first attempt and all well under the ~1 MB this file warns about: RD 1594/1994
  (57 kB), Ley 10/1986 (57 kB), Ley 44/2003 (272 kB) and RD 769/2014 (288 kB).
  Worth knowing: **the consolidated page states its own amendment history** under
  "Seleccionar redacción". RD 1594/1994 offers only "Texto inicial publicado el
  08/09/1994", which is how this run could state that the decree has never been
  amended, and Ley 10/1986 names its last update as 25 July 2013. Check that
  selector before writing that a provision is current.
- **Watch the BOE id for Ley 10/1986: it is BOE-A-1986-7436, not -7050.** The
  wrong id resolves to a different 1994 Resolución and `act.php` 302-redirects to
  `doc.php` for it, which looks like a working fetch and is not one. Follow
  redirects and check the title of what came back.
- **`gesetze-im-internet.de` answered on the first attempt for both `/zhg/__1.html`
  and `/zhg/__18.html`**, via curl plus local tag-stripping. Sixth consecutive
  entry recommending curl over WebFetch for this host.
- **The BZÄK Delegationsrahmen is at the Zahnärztekammer Nordrhein** under
  `wp-content/uploads/praxisteam/ausbildung-zur-zum-zfa/`, 161 kB, 6 pages, clean
  under `pdftotext -layout`, Fassung Juli 2021. The `bzaek.de` page the PDF names
  as its own Quelle also returns 200 and confirms the title, so the version can be
  checked without relying on the mirror. This is a second chamber route alongside
  the `lzk-bw.de` ones above.
- **Two corrections to that row, both from the text.** It says both markets use "a
  closed statutory list plus a reserved core". **Germany's list is not closed**:
  § 1 Abs. 5 and Abs. 6 ZHG both open with «insbesondere», so the enumeration is
  expressly non-exhaustive, and a post built on a symmetry with Spain's closed
  art. 11.2 would be wrong on its central point. And it says the Delegationsrahmen
  supplies "what Spain's decree does not spell out, the conditions". **Spain has
  conditions, just not in RD 1594/1994**: art. 9.4 LOPS requires the terms of a
  delegation inside a team to be established *previamente* and the recipient's
  capacity to be «objetivable, siempre que fuere posible, con la oportuna
  acreditación». Check the general professional statute before concluding that a
  sector decree is the whole of the national answer.
- **Two smaller fixes.** Art. 11.3 RD 1594/1994 excludes «la dosificación de
  medicamentos» and «la extensión de recetas» as two separate items, not the row's
  "la dosificación y prescripción de medicamentos"; and the Delegationsrahmen
  carries a ninth condition the row's list omits, that the patient is aware the
  service is a delegated one.
- **The subgingival question the row flags is half-answered by the sources, and
  that half is quotable.** Both § 1 Abs. 5 ZHG and section 5 f) of the
  Delegationsrahmen name «Entfernung von weichen und harten sowie klinisch
  erreichbaren subgingivalen Belägen». Neither draws a line for subgingivale
  Kürettage as a therapeutic act, so the post states the first and declines the
  second, per the rule below.

When the text of a provision cannot be reached from an official source,
**the provision is dropped from the post and the post says so**, with
the reader sent to the body that can answer. That is what the Italian
maltreatment post does about the obbligo di referto. It is not softening
a claim, it is declining to make one.

## Which languages a post gets written in

**The two queues localise differently, and getting this backwards is the
most expensive mistake available here.**

**A comparison goes out in the language of the market where that vendor
sells, and nowhere else.** Someone choosing between Julie and us searches
`alternative logiciel Julie` in French. A Spanish translation of that post
reaches no one who is deciding, ranks for nothing, and still has to be
re-verified every time Julie changes a page. Three vendors × four
languages is nine pages that exist only to dilute the three that work.

The market decides the language, not the vendor's nationality:

- Gesden, Odontonet, Klinikare, Clinic Cloud → **ES**. Spain only.
- Dentalink, Doctocliq, DentalCore → **ES**. One post covers Spain and
  Latin America; there is no second language to write.
- Julie, LOGOSw, Veasy, Desmos → **FR**. France only.
- NewSoft, Doctusware, Gestrato, NoviGest, Simples Dental, Clinicorp,
  Dental Office, EasyDental → **PT**.
- Dentrix, Eaglesoft, Curve, Open Dental, Dentally, Software of
  Excellence → **EN**.
- charly, Dampsoft, CGM Z1.PRO, EVIDENT, ivoris, LinuDent → **DE**.
  Germany, and it carries into Austria and German-speaking Switzerland
  without a second post. Two rows are the exception and are written for
  one country: ZaWin is Swiss and power.dent is Austrian, because what
  decides those posts is the national billing regime, not the language.
- OrisDent, XDENT, GipoDental, AlfaDocs, Appuntoo, FELG Dent → **IT**.
  Italy only.
- Estomed, ProDentis, Medfile, SmartDental, Proassist, FELG Dent → **PL**.
  Poland only. Verified from their own sites on 28 August 2026; until
  then Polish took topic posts only. FELG Dent sells into both Poland and
  Italy from its own sites in each, so it is the one vendor with a row in
  two sections, and they are two different posts rather than one
  translated.

**A comparison for Brazil or Mexico goes in that market's directory**, not
in the parent's. `blog/pt-br/dentalpin-vs-simples-dental.md` for a Brazilian
vendor, `blog/es-mx/` for a Mexican one: the vocabulary, the currency and the
regulation in the post all belong to that market, and the reader who is
choosing is there. A vendor selling into Portugal or Spain stays in
`blog/pt/` and `blog/es/`.

A second language is only worth it when the vendor genuinely sells into
that second language's market, and then it is a **rewritten** post, not a
translation: different search phrasing, different regulation, different
"choose them if" list. If you cannot name the query it would rank for,
do not write it.

**A topic post goes out in every language the site publishes.** Backups,
no-shows, migrating without losing the clinical record, cloud versus your
own server: none of that is market-specific, all of it is searched in
every language, and there is no competitor claim to re-verify per copy.
This is where breadth pays.

Two things that look like translations and are not:

- **Head terms.** `software-clinica-dental-gratis`,
  `logiciel-dentaire-gratuit`, `zahnarztsoftware-kostenlos`,
  `software-gestionale-dentisti-gratis`, `free-dental-practice-software`
  are one target each, written natively, naming the vendors that market
  actually knows. Translating the Spanish one into French produces a page
  about Gesden for a reader who has never heard of it.
- **Regulation.** Verifactu is Spain. The French equivalent is the
  e-invoicing reform and its PDP obligations; the German one is the
  E-Rechnung mandate; the Italian one is the SDI plus the Sistema Tessera
  Sanitaria filing that every dental practice there already lives with.
  Four different posts, four sets of dates, four official sources. Never
  translate a compliance post across a border.

## The two regional variants

`pt-BR` and `es-MX` are not languages, they are the same language priced and
named for another market. They inherit their parent's dictionary and only
override what differs, in
`apps/web/src/i18n/{pt-br,es-mx}.overrides.json`. An override equal to its
parent fails the build, so the files stay down to what actually changed.

What changes, and it is always these four things:

- **Currency.** `R$` and `$0 MXN`, never euros. A euro price on a Brazilian
  page reads as a translated page whatever the grammar does.
- **Tax vocabulary.** Brazil issues `notas fiscais` and the module is
  `Faturamento`; Mexico keeps `IVA` and `facturación`, which it already uses.
- **Head terms.** The clinical record is `prontuário eletrônico` in Brazil and
  `expediente clínico` in Mexico, and both have their own URL
  (`/pt-br/funcionalidades/prontuario-eletronico/`). These are the two terms
  worth a slug of their own; the rest of the catalogue inherits.
- **Everyday words.** `celular` not `telemóvel`/`móvil`, `computadora` not
  `ordenador`, `contador` not `contabilista`/`gestoría`, `tela` not `ecrã`,
  `por padrão` not `por omissão`, and no enclitic pronouns in Brazilian copy
  (`nos escrever`, not `escrever-nos`).

Blog posts are inherited too: `/pt-br/blog/` serves the Portuguese posts under
Brazilian URLs, which is what hreflang is for. Writing
`blog/pt-br/<same-filename>.md` overrides that one post and leaves the rest
inherited, so a Brazilian rewrite of the migration guide does not hide the
other four.

Two things that stay Spanish and Portuguese from Spain and Portugal: the
`/es/` and `/pt/` copy. The variants are additions, not replacements.

## The words the profession actually uses

A post written in the vocabulary of a translator, not of a practice,
reads as imported however correct the grammar is. These are the terms
that give it away, and they are not optional:

| | ES | EN | FR | PT | DE | IT | PL |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Odontograma | odontograma | odontogram / dental chart | odontogramme | odontograma | Zahnschema | odontogramma | diagram zębowy |
| Periodontograma | periodontograma | periodontal chart | parodontogramme | periodontograma | Parodontalstatus | parodontogramma | periodontogram |
| Presupuesto | presupuesto | quote / treatment plan | devis | orçamento | Kostenvoranschlag | preventivo | kosztorys |
| Agenda | agenda | schedule | agenda | agenda | Terminkalender | agenda | kalendarz wizyt |
| Historia clínica | historia clínica | clinical record | dossier clinique | histórico clínico | Patientenakte | cartella clinica | dokumentacja medyczna |
| Gabinete | gabinete | operatory / chair | fauteuil | gabinete | Behandlungsstuhl | riunito | gabinet / fotel |
| Higienista | higienista | hygienist | assistante dentaire | higienista | Dentalhygienikerin / ZFA | igienista | higienistka |
| Recall | recall | recall | relance | recall | Recall | richiamo | zaproszenie na kontrolę |
| El software | software de gestión dental | practice management software | logiciel dentaire | software de gestão | Praxisverwaltungssoftware | gestionale odontoiatrico | program dla gabinetu stomatologicznego |

Two traps specific to the new languages:

- **German has no single word for "clínica".** A dental practice is a
  `Praxis`, and only a large multi-chair centre is a `Klinik`. Writing
  `Klinik` throughout addresses roughly nobody.
- **Italian "studio" is the practice, "riunito" is the chair.** A price
  per chair is `un canone per riunito`, which is the phrasing a dentist
  in Italy recognises from their current contract.
- **Polish `gabinet` is both the practice and the room.** A single-dentist
  practice is a `gabinet stomatologiczny`, which is also the head term
  people search; `klinika` reads as a large multi-chair centre. The chair
  itself is a `fotel`, so a per-chair fee is `opłata za fotel`. Prices go
  in złoty, not euros, or the page reads as translated.

## Every comparison post has this shape

1. **Who it is for** — one paragraph, honest, no preamble. Then a callout
   explaining how the comparison is sourced.
2. **"En treinta segundos"** — the verdict up front, in three short
   paragraphs: what the competitor is strongest at, what we are strongest
   at, and the one question that actually decides between them. A reader
   who stops here should already have their answer.
3. **What <competitor> is** — sourced, neutral, generous. A comparison
   that cannot describe the other product fairly convinces nobody.
4. **What DentalPin is.**
5. **Side-by-side table** — only verifiable rows, chips where there is a
   verdict. Deployment, licence, published price, data ownership, API,
   modules, compliance, and at least two rows the competitor wins.
6. **Choose <competitor> if…** — a real list, not a straw man. If they
   have 30 years of Spanish support and 400 migrations a year, say so.
   This section is why the post gets trusted and linked.
7. **Choose DentalPin if…**
8. **How migration actually works** — a numbered list, with the module.
9. **Sources** — every URL with the date consulted.

Section 6 is not a concession. It is the section that makes the other
seven credible, and it is the one the routine must never quietly drop.

## Making a post look designed

The posts are plain Markdown. There are no components to import and no
classes to write — `apps/web/src/styles/post.css` and the
`rehype-table-chips` plugin turn ordinary Markdown into the finished
article. Your side of that bargain is using the constructs below, because
a post that ignores them renders as an undifferentiated wall of text.

**The opening paragraph is set larger than the rest.** Make it a real
opening — one or two sentences that could stand alone as the answer to
the headline. Never waste it on throat-clearing.

**Blockquotes become callouts** — a bordered card with an accent rule.
Use them two or three times per post, for the thing a skimmer must not
miss: the caveat, the number that decides it, the mistake to avoid. Open
with a bolded phrase so the card has a headline:

```markdown
> **Infomed no publica tarifas en su web.** Remite a consultar con su
> coordinador de zona, y ninguno de los blogs que citan cifras es Infomed.
```

Four or five callouts in one post is noise. If everything is highlighted,
nothing is.

**Comparison tables are the centrepiece.** They break out wider than the
text, the header row is styled, and below 640px each row restacks into its
own card so both products stay visible on a phone. Two rules make that
work:

- **Dentalpin is always the last column.** It gets the tinted treatment.
- **Open a cell with `✓`, `✗` or `~`** and the whole cell becomes a
  coloured pill — green, red or amber. Use them only where the cell is a
  verdict, and only where the verdict is defensible. A row like
  `| Modelo | Licencia comercial | Open source |` is a fact, not a
  verdict: leave it plain.

```markdown
| | Gesden | Dentalpin |
|---|---|---|
| Modelo | Licencia comercial | Open source (BSL 1.1) |
| Precio publicado | ✗ No publica tarifas | ✓ 0 €, todo incluido |
| Módulos | ~ Varios se contratan aparte | ✓ Todos incluidos |
| Años en el mercado | ✓ Más de 30 | ✗ Desde 2026 |
```

**Give the competitor green chips where they earn them.** A table where
every green pill is ours and every red one theirs reads as marketing and
gets believed by nobody. Years on the market, support headcount, install
base — those are theirs, and saying so is what makes the rest credible.

**Sections are scannable.** Every `##` gets a rule above it and feeds the
page index, which appears once a post has more than two. Write headings
that say what the section concludes ("Elige Gesden si") rather than what
it is about ("Consideraciones").

**Bulleted points open with a bolded lead-in**, then the explanation.
The reader who only reads the bold parts should still get the argument.

**Numbered lists for anything sequential** — a migration, an install.
Prose describing four steps is four steps nobody can follow.

**Keep paragraphs to two or three sentences.** This is the single biggest
difference between a post that gets read and one that gets closed.

**No em dashes in the prose.** Use a comma, a colon, parentheses or a
full stop. The character reads as machine-written to the people most
likely to be evaluating us, and every alternative is clearer anyway. This
applies to every language. (It does not apply to this file, which is an
internal spec, or to a quoted source.)

**No marketing filler.** Nothing opens with "Descubre", "Desbloquea" or
"Potencia", and adjectives do not come in threes. Say the concrete thing:
"docker compose up" beats "una experiencia de instalación sencilla,
rápida y potente".

Everything else — links, code, the sources list — is styled
automatically. Do not hand-write HTML or CSS classes in a post.

## Every post carries images

A post with no image is not finished. It reads as a wall of text however
good the prose is, and it is the single most common thing wrong with what
this loop publishes.

**A comparison gets three visuals**, spread through the piece and never
two in a row: one in the first third, one where the post describes what
the product does, one in the second half.

**A topic post gets two, and they illustrate the reader's problem**, not
the product. The same screenshot works either way, it is the caption
that decides: an invoice list under a paragraph about numbering
continuity is a guide, the same image captioned "our invoicing module"
is an advert in a post that promised to help. The rule about mentioning
the product once, at the end, covers images too.

Markdown only:

```markdown
![Alt text saying what is on screen, in the post's language](/screenshots/dental-chart.png)

*One line telling the reader what they are looking at.*
```

- **The all-italic paragraph straight after an image becomes the
  caption.** That is the only place a paragraph should be entirely
  italic.
- **Alt text describes the screen**, in the post's language. Not
  "captura de Dentalpin".
- **A caption describes only what is visible.** Any claim about the
  product goes in the prose, where the sourcing rule applies to it.
- **Numbered lists already render as numbered cards**, so every
  sequential section is a diagram for free. That is one more reason to
  write the migration and the install as lists.

### The screenshots that exist

In `apps/web/public/screenshots/`, referenced as `/screenshots/<file>`.
They are the demo clinic, so no real patient data. The interface is in
Spanish except where noted, and they are used in every language, the
same way the home page does.

**Only reference a file that exists in that directory — `ls` it before
writing the image line.** A missing screenshot is not a placeholder in a
blog post, it is a broken image on a published page, and this table has
already drifted from the directory once: in August 2026 eleven posts and
the home hero referenced screenshots that were never exported, and every
one shipped broken.

| File | What it shows |
|---|---|
| `home.png` | Home: appointments today, who is in the clinic, overdue payments, recent patients, day timeline |
| `dental-chart.png` | Patient record: odontogram, clinical alerts, active plan, next appointment |
| `budgets.png` | One quote: treatments, totals, validity, linked plan |
| `invoices.png` | Invoice list with issued, paid, part paid, overdue and draft states |
| `patient-timeline.png` | Patient record, activity tab: clinical alerts, active plan, timeline filterable by visits, treatments, financial, communications |
| `ai-copilot.png` | The AI assistant with its workflows. **Interface in English**, so it fits an English post best |
| `patients.png` | Patient record, patient info tab |
| `periodontogram.png` | Periodontal chart with the six sites per tooth |
| `treatment-plan.png` | A treatment plan with its stages |
| `schedule-day.png` | Agenda, day view |
| `schedule-week.png` | Agenda, week view |
| `schedule-canban.png` | Agenda as a board |
| `reports.png` | Reports |

**The seven files listed above as pending were exported on 10 August 2026**
and are in the directory, so the posts that had to describe those screens in
prose can now show them. Nothing is pending today. That does not retire the
rule: `ls` the directory before writing an image line, because this table has
drifted from it in both directions.

Need something not on the list? Take it from the demo, log in as the
demo admin, and drop the PNG in that directory rather than describing
the screen in prose. Exporting one of the pending files above also
unblocks the feature page that waits for it.

### Diagrams

In `apps/web/public/diagrams/`, referenced as `/diagrams/<file>.svg`.
`install-stack.svg` is the self-hosted stack: browser, Caddy, API,
frontend, PostgreSQL.

One SVG serves all six languages, and it only does that if **every
label is a technical token that does not translate**: `Caddy`, `/api/*`,
`Nuxt`, `PostgreSQL`, `:443`. The moment a diagram needs a sentence in
it, it needs six files, and it is not worth it. Say it in prose instead
or use a table.

Add a diagram only where a structure is genuinely hard to describe:
what talks to what, what runs where, what happens in which order. Light
theme colours, taken from `packages/tokens`, because the marketing site
never renders dark.

### Stock photos, when nothing else fits

When no screenshot or diagram illustrates a passage and the post would
otherwise miss its image count, use a royalty-free stock photo:

- **License first.** Only images free for commercial use with no
  attribution required (CC0, or the Unsplash/Pexels/Pixabay licenses).
- **Download it into the repo**, `apps/web/public/photos/<file>.jpg`,
  referenced as `/photos/<file>`. Never hotlink: the remote file can
  change or die under a published page.
- **Record the source** (page URL and license) in a one-line HTML
  comment next to the image in the markdown, so a future licensing
  question has an answer.
- **Pick a photo about the reader's situation**, a front desk, a
  treatment room, paperwork, not a generic smiling patient or a
  close-up of teeth. Screenshots and diagrams still come first; the
  photo is the fallback, not the default.

### What does not go in a post

- **No AI-generated images.**
- **No screenshots of a competitor's product.** They are theirs, they
  go stale, and the comparison does not need them: quote the page and
  link it, which the sourcing rule already requires.

## SEO conventions

- **Quote the title and description in the frontmatter.** Comparison
  titles carry a colon almost every time, and an unquoted colon is a YAML
  parse error that fails the build.
- Slug in the language of the post: `dentalpin-vs-gesden` /
  `dentalpin-vs-gesden`, but `alternativa-open-source-a-X` /
  `open-source-alternative-to-X` / `alternative-open-source-a-X`.
  Translations do not share a filename on purpose — the directory carries
  the language and the filename is the URL, in that language.
- **Translated filenames must be linked with `translationKey`.** hreflang
  is built from it: every translation of a topic post declares
  `translationKey: <the Spanish filename>` in its frontmatter (the Spanish
  post itself needs nothing, its filename is the key). Posts that share a
  filename across directories are linked automatically. Without the key,
  each translation looks like an unrelated article and emits no hreflang,
  which is the state the whole blog was in until August 2026. A comparison
  written in one language only has no translations and needs no key.
- `description` reads as a search result, not a teaser. 140-160 chars.
- One `<h1>` (the title), `##` for the sections above.
- Link to the pricing page and the install post **in the post's own
  language** from every comparison: `/es/precios/`, `/en/pricing/`,
  `/fr/tarifs/`, `/pt/precos/`, `/de/preise/`, `/it/prezzi/`,
  `/pl/cennik/`, `/pt-br/precos/`, `/es-mx/precios/`. A
  Portuguese post linking to `/es/precios/`
  sends the reader to a page they cannot read, and the build's link check
  will not catch it because the page exists.
- Tag with `comparativa` / `comparison` / `comparatif` / `vergleich` /
  `confronto` / `porownanie` plus the vendor slug.

## Two queues, twenty-eight targets a week

The three morning runs (04:00, 08:00 and 11:00 UTC) are **comparisons**.
The afternoon run (16:00 UTC) is a **topic** post. The routine reads the
hour (`date -u +%H`) and picks the queue from it, so every day, weekends
included, produces three comparisons and one guide. The split follows the
work: a comparison is one file, a guide is seven.

The cadence doubled on 30 July 2026 and settled at four daily runs on
3 August 2026. Nine markets at five targets a week is
years of waiting for the newest ones, and the arithmetic below is what it fixed.
What grows is the number of runs, not the work inside a run: one target per
run, still derived from the filesystem, still one queue per run.

### The comparison queue rotates by language

**Do not work down Queue A in file order.** Each comparison run picks the
language with the **fewest published comparisons**, and only then takes
the next unwritten target inside that language's section. Ties break in
this order:

    PL → BR → MX → DE → IT → PT → FR → EN → ES

Skip a language whose section has no unwritten target left, and carry on
with the next.

This is still derivable from the filesystem, so the state rule holds:
count the comparison slugs already published per locale, take the
smallest. Nothing new to keep in sync.

The reason is arithmetic. Queue A holds 102 targets grouped by market:
22 Spanish (Spain, Latin America and Mexico), 20 English, 11 French,
16 Portuguese (Portugal and Brazil), 13 German, 12 Italian, 8 Polish
(counts as of the 17 September 2026 extension). Taken in
file order at twenty-one comparisons a week, Spanish fills the first week and
the locales that launched most recently, with the emptiest blogs, wait the
longest. Rotating gives every language a comparison
roughly once a month starting from week one, and Polish, German and
Italian lead the tie-break because they are the newest locales and their
blogs are still empty. Polish and Mexican vendors were verified
on 28 August 2026, so neither language is skipped any more. Brazil is not skipped: A5
already holds three verified Brazilian vendors, and their posts belong in
`blog/pt-br/`.

Count comparisons per directory, not per language. `blog/pt/` and
`blog/pt-br/` are two counters, which is what stops Brazil from looking
served because Portugal was.

The old ordering was written when only Spanish and English existed and a
post in the other markets could not rank. All those sections rank now, so
grouping by market is only a way to organise the catalogue, not an
instruction about what to write next.

When the comparison queue runs dry, the morning run takes a topic target too
and the site simply publishes ten topic targets a week. Do
**not** invent a competitor to keep the comparison cadence — a made-up
or misremembered vendor is the one mistake here that cannot be walked
back. Adding a new one means verifying it trades, this run, from its own
site.

Two entries in the first draft of this list were wrong, in the two ways
this fails:

- `charly.de` does not resolve. The German vendor trades as
  `solutio.de` — the name was right, the domain was invented.
- **Dentazon** was taken from `recepcionista.com`, an aggregator this
  same document bars as a source, and no such vendor could be found at
  any domain. The entry looked plausible and was sourced from exactly
  the place the rule forbids.

A vendor that cannot be reached at its own site does not go in this
queue.

Same state rule for both queues: what is published *is* the state. Look
at which slugs already exist and take the next unwritten target. For
Queue B that means the next row down; for Queue A it means the next row
in the language whose turn it is, per the rotation above.

A target counts as done when **every language its row lists** exists.
A comparison in A4 is done the moment the French post exists, because
French is the only language that row lists.

**A run writes all of a target's languages, in that run.** A topic target
produces seven files on the day it comes up, not one file on seven separate
days. Splitting it seven ways would drop each language to one guide every seven
runs.

Two cases produce a subset, and only two:

- **Catch-up.** A target published before a locale existed is still "next" until
  the missing language exists, and that run writes only what is missing.
- **A run that ran out of room.** Seven languages is a lot for one session. A
  run that cannot finish commits what is complete and names the missing
  directories in its summary; because a target is only done when every due
  directory has it, the next run picks the same target and fills the gaps. What
  is never acceptable is claiming a language that was not written, or
  machine-translating one to make the count.

**Polish went live in July 2026 with an empty blog, and German and Italian
started theirs the same month.** Every topic target already published is
therefore "next" until its Polish copy exists, and the first topic runs after
that date are catch-up runs writing one or two files instead of seven. Work down
Queue B from the top, exactly as if the targets were new.

## Topic posts are guides, not comparisons

A comparison post argues. A topic post **helps**, and earns the right to
mention the product at the end. They are not the same shape and writing
one like the other wastes the slot.

- **Answer the question in the first hundred words.** Someone arriving
  from a search has a question. Answer it immediately, then explain. A
  post that warms up for three paragraphs loses the reader and the
  featured snippet.
- **It has to be useful to someone who never installs Dentalpin.** That
  is the bar. If the guide only works as a pitch, it will not rank and
  it will not get linked.
- **Mention the product once, at the end, and only where it genuinely
  answers what the post is about.** A guide that pushes the product in
  every section reads as an advert and converts worse than one that does
  not.
- **No competitor claims.** The sourcing rule above exists for
  comparisons; topic posts should simply not need it. If one does, it is
  a comparison wearing a guide's title.
- **Regulation is a special case.** Verifactu, RGPD, LOPD: state what
  the rule requires, cite the official source (BOE, AEPD, AEAT), give
  the date consulted, and never give legal advice. "Esto no es
  asesoramiento legal" belongs in those posts.

The visual conventions above apply unchanged — opening paragraph,
callouts, numbered lists for anything sequential. Tables in a topic post
compare options or requirements, not vendors, so the chip markers stay
useful but the last column is not automatically ours.

## Queue A — comparisons (the 04:00, 08:00 and 11:00 UTC runs)

Every vendor here was verified to exist and to be trading when it was
added — name, site and market evidence. Research each again before
writing: this list is a queue, not a source of facts.

**These sections are a catalogue, not a running order.** What gets
written next is decided by the language rotation in *Two queues*, not by
reading this list top to bottom. Grouping is by market so the vendors sit
next to their competitors, which is what you want when researching one.

Two things the grouping still decides:

- **Order within a language.** Take the earlier section first, then work
  down its rows: Spanish means A1 before A2, and inside each section the
  biggest audience is already at the top.
- **What is out of play.** A vendor whose market speaks a language the
  site does not publish reaches nobody who is choosing between them and
  us. Those vendors sit in the blocked section at the bottom and are
  worth writing only once the site publishes in their language. That
  section is empty today: German and Italian went live in July 2026 and
  every row that was parked in it moved up into A6.

A5 covers Portugal and Brazil together because they share a language.
Spanish is split across A1 and A2 because Spain is the home market and
deserves to be drained first, not because the two need different posts.

Useful for any Spanish post: they publish their own comparison pages at `/es/comparativas/{gesden,mnprogram,nubimed}`, and **Dentalpin appears nowhere on the site**, so there is nothing to answer |

Mexico has its own directory since July 2026, and the hreflang for Argentina,
Chile, Colombia and Peru points at it. A post aimed at a Mexican reader goes
in `blog/es-mx/` with Mexican vocabulary (expediente clínico, consultorio,
celular, computadora, contador) and pesos. A vendor that sells across the
region without a Mexican pitch stays in `blog/es/`.

Two more things the row did not know: the legal entity is ZOLUPRO S.A.C. (Peru), and its own terms give a fifteen-day window around cancellation to request your data plus deletion within ninety days. Electronic invoicing is "Disponible para Ecuador y Perú" only. Its tariff and its funcionalidades page disagree on whether consentimientos informados are in the Básico plan or a paid add-on; the published post states both and cites each.** |

| Odontosys | `dentalpin-vs-odontosys` | `odontosys.com` (**the www subdomain does not resolve, always link the apex**), verified 28 August 2026. 25 years, claims 400+ users across 20+ Spanish-speaking countries, cloud on AWS. Sells region-wide, so it stays in `blog/es/`. **Checked 9 September 2026: the "Argentina-based" in this row was wrong. Every country marker on their own pages is Uruguayan: the privacy policy claims compliance with "la Ley N.º 18.331 de Protección de Datos Personales de Uruguay", the deletion policy cites "la República Oriental del Uruguay", the only phone is a +598 WhatsApp, the two testimonials that name a city name Montevideo, and the sibling brand is Clinicsys at `clinicsys.uy`. The 400 is **users, not clinics**, and their own testimonials describe Montevideo clinics of 10 and 12 users. Hosting is AWS with TLS 1.2+/AES-256 and two daily backups on servers outside AWS in other geographic zones, but **no region or country is named** for the primary data, and the ISO 27001 / SOC 2 / PCI DSS certificates they cite are AWS's, not theirs. **Verifactu appears on none of the pages consulted** although the FAQ claims España as a market, which is what makes the post for a Spanish reader. The DGIS NOM-024 register lists **ASTRAVERTEX S.A.S. — ASTRA DENTOS 1.1** as *programado para pre-verificación / primera verificación*, requested 25/09/2026: that is an application, not a certificate, and the post must say exactly that. Two things to handle carefully: the privacy page promises "Zero Knowledge — Nuestro personal tiene prohibido el acceso a datos clínicos" while the product sends dictation to an AI, so ask which processor does that before repeating it; and the home page threatens "Multas hasta $300,000 MXN según NOM-004", which is their marketing, not a sourced figure |

The feature table is **30 rows**: Starter is crossed on 15 of them (11 of which Professional has, 4 Enterprise-only) and Professional on 4. The "/mes / sucursal" label plus a branch cap is a real ambiguity worth a callout: 3 × $1,199 = $3,597, more than Enterprise at $3,299 for unlimited. No IVA statement anywhere. The Mensual/Anual switch showed no distinct annual figure this run, so assert nothing about it. **Install base: three different figures on one page, all theirs.** Hero "40 clínicas registradas"; a block headed "Cliniodent en números · Datos reales de la plataforma, en vivo" saying "6 Clínicas registradas", 1,062 pacientes, 147 citas, 19 módulos; and the English hero adding "+500 clinics already grow with us", a number the Spanish copy does not print. `/directorio` returns **6 resultados**, one named "Clínica Demo". The JSON-LD claims 4.9 from 87 reviews while all six directory entries read "Sin opiniones aún", and `foundingDate` is 2025. Quote them and send the reader to ask for paid-clinic numbers in writing; no adjective is needed and none was used. **Terms (1 January 2026)**: 30 days to export after cancellation, auto-renewal unless cancelled 3 days before the cycle, price changes on 30 days notice, liability capped at the last month paid, Mexican law and CDMX courts. **No export format or mechanism is published anywhere**, which is what makes the post: set the 30-day window against NOM-004's "periodo mínimo de 5 años, contados a partir de la fecha del último acto médico", an obligation that is the clinic's and not the vendor's. **Privacy**: subprocessors named are **FacturAPI** (CFDI timbrado) and **Meta** (WhatsApp Business API), plus unnamed cloud providers; "Algunos de nuestros proveedores de infraestructura se encuentran fuera de México" with no country or provider; AES-256-GCM at field level, TLS 1.3, 2FA available, no ISO 27001 or SOC 2 named; ARCO in 20 días hábiles. `/lfpdppp` gets the **Responsable/Encargado split right**, which is rare and was credited. **Reusable by every Mexican post: their `/lfpdppp` cites the abrogated "LFPDPPP, DOF 05/07/2010" and routes complaints to the INAI.** The acronym is still correct, the citation and the authority are not, and the official text settles it: `diputados.gob.mx/LeyesBiblio/pdf/LFPDPPP.pdf` (browser user agent, and **`pdftotext` does exist on this box at `/usr/bin/pdftotext`**, so the A5 note saying it does not is wrong) is the new law published DOF 20-03-2025, "TEXTO VIGENTE", última reforma DOF 14-11-2025, whose transitorio Primero puts it in force the next day and transitorio Segundo fracción I abroga the 2010 law expressly; it defines "Secretaría: Secretaría Anticorrupción y Buen Gobierno", and transitorio Cuarto says mentions of the INAI are understood as made to whoever took the functions. Also confirmed there: art. 3 counts "estado de salud presente o futuro" as sensitive, art. 8 requires "consentimiento expreso y por escrito" for sensitive data, art. 31 gives 20 días to answer ARCO. NOM-004 answers from `dof.gob.mx/nota_detalle.php?codigo=5272787&fecha=15/10/2012` as well as the SIDOF link the es-mx posts already use. **Absences, scoped to the pages consulted**: periodontograma, any public API or webhooks, an export format, ISO 27001 or SOC 2, any on-premise option, and any imaging or DICOM bridge. Rows they win and the post gave them: CFDI 4.0 timbrado (which we do not do in Mexico at all), the AI WhatsApp bot, nómina/laboratorio/gastos/caja, a free team migration "en menos de 24 horas", published phone and WhatsApp support Monday to Saturday (docs say 9am-8pm), and the compliance disclaimer that hands COFEPRIS, the responsable sanitario and RPBI back to the clinic. Partner programme pays 20% recurring, lifetime, across LATAM |

Checked 20 September 2026 and **not** added: **DentDoc / DentFlow**
(`dentdoc.com.mx`), which searches well for the Mexican head term and
fails the same check Dentra failed in A4. It publishes a price ("desde
$250 MXN/mes por consultorio"), a free trial and a NOM-004 claim, but its
footer is unedited Divi template placeholder, "1234 Divi St. #1000, San
Francisco, CA 94220" with a `dividentistoffice.com` contact address, and
no RFC or razón social appears anywhere on the page consulted. A working
site with a placeholder identity is not a verified vendor. Do not re-add
without a real fiscal identity published on their own domain. Noted the
same run: the `dentosapp.lovable.app` result that ranks for DentOS is on
a Lovable preview subdomain, not a vendor domain, and A2 already records
that Dentum was formerly DentOS.

Checked 24 September 2026 and **not** added: **Agenda Clínica**
(`agenda-clinica.com`). It verifies and it trades — "Software médico ·
México", "Expediente clínico conforme a la NOM-004", and a published
pay-per-use model ("Pagas por lo que usas": consultas bought in advance,
no monthly fee, credits that do not expire) with no figures on the page
("Escríbenos a hola@agenda-clinica.com y te pasamos los precios"). It is
out on scope, not on verification: **it is general medical and no
odontograma and no dental module appear on the pages consulted.** That is
the line between this and the Klinikare and Nubimed rows in A1, which are
multi-vertical but do ship a dental product. A comparison against a
system with no odontograma ranks for nothing a Mexican dentist types.
Move it up if they publish a dental page. The same run re-confirmed that
`medware.com.mx` is **not** a second target: Medware Digital Consulting
is the publisher of **OdontoWare**, already a row above, alongside
FarmaWare, FaunaWare and Medware Consulta. Search results for the Mexican
head term surface the publisher name rather than the product, so check
this section for the product before adding what looks like a new vendor.

Checked 1 October 2026 and **not** added: **Tu Asistente Dental**
(`tuasistentedental.com`). It fails the kiCLINIC test rather than the Dentra
one. The site is a single-page application: the apex, `/terminos` and
`/privacidad` all return the **same 8,5 kB shell** with no readable body text,
so no feature, no entity and no contract term on it can be cited. What it does
publish in fetchable HTML is its own JSON-LD, which is the vendor's own
assertion and worth recording for whoever adds it later: `areaServed` MX,
"Software dental en la nube para consultorios en México: expedientes clínicos,
agenda, facturación CFDI 4.0, WhatsApp con IA y reportes", and three offers,
**Gratis 0 MXN, Básico 299 MXN per MONTH and Completo 499 MXN per MONTH**. No
RFC and no razón social appear anywhere fetchable. **Do not add it from the
JSON-LD alone**: structured data is not a product page, it names no
odontograma, and the rule asks for pages they publish. It comes back under the
same condition as kiCLINIC, a run that can render JavaScript. The same run
**re-confirmed Agenda Clínica is still out**: `agenda-clinica.com` still
publishes no dental page and no odontograma, and still no RFC, so the
24 September scope finding stands on a second check.

**Reversed 8 October 2026: Tu Asistente Dental is now a row above.** Rendered
in a real browser, its pages show the prices, an odontograma and the operator,
which is the condition the paragraph above set. A run that cannot render
JavaScript cannot re-source that row, nor MR Dental Cloud and Cliniodent.

| tab32 | `dentalpin-vs-tab32` | `tab32.com` (**www redirects to the apex**), verified 28 August 2026. Cloud, claims 1.000+ practices in 40+ states. **Publishes prices (`/pricing/`)**. **Checked 9 September 2026: the site sits behind the same SiteGround proof-of-work captcha as UNO in A7, and here the cookie-jar retry does not clear it, because this session's egress rotates across an IP pool and the challenge is IP-bound. What works is solving it: the challenge page publishes `sgchallenge="<complexity>:..."`, and the answer is a nonce (1-4 bytes, big-endian, minimal length) appended to the challenge string such that the **first `complexity` bits of its SHA-1 are zero**, submitted to `sgsubmit_url` as `?sol=<base64 of challenge+nonce>&s=<ms>:<hashes>`. Base64, not hex: the bundle's encoder module is `enc.Base64`. Pin the whole flow to one pooled connection so the challenge and the submission share an egress IP. Headless Chromium is not an alternative: it cannot negotiate TLS through this session's relay for any host, so that fallback is dead across the board, not just here. Five things this row did not know. The price is a Year 1 introductory rate: Alpine Start-Up is "$125/mo Year 1, $225/mo Year 2+" for up to 3 providers, Alpine Established Practice $225 for up to 5, Summit "Custom Pricing", and a per-use layer on top at $0.20/claim, $0.50/claim attachments and $1.25/eligibility check, with AutoRemind free in Year 1 then $69/mo plus $0.08 per message over 500. Their own calculator on the same page returns $311/mo Year 1 and **$461/mo Year 2+** for a 15-appointment day, so the vendor publishes the number bigger than its headline: quote it, it is the strongest thing on their pricing page. What makes the post is where the openness sits: **Open API Access, Data Warehouse Access and an MCP Server are listed only under Summit** ("Available Options"), starred on `/summit/` as what distinguishes the tier, and named nowhere in either Alpine plan or on `/alpine/`, from the vendor that claims "the first open data warehouse in dentistry". Their own pages disagree twice and the post carries both sides: `/pricing/` labels Online Booking, ePrescribe, Patient Membership Plans and AI Imaging "Coming soon" while the home FAQ describes built-in online booking and the platform page publishes "PiAI detects conditions on dental X-rays" with an About milestone dating AI radiology to 2023. The customer contract is not published (Terms of Use 1.2: "you may be asked to agree to a user agreement"), so contract length, any SLA behind the published "99.95% uptime since 2017", migration pricing, what a sixth provider costs and any export on termination are all scoped to "the pages consulted"; what *is* published is a content licence in the Terms ("worldwide, royalty-free right to ... create derivative works of ... anonymized or de-identified versions"), narrowed only by that unpublished User Agreement, plus a five-year archive after deactivation and indefinite retention of de-identified health information. Rows they win outright: since 2012, SOC 2 and HIPAA, the published uptime figure, built-in imaging, US claims and eligibility, end-to-end orthodontics, a staffed migration from 20+ named systems, and 2-3 days to go live for a de novo practice. Their migration carries only "the last 2 years of appointments".** |

The cloud page's "ISO 9001 and SSAE 18 certifications" describe the **AWS infrastructure**, not Archy, and `trust.archy.com` returns an empty SPA shell, so no certification of their own could be sourced; the AWS region is never named. No uptime figure, no SLA, no API or developer documentation, no suite prices and no conversion fee or timeline appear on any page consulted, and the post scopes each of those to "the pages consulted". Rows they win outright: the published flat price, unlimited providers, integrated imaging and sensor support, e-prescribing, AI radiology, a native mobile app, the published BAA, the published contract and the free exit export.** |

The visible copy publishes no price anywhere, and the boutons "Essayer gratuitement" and "Démarrer gratuitement" all route to a fifteen-minute Calendly demo rather than a trial, but the home page's schema.org JSON-LD declares `"price": "75.00"`, `"priceCurrency": "EUR"` with no period, no unit and no scope: cite it as a published figure that computes nothing, never as a monthly rate. No install-base figure appears on any page consulted (the home page's "des cabinets satisfaits" stat renders with no number); named client practices with dates are all they publish. The +20 % devis, −33 % no-shows and +3h/week figures are theirs and are scoped on their own site to "constaté en moyenne chez les cabinets équipés Matisse", with no method published.** |

Third, **the CNDA registry settles the dental question and it goes their way**: editor CALIMAPS carries **57 inscriptions, WINDOWS on all 57**, across two product names (DR SANTE and VITACLIC), and the latest agrément (DR SANTE 23.5.0, 18 August 2025, CDC 1.40.14.13) lists **"CCAM Dentaire"**, **"EV124 - Actes Complexes pour Chirurgiens-Dentistes (Avenant 28)"** and **"EV105 - Réforme 100% Santé (Avenant 19)"** among its included evolutions, plus a dozen dental regulatory sheets; the newest inscription (DR SANTE 26, téléservice INSi, conforme 02-03-2026) names **"Chirurgien dentiste"** in its PS categories. Never write that this product cannot bill dental acts. The product is a multi-vertical medical PMS with one landing page among sixty specialities. Fifth, **the tariff has a contract shape the row did not carry**: their CGV fix "une période ferme de douze (12) mois, payable en une annuité, avec tacite reconduction", early termination still owes the running period, rights are granted "pour un nombre d'Utilisateurs nommés" and the editor reserves a lock plus usage inspection "à tout moment et avec ou sans notification". Their RGPD annex is the counterweight and must be conceded: no transfer outside the EU, sub-processors all in France, HDS host named (ITS INTEGRA), and an end-of-contract commitment to return all data "dans un format standard" or destroy it and certify the destruction. There are also three licences, not one (58 € médecins, 32 € paramédicaux, 18 € coordination), Vidal Expert is 32 € TTC/mois on top, and the mobile app alone is 72 €/an per user |

The French e-invoicing reform is the local counterpart to Verifactu and
belongs in Queue B as its own French post, not as a translation of the
Verifactu one.

### A5 · Portuguese — Portugal in `blog/pt/`, Brazil in `blog/pt-br/`

Unblocked: the site publishes in Portuguese since July 2026. **These are
written in Portuguese only.**

**`/pt/` keeps the neutral register; `/pt-br/` does not.** The shared
Portuguese copy still avoids the words that split the two (ecrã/tela,
telemóvel/celular, ficheiro/arquivo, utilizador/usuário, equipa/equipe,
faturação/faturamento) and uses the European spelling where no neutral form
exists (`contacto`). That rule applies to anything written in `blog/pt/`,
because it is read in both countries.

A post written for Brazil goes in `blog/pt-br/` and is written in Brazilian
Portuguese without hedging: celular, tela, arquivo, usuário, faturamento,
prontuário, `contato`, prices in reais, no enclitic pronouns. Same filename
as the parent post means it replaces that one post under `/pt-br/` and leaves
the rest inherited, which is how a Brazilian rewrite of a guide ships without
touching Portugal.

**One queue, ordered by opportunity, and that puts Brazil first.** There
is no separate Portuguese and Brazilian section, the same way A2 does not
split Spain from Latin America. Brazil is the larger dental market by a
wide margin and the vendors' own numbers say so: Clinicorp claims
100.000+ active users where NewSoft claims 2.000 clinics. Working down
this list in order means the biggest audience gets served first, which
was not true while Portugal sat at the top for no reason other than
having been added second.

What the country still decides is one line per post: the **search
vocabulary in the title and description**. "Software de gestão para
clínicas dentárias" reads Portuguese, "software odontológico" reads
Brazilian, and each post should use whichever its vendor's customers
type. That is a per-post choice, not a reason to split the queue.

| Target | Why |
|---|---|

| Clinicorp | Brazil, so `blog/pt-br/`. Claims 100.000+ active users |
| Dental Office | Brazil, so `blog/pt-br/`. 25 years, ~40.000 dentists |

And it is one of the very few vendors in any market that publishes a full tariff (35 €/mês with 3 users, +8 €/mês each additional), so the usual "não publica preços" line is wrong here. The published post carries both.** |
