Brak vendor lock-in" and the home FAQ specifies a ZIP of JSON files plus attachments. Our remaining wins are price, open source, direct PostgreSQL access and a documented REST API with generated OpenAPI (theirs is "Webhook & REST API" from plan Pro with no public documentation on any page consulted). **Three things the vendor publishes two ways**, all carried in the post and sourced separately: hosting location three different ways ("Polski hosting (UE)" and "Serwery w polskim data center" on every page, "UE (Niemcy/Polska, zależnie od regionu)" in the home FAQ, "UE (OVHcloud, Polska i Francja)" on `/bezpieczenstwo` and the subprocessor list); **KSeF is not shipped** ("BDO i KSeF gotowe" and "e-faktury wbudowane" on the home page against "integrację KSeF prowadzimy jako moduł... pokażemy status" in the KSeF FAQ, which also puts *sales* invoicing at "na życzenie, w cenie planu Pro/Klinika"); and ZnanyLekarz is advertised as an "Integracja" on the Pro card but is "import wizyt z plików CSV" in the FAQ. One further contractual rozjazd: the post-termination download window is **15 days** in DPA §8 against **30 days** in EULA §6. **The strongest things they publish**, and the post concedes them: BDO with real API integration (KEO created automatically in the government system on each pickup, KPO from one screen, recipient looked up by NIP), sterilisation cycle records tied to the equipment passport, equipment passports with QR and deadline alerts, kadry with ZUS payroll, an append-only audit log enforced at database level that logs reads too, approved clinical entries sealed with a SHA-256 checksum, AES-256 at rest with a per-clinic rotating key, RCPD and DPA generated from the app, 20-year retention per art. 29, free migration done by the vendor, no setup fee, price grandfathering ("Konta założone przed zmianami cennika zachowują swoje dotychczasowe warunki bezterminowo"), an indefinite-term contract cancellable any time with no commitment period, and a **contractual** 14-day no-reason refund for every customer on top of the statutory right. **Unusually candid in two places worth crediting**: `/zaufanie` states outright that they work to the ISO/IEC 27001:2022 framework but hold no accredited certificate, publishes a self-assessment with three "częściowe" and two "planowane" statuses (external pentests among the planned), and `/funkcje/bdo` has an "Uczciwie: co robisz Ty, a co druga strona" section. **What is absent across all 19 pages**: any install-base figure, founding year or other measure of scale (there are no JS counters either, no data-target attributes, so nothing was missed the way the Medyc row warns), any uptime figure or SLA (the word SLA appears exactly once, on the Klinika plan card, while regulamin §11 is best-efforts with liability capped at one month's fees), public API documentation, and any Android or iOS app (the EULA §2 does mention "aplikacji mobilnej" and the home FAQ calls the product a PWA with no full offline support). The entity in this row is confirmed from the footer of every page. **They also publish their own comparison pages** (`/alternatywa`) against Estomed, Proassist, Prodentis, Mediporta, Dr100, Medfile, KS-SOMED and MEDchart, and **Dentalpin is named on none of the 19 pages consulted**. They state repeatedly and by design that they do not do e-recepta, e-skierowanie, P1 zdarzenia medyczne or NFZ, so that table row is red on both sides. **The honest verdict is that for a private Polish dental practice without an NFZ contract they fit better today, and the published post says so.** |
| mMedica (Asseco Poland) | `dentalpin-vs-mmedica` | `mmedica.asseco.pl/moduly-dodatkowe/stomatologia/`, verified 8 October 2026. Desktop/client-server on Windows (`/wymagania-sprzetowe/`: "System operacyjny Windows z aktywnym wsparciem", a workstation can act as the server "Przy instalacjach do 15 stanowisk"). Multi-vertical clinic system (POZ, AOS, nursing, occupational medicine) sold with a **paid dental module**, so state it plainly: dentistry is an add-on, not the product. The module page describes a tooth diagram where marking a procedure fills in the billing data automatically, with NFZ guaranteed-benefit procedures pre-mapped "zgodnie z załącznikiem do Rozporządzenia Ministra Zdrowia", which makes it the default for NFZ-contracted practices. **Publishes a full licence price list in PLN net (`/cennik-mmedica/`, "Ceny obowiązują do 31.12.2026 r.")**: base editions per first / each further workstation, mMedica PS 375,00 / 242,00 zł, Standard 558,00 / 368,00 zł, Komercja 461,00 / 298,00 zł ("nie obsługuje rozliczeń z NFZ"), Komercja+ 698,00 / 460,00 zł; **Stomatologia module 394,00 / 240,00 zł**, available for PS+, Standard(+) and Komercja(+). The post is per-workstation licence + per-module pricing + NFZ settlement against free self-hosted with no NFZ integration: concede the NFZ row outright |
| KS-SOMED (KAMSOFT S.A.) | `dentalpin-vs-ks-somed` | `kamsoft.pl/ks-somed/`, verified 8 October 2026. KAMSOFT S.A., "Od ponad 40 lat tworzymy systemy IT" for clinics, hospitals, pharmacies and wholesalers. Multi-vertical clinic system "przeznaczony przede wszystkim dla średnich i dużych placówek" with a **Stomatologia module** ("Interaktywny diagram uzębienia", "Automatyczna aktualizacja diagramu po zabiegach", integration with external equipment) next to Okulistyka, RIS, Medycyna Pracy. Client-server with "kopie w chmurze" rather than a SaaS. **Links a price list per product at `cennik.kamsoft.pl/cennik.html?produkt=KS-SOMED` ("Wszystkie ceny podane są w PLN"), but the page renders the figures with JavaScript and a plain fetch returned none, so the run must open it in a browser before quoting any number.** Already named by MedEQ's own `/alternatywa/ks-somed` page, so Polish buyers do compare against it. The interesting angle: the biggest Polish healthcare-IT house with a dental module bolted onto a clinic system, against a dental-first open codebase |
| MyDr EDM (MyDr Sp. z o.o.) | `dentalpin-vs-mydr` | `pro.mydr.pl/rozwiazania/stomatologia`, verified 8 October 2026. Cloud, multi-vertical medical EDM with a **dedicated dental landing**: "Interaktywny diagram zębowy" with automatic suggestions, staff schedule, patient account with online booking, an NFZ settlement module. **Publishes prices in PLN gross per 30 days (`pro.mydr.pl/cennik`)**: Start free ("10 wizyt w miesiącu", after which no further visits can be added), Optymalny 129 zł brutto / 30 dni, Pro 339 zł brutto / 30 dni (adds AI tools and the NFZ module), 7-day trial. Entity from the footer: **MyDr Sp. z o.o., ul. Puławska 465, 02-844 Warszawa, NIP 5252730290, KRS 0000704950**. The site links a ZnanyLekarz page (`pro.znanylekarz.pl/produkty/polacz-systemy-z-mydr-edm`) for connecting systems to MyDr EDM; the run should establish the exact relationship to ZnanyLekarz/Docplanner from their own pages before stating it, not assume ownership. Comparison angle: a freemium capped by visit count against no cap at all |
| PL-Med (PL-Med Software) | `dentalpin-vs-pl-med` | `pl-med.pl`, verified 8 October 2026. Desktop (demo is a download link sent by e-mail; remote support via `download.pl-dent.pl/iConnect.exe`), multi-specialty with **Stomatologia listed first** among "Specjalizacje programu PL-Med: Stomatologia, Medycyna Estetyczna, Dermatologia, Kosmetologia, Fizjoterapia…". **Claims on its home page: "11100 aktualnie działających licencji w całej Polsce", "150000 kart pacjentów", "23 lat obecności na rynku"**, editions PL-MED FREE, LIGHT/TO-GO and PRO. Address: ul. Marcina Kasprzaka 31 lok. 126, 01-234 Warszawa; no company ID on the pages fetched. **No published prices.** Two cautions for the run: the site reads dated (it still mentions Windows Phone, and two menu links go to `/stona-w-przebudowie/`), though the header says "Oprogramowanie PL-Med 2026 nowość"; and no page fetched mentions e-Recepta, P1 or EDM, so do not credit or deny those without finding it on their site. The interesting part is a free tier from a desktop vendor. **Written 13 October 2026, and the row needed three corrections.** **First, the site is bigger than its menu**: `wp-sitemap.xml` lists fifteen pages and several of the useful ones are not in the navigation. `/program/komfort-i-efektywnosc-pracy/` is the real versions page and the most useful page on the site. **Second, "editions FREE, LIGHT/TO-GO and PRO" is short by half: there are six**, FREE, Light, TO-GO, PRO, **DB-Cloud** and **TO-GO DB-Cloud**, so "Desktop" needs qualifying. PRO is "oparte na systemie bazy danych MS-SQL Server 2008"; DB-Cloud is the same four modules (Recepcja, Gabinet, Administracja, Sprzedaż/Magazyn) "z danymi w chmurze, umożliwiające pracę rozproszoną / zdalną" on MySQL, and TO-GO DB-Cloud is a pendrive against that shared database "przez użytkowników znajdujących się w różnych lokalizacjach". It is a Windows-installed program with an optional cloud database, neither pure desktop nor a cloud PMS: do not write it as either. **Third, "No published prices" is wrong in the most useful way available.** `/program/pliki-do-pobrania/` publishes five downloads including `Cennik PL-Med Software 2017.pdf` and `wymagania sprzętowe.pdf`, so a price list and a system requirements document both exist. All of them sit on `download.pl-dent.pl`, and **the domain `pl-dent.pl` returns NXDOMAIN**, checked 13 October 2026 in two independent public resolvers (dns.google and cloudflare-dns.com) while `pl-med.pl` resolved normally from the same place. So the installer, the remote support tool, the iConnect link on the home page, the price list and the requirements are all unreachable, and **no figure is obtainable from the vendor at all**: the published post states that and quotes no price, which is stronger than the standard sentence. The same dead domain is in their contact links: the page displays `dorota.panczak@pl-med.pl` and `support@pl-med.pl` while the mailto hrefs go to `pldent.pl`, so only `info@pl-med.pl` is live and the four phone numbers are the working route. **The row’s caution about e-Recepta, P1 and EDM is settled and the answer is scope, not deny**: across all fifteen pages the words e-recepta, EDM, P1, NFZ, KSeF, eksport and kopia zapasowa appear nowhere, and neither do **diagram zębowy, periodontogram, RTG, kosztorys or plan leczenia**, although Stomatologia is first on both specialisation lists (seven on the home page, eleven on the versions page). The "Gabinet" module is named and never described, because every "Czytaj więcej" and the Galeria menu item land on `/stona-w-przebudowie/` ("Zapraszamy wkrótce..."). Light does publish "prowadzenia elektronicznej dokumentacji medycznej", so records-keeping is claimed even though the regulatory EDM layer is not: keep those two apart. **The FREE tier is not a separate product**, it is a "bezpłatny pakiet opcji dostępny bez rejestracji w wersjach Light, TO-GO oraz PRO", granted "bezterminowo, na zasadach FREEWARE SOFTWARE, bez możliwości modyfikacji, kopiowania oraz udostępniania programu osobom trzecim", which is the cleanest freeware-is-not-open-source contrast in any market queue. What FREE excludes is published nowhere; what Light excludes is, and it decides the post: "wersja jednostanowiskowa z możliwością wykonywania naraz jednej operacji np. otworzenie w tym samym czasie tylko jednej karty pacjenta", sold "w bardzo przystępnej cenie" with no figure attached. **The licence is bound to a named computer** and that is in the privacy policy, not the product pages: "gdy zweryfikujemy twoją licencję na zarejestrowanym komputerze" plus collection of "dane identyfikacyjne z urządzenia z systemem Windows, takie jak numery seryjne". Its RODO section covers people who order and register licences, not powierzenie przetwarzania of patient data, so scope it the way the ZaWin row scopes its Datenschutzerklärung. Four more things the row did not know. The two tenure claims on the home page do not reconcile, "23 lat obecności na rynku" in the counters against "Przez kilkanaście lat obecności na rynku" in the prose above them, and the product year does not either: the header says "Oprogramowanie PL-Med 2026 nowość" while the versions page says "PL-Med Software 2012". The 11 100 is "aktualnie działających licencji", licences and not practices, as with teemer and ZaWin, and the 150 000 is "kart pacjentów zgromadzonych w jednej bazie". The newest dated content on the site is an iCloud notice of 15.06.2017. And one row they win outright that nothing else in A8 offers: **SMS go out from the practice’s own number** "w ramach abonamentu u swojego operatora", through an Android handset running SMS Gateway Ultimate or the `serwersms.pl` gateway, so there is no per-message vendor margin. TO-GO on a pendrive is the second. Only three testimonials are published, two of them naming a clinic (Impladent, Dental House), and there is **no NIP, KRS or REGON on any page**, only the Kasprzaka address and the phones; a CEDE 2015 exhibitor catalogue lists PL-MED SOFTWARE at an older Warsaw address but it is a third party and was not used in the post |

newly urgent: the vendors above advertise e-Recepta and EDM integration,
and the comparison pages will want to link it.

### A9 · Blocked on language — do not write these yet

Real vendors, real markets, and a post in a language we publish reaches
almost none of the people choosing between them and us. Move a row up
into its own section when the site publishes in that language.

**This section is empty.** The three German rows that lived here moved
into A6 when the site started publishing in German. The rule stays: a
vendor whose buyers search in a language we do not publish goes here, not
into a section it cannot rank in.

## Queue B — topics (the 16:00 UTC run)

Ordered by intent. Work down it; the top of this list is where a clinic
is closest to needing what we sell.

**These go out in every language the site publishes**, except where the
row says otherwise. The two regional variants are the exception that is not
worth a column: they inherit the parent's post, and a variant only gets its
own file when the market changes the answer (currency, tax, regulator). The problems are the same in Madrid, Lyon, Munich,
Milan and Manchester, and there is no competitor claim to re-verify per
copy.

The exception is the rows marked **country-locked**. Compliance is
national: the Spanish, French, German and Italian rows below are
different posts with different law, different deadlines and different
official sources, not translations of each other.

**The slug column gained a seventh position, `pl`, on 26 October 2026**, and
reading an older row needs one rule because of it. Polish went live in July
2026 and the column was never widened, so every row added before that date
carries six positions and Polish was left to be derived. The two shapes read
differently and both are still correct:

- **Six positions, all of them slugs** means the row is a full-breadth topic
  and is due in `pl` as well, under a natively chosen Polish slug that is not
  written in the table. Check that one with `translationKey`, not by guessing
  the filename.
- **Six positions with any `—` in them** means the row is limited to the
  locales it names, and `pl` is not due.
- **Seven positions** is a row added from 26 October 2026 on. It names every
  locale explicitly, `pl` included, and nothing about it has to be derived.

All 128 full-breadth rows were published in `pl` before the column existed, so
widening it changes no coverage; it only removes the one case the six-position
shape could not express, a row due in Polish and in some but not all of the
rest.

| Liquidar a los doctores colaboradores: lo que el software tiene que calcular | `liquidacion-doctores-colaboradores` / `associate-dentist-pay-calculation` / `retrocession-honoraires-collaborateur-dentaire` / `honorarios-dentistas-colaboradores` / `umsatzbeteiligung-angestellte-zahnaerzte` / `compenso-collaboratori-studio-odontoiatrico` | liquidación colaboradores clínica dental · Added 15 September 2026, intent verified that run in five markets, and the head term is different in every one: `rétrocession d'honoraires` is the contractual word in France (and the Ordre National des Chirurgiens-Dentistes publishes on its VAT thresholds), `compenso ai collaboratori` in Italy, `Umsatzbeteiligung` for an angestellter Zahnarzt in Germany, `rozliczanie lekarzy współpracujących` in Poland (Polish slug `rozliczanie-lekarzy-wspolpracujacych`). **Translating this row is the way to waste it.** The post is the arithmetic the software has to hold, not the percentage: which treatments are attributed to which practitioner, what is deducted before the percentage applies (laboratory and materials, and whether the deduction is gross or net), what happens to a treatment two practitioners touched, and what the monthly statement has to show so the practitioner can check it. **Publish no recommended percentage**: the figures circulating in each market come from consultants and trade press, not from an official source, so cite them as what a named source says, with its date, or leave them out. The tax and employment status of the collaborator is national, so name the national source or say plainly that the post does not cover it, and it is not legal or tax advice |
| Un paciente pide su historia clínica: qué entregar, en qué plazo y en qué formato | `paciente-pide-su-historia-clinica` / `patient-requests-their-dental-records` / `patient-demande-son-dossier-dentaire` / `paciente-pede-o-seu-processo-clinico` / `patient-fordert-patientenakte-an` / `paziente-chiede-la-cartella-clinica` | paciente pide historia clínica · Added 15 September 2026, intent verified that run in Spanish, German and Polish. RGPD art. 15 is the floor everywhere the site publishes, which is why this is not country-locked, but **the deadline, the fee and the medical-record layer on top are national and each post cites its own regulator**: AEPD and Ley 41/2002 in Spain, § 630g BGB and the Court of Justice ruling on the first copy being free in Germany, the ustawa o prawach pacjenta in Poland (14 days, first copy free since 4 May 2019, Polish slug `udostepnianie-dokumentacji-medycznej-pacjentowi`), CNIL and the ONCD in France. Re-source every deadline and fee the run it is written, never from another language's copy, and say plainly it is not legal advice. The software half is the useful half: what a complete export contains (notes, odontogram, images, quotes, consents), what format it comes out in, and how the request is logged |
| Traspaso de la clínica: qué pasa con las historias clínicas | `traspaso-clinica-dental-historias` / `dental-practice-sale-patient-records` / `cession-cabinet-dentaire-dossiers-patients` / `trespasse-clinica-dentaria-processos` / `praxisabgabe-patientenakten` / `cessione-studio-odontoiatrico-cartelle` | traspaso clínica dental pacientes · Added 15 September 2026, intent verified that run in Spanish, German and French. The clinic-side mirror of the `proveedor-software-cierra` row: there the vendor goes, here the practice changes hands and the records cannot simply move with the furniture. Germany publishes the most concrete model and it is worth describing rather than inventing one, the Zwei-Schrank-Modell that the data protection authorities set out for a Praxisübergabe, with the digital version being a locked predecessor archive the buyer opens per patient on consent; France frames it as cession de patientèle with CNIL rules on how the files travel; Spain as a change of controller under the RGPD. Source each from its own regulator or professional body the run it is written, and keep the post on what the buyer and seller have to be able to do in the software: separate the two record sets, keep the audit trail, export completely. Not legal advice |
| Cuánto hay que guardar una historia clínica dental y cómo destruirla después | `conservar-historia-clinica-dental-plazos` / `how-long-to-keep-dental-records` / `duree-conservation-dossier-dentaire` / `prazo-conservacao-processo-clinico-dentario` / `aufbewahrungsfristen-patientenakte-zahnarztpraxis` / `conservazione-cartelle-cliniche-odontoiatriche` | cuánto tiempo conservar historia clínica · Added 16 September 2026, intent verified that run in Spanish, German and Polish, and it is a different question in each: five years from the last asistencia in Spain, ten in Germany, twenty in Poland. The mirror of the `paciente-pide-su-historia-clinica` row and it must not repeat it: that one is about handing a copy out, this one is about how long the practice is obliged to hold the record and what it does when the clock runs out. **Each post cites its own primary text, never another language's copy and never the trade blogs that carry these figures**: Ley 41/2002 art. 17 plus the autonomous community's own rule in Spain (the plazos differ by comunidad and the post has to say so rather than publishing one number for the country), § 630f Abs. 3 BGB in Germany with the separate limitation periods of §§ 195-199 BGB as the reason practices keep longer, the ustawa o prawach pacjenta i Rzeczniku Praw Pacjenta art. 29 in Poland (Polish slug `okres-przechowywania-dokumentacji-medycznej-w-gabinecie-stomatologicznym`, and the exceptions are the interesting part: records of a child treated before its second birthday, analogue radiographs held outside the record, skierowania), the Code de la santé publique in France, and the national text for Portugal and Italy rather than the Spanish one. Radiographs, consents and casts can each carry their own clock, so state which. The software half is what the post is for: a retention period is unenforceable if nothing tells the practice which records have aged out, so cover what has to be searchable (date of the last entry, not the date the patient was created), what "destruir" means for a database and a backup that still holds the row, and what the practice keeps as evidence that it destroyed anything. Not legal advice |
| Periodontograma digital: qué se registra en cada sondaje y cómo se lee la evolución | `periodontograma-digital` / `periodontal-charting-software` / `parodontogramme-numerique` / `periodontograma-digital` / `parodontalstatus-digital-dokumentieren` / `parodontogramma-digitale` | periodontograma digital · Added 16 September 2026, intent verified that run in Spanish and German. The `odontograma-digital` row is the tooth-by-tooth chart; this is the periodontal one, six sites per tooth, and the two posts must not overlap: link them instead. Polish slug `cyfrowy-periodontogram` (the row named `periodontogram-cyfrowy` when it was added; the published file is `cyfrowy-periodontogram.md` and the row now matches it, so no run writes a second copy), and the vocabulary table in *The words the profession actually uses* has the term for every language, `Parodontalstatus` in German being the one a translator gets wrong. The post is what the chart has to hold and why: profundidad de sondaje and margen gingival as separate measurements because the attachment level is derived from both, sangrado and supuración per site, movilidad, furcas, placa, and the reason a practice records all six sites rather than the worst one. Then the half no vendor page covers, reading two charts months apart: what a millimetre of change means, why the same examiner and the same probe matter, and what the reevaluation after periodontal treatment is comparing. Clinical claims are sourced like regulatory ones, from a professional body or a peer-reviewed source with the date consulted, never from a competitor's blog; the EFP and the 2018 classification are the obvious place to start, and the post states plainly that it is not a clinical protocol. `periodontogram.png` is in the screenshots directory and is the natural first image |
| Receta electrónica privada en la clínica dental | `receta-electronica-privada-clinica-dental` in `blog/es/` | receta electrónica privada dentista · **country-locked: ES**. Added 16 September 2026, intent verified that run. Source from the Consejo General de Dentistas and the official Sistema de Receta Electrónica Privada pages, never from the prescribing platforms' own marketing, and re-source the coverage and participation figures the run it is written rather than reusing the ones circulating in the trade press. What the post has to establish first, and this is the part a run must not assume: whether an electronic private prescription is an obligation or an option for a private dental practice today, and what the paper receta médica privada still requires beside it, both sourced from the Consejo. Then the practical half: what a dentist has to hold (colegiación, certificate, an accredited platform) and where that sits relative to the practice software. **Say plainly that Dentalpin does not issue REMPe prescriptions**, because two Queue A rows already concede receta electrónica to competitors and a guide that implies otherwise contradicts them. Not legal advice |

Link all three. This post is the questionnaire itself: what it has to ask and why each question is there, and the half that decides it, that a health questionnaire is a living document rather than a form filled once at the first visit. The software questions are the useful ones: who updates it and when, how a change is recorded so the previous answers are not silently overwritten, how an answer becomes a visible alert rather than a line nobody reads again, and what a patient filling it in at home before the appointment changes about the record. Clinical content is sourced like a regulatory claim, from a professional body or a peer-reviewed source with the date consulted, never from a vendor's blog. The questionnaire is part of the clinical record, so retention and rectification follow each country's own text, cited per country and never from another language's copy, and the post says plainly it is neither legal nor clinical advice. The German copy has one extra thing to state that the others do not: what the practice must still offer a patient who declines to answer digitally |

The method is the part the sources broadly agree on (the cost of an hour of chair time, the variable cost of the treatment, the laboratory cost and the specialist's share) and each component cited to whoever is being followed. **The national layer differs and is sourced per country the run it is written, never translated**: whether minimum tariffs bind at all, what a public or conventioned schedule fixes beside private pricing, and what each country's consumer law requires a practice to display. Establish it from the national text or the professional body, not from another language's copy of this post. Then the half no consultancy article covers, which is what makes it a guide: how a price list is versioned, what happens to a quote already issued and to one already accepted when prices go up, how a treatment plan running across months is priced, and whether a practice can hold a separate schedule per insurer. | Protección radiológica en la clínica dental: qué registros hay que tener al día | `proteccion-radiologica-clinica-dental` / `dental-x-ray-radiation-protection-records` / `radioprotection-cabinet-dentaire` / `protecao-radiologica-clinica-dentaria` / `strahlenschutz-zahnarztpraxis` / `radioprotezione-studio-odontoiatrico` | protección radiológica clínica dental · Added 20 September 2026, intent verified that run in Spanish and German. Polish slug `ochrona-radiologiczna-w-gabinecie-stomatologicznym`. **Two published rows are adjacent and this one must not repeat either**: `almacenar-radiografias-digitales` is where the images live and `integrar-radiologia-software-dental` is the bridge to the imaging software. This row is the legal and record-keeping layer around the machine: the declaration or licence of the installation, the shielding report, the acceptance and constancy tests, the staff dosimetry and training record, the radiation-protection programme, and the justification of each radiograph inside the clinical record. **The authority and the text are national and each post cites its own**, never another language's copy. Two were verified this run and are safe to name: Spain is the CSN plus **Real Decreto 1085/2009, de 3 de julio, por el que se aprueba el Reglamento sobre instalación y utilización de aparatos de rayos X con fines de diagnóstico médico**, in force with its last update published 21 December 2022 per the BOE consolidated text, and the CSN publishes its own inspection actas for dental clinics; Germany is StrlSchG and StrlSchV (the every-one-to-three-years quality-assurance duty is anchored in § 86 Abs. 2 Nr. 9 StrlSchG with § 130 StrlSchV per the Landeszahnärztekammern) plus the **Zahnärztliche Stelle Röntgen** at the state chamber, which is the body a German practice actually deals with and which no other market has. France, Italy, Portugal and Poland were **not** verified this run: establish the authority and the text in force from the national regulator before writing, and if a run cannot, write the languages it can and leave the rest to the next one rather than guessing a decree number. The software half is the half that is ours to be useful about: a radiograph is only defensible if the record says who justified it, on what indication and with what exposure, so cover what has to be stored next to the image, what the retention clock is (it can differ from the record's own, see `conservar-historia-clinica-dental-plazos`) and what a practice can actually produce when an inspector asks for the last year of exposures. Not legal advice, and not a substitute for the UTPR, SPR or Zahnärztliche Stelle. **Written 21 September 2026 in all seven locales, and the four unverified authorities resolved.** Spain: RD 1085/2009 art. 17 classifies **dental intraoral as tipo 3 but dental NON-intraoral (panorámico, CBCT) as tipo 2**, and the type sets the calendar, so the certificado de conformidad by a UTPR/SPR is anual/bienal/quinquenal for types 1/2/3 (art. 18.e-f), types 1 and 2 send an annual or biennial informe to the CSN (art. 18.g) and type 3 instead keeps the same information as registros for at least ten years (art. 18.h); vigilancia de niveles de radiación is anual for everyone (art. 18.d), dosimetry is determined monthly (art. 19.3.b), and art. 19.4 requires three archives, dosimetry, radiological surveillance and staff training. The declaración goes to the comunidad autónoma before puesta en funcionamiento with three anexos, and failing to subsanar in 15 days obliges the titular to return the equipment to the supplier (art. 12). CSN acreditación has no validity period in the RD itself, so the post does not claim one. **France: régime de déclaration**, décision ASN 2018-DC-0649 under R.1333-109 2° and R.1333-110 CSP, récépissé with a CODEP and a SIGIS number is the act permitting the activity; formation à la radioprotection des patients is valid **ten years** for chirurgiens-dentistes; the maintenance and quality-control register of art. R.5212-28 CSP is kept **five years after the device leaves service**, not after the operation; vérification initiale by an accredited body at commissioning and important modification, renewed every three years only for interventional equipment; the compte rendu content is the arrêté du 22 septembre 2006 under R.1333-66 CSP. Source: the ASN guide *Principales dispositions réglementaires... en radiologie médicale et dentaire*, édition octobre 2024, served from the ASNR site. **Italy: notifica preventiva di pratica at least ten days before detention** (art. 46 c. 2 D.Lgs. 101/2020) to ASL, ITL, vigili del fuoco and ARPA, preceded by the nomina dell'ERP (art. 128) and accompanied by the relazione ex art. 109 c. 2. A dentist can be RIR only as esercente, or, if not esercente, only for endorali up to 70 kV. The centrepiece for us is **Allegato XXIX**: a registro informatico of every exam recording sesso, età and the tempo di scatto in seconds for endorali or the prodotto dose per area in mGy/cm² for OPT and cone beam. Sorveglianza fisica and controllo di qualità are at least annual; cessazione is notified 30 days ahead. Source: the INAIL/ASL Roma 6 guide *La pratica radiologica nel settore odontoiatrico*, July 2021. **Portugal: intraoral is registo, everything else is licença.** APA's own presentation lists 'Operação de equipamentos de medicina dentária intraoral' under registo (art. 4, art. 22), so an OPG or CBCT moves the clinic to licenciamento; art. 32 lists the six registo elements and art. 33 the licença additions; the RPR (art. 159) needs nível 1 or 2 under DL 227/2008, with the art. 207 transition closed in 2022; acceptance tests before first patient use per art. 104(3) against EC Radiation Protection 162; **portable intraoral units are explicitly desencorajados**; inspection is IGAMAOT on the mainland, DROTA in Madeira and IRA in the Açores, with regulation by APA except the Açores (DRA). **Poland: the permit comes from the państwowy wojewódzki inspektor sanitarny**, not the PAA (art. 4 ust. 1 pkt 10-11 and art. 5 ust. 4 Prawa atomowego), and intraoral units are exempt from the projekt osłon stałych and from the IOR opinion in that application but not from the permit itself. The two regulations that make the post are Dz.U. 2023 poz. 195 (§ 3 ust. 3: no skierowanie for intraoral; § 3 ust. 4: **the DICOM obligation does not apply to intraoral**; § 5: exposure parameters go in the dokumentacja medyczna except for intraoral, **where a record that the examination was performed is still required**; 60-70 kV, rectangular collimation, thyroid shielding) and Dz.U. 2022 poz. 2759 (testy podstawowe co miesiąc and co 6 miesięcy; testy specjalistyczne **every 24 months for intraoral and every 12 for pantomograficzne and cefalometryczne**). Both Polish acts were fetched from dziennikustaw.gov.pl because ISAP serves a CAPTCHA. The English copy is anchored on the UK (IRR17 SI 2017/1075 and IR(ME)R17 SI 2017/1322) and says plainly that the answer is national: HSE registration before first use, the identity of the IR(ME)R practitioner recorded for every exposure, exposure factors recorded only on deviation from a guideline protocol, routine testing normally at intervals not exceeding three years but annual for hand-held sets, procedures reviewed at intervals not exceeding three years, staff training records at least five years, and **IR(ME)R specifies no retention period at all**, with the guidance advising five years. Source: the PHE/FGDP *Guidance Notes for Dental Practitioners on the Safe Use of X-ray Equipment*, 2nd edition, 2020. |
| La inspección sanitaria llega a la clínica: qué tiene que poder enseñar ese día | `inspeccion-sanitaria-clinica-dental` / `dental-practice-inspection-readiness` / `inspection-sanitaire-cabinet-dentaire` / `inspecao-sanitaria-clinica-dentaria` / `praxisbegehung-zahnarztpraxis` / `ispezione-sanitaria-studio-odontoiatrico` | inspección sanitaria clínica dental · Added 20 September 2026, intent verified that run in Spanish, German and Polish, and **the query is a different question in each, which is the whole reason this is not one post translated seven times**. Germany searches `Praxisbegehung Zahnarztpraxis` and the competing pages are chamber checklists (the Zahnärztekammer Berlin publishes a *Checkliste Praxisbegehung*, the KVSH another) plus hygiene suppliers, so the German copy is organised around the Begehung by the Gesundheitsamt and the hygiene, Medizinprodukte-Aufbereitung and QM documentation it turns on. Poland searches `kontrola sanepidu w gabinecie stomatologicznym` (Polish slug `kontrola-sanepidu-w-gabinecie-stomatologicznym`) and the competing pages are trade press, so that copy is organised around the Państwowa Inspekcja Sanitarna, the procedury przeciwepidemiczne, the sterilisation register and the staff sanitary-epidemiological examinations. Spain is not an announced Begehung at all but the autonomic **autorización sanitaria de funcionamiento** under Real Decreto 1277/2003 and its renewal, with an on-site inspection: the Comunidad de Madrid publishes a *Lista de verificación para clínicas dentales* (Dirección General de Inspección y Ordenación Sanitaria, asset dated 31 March 2025) and the Basque Government publishes the procedure including the inspection visit and the five-year renewal, so the Spanish copy is built on the autonomous community's own checklist and says plainly that the requirements differ by comunidad rather than publishing one national answer. Source each country from its own regulator or chamber the run it is written, never from another language's copy and never from the hygiene suppliers that dominate these results. **Four published rows are adjacent and this one links rather than repeats them**: `trazabilidad-esterilizacion-dental`, `auditoria-accesos-historia-clinica`, `rgpd-clinica-dental` and `abrir-clinica-dental-software`, plus the radiation row above. What makes it a guide and not a checklist reprint is the software half: which of the things an inspector asks for are records a practice can only produce if something was logging them all year, what has to be searchable on the day (sterilisation cycles by date, consents by patient, exposures by equipment, access log by user), and what is a paper folder that no software will save. Not legal advice  **Written 22 September 2026 in all seven locales, and the four authorities the row left open are now established.** **Spain**: the frame is not a Begehung but the autonomic autorización, RD 1277/2003 (tipología C.2.5.1, unidades U.44/U.45/U.35) plus Decreto 51/2006 and Orden 1158/2018 in Madrid; the Madrid checklist (ed. 31/03/2025) gives gabinete 8 m2 destinado en exclusiva, structure must match the filed planos, colegiación certificate valid at most 6 months, director asistencial on the plantilla, Orden de Visibilidad and plantilla displayed, and renovacion every five or eight years with three months notice (Euskadi: five years, with an inspection visit). New and worth carrying: **Real Decreto 239/2026, BOE 26 March 2026, in force 1 July 2026**, adds art. 7 Garantias de atencion sanitaria, an up-to-date staff-documentation duty. **France** is two controls, not one, and that is the whole shape of the post: ARS inspection of the risque infectieux (ARS Paca: dental activity in the inspection programme since 2015, suspension immediate of a centre or of a liberal practitioner for a maximum of five months, 2 and 8 such suspensions in 2025) plus the DGCCRF control of information des patients that ONCD announced on 14 January 2025 (tarifs, devis, 100% sante cosignes, tracabilite des DM). ARS Paca's September 2024 fiches give the marche en avant and the zone sale/propre layout; ONCD gives the arrete du 30 mai 2018 affichage and the 70 euro devis threshold. sante.gouv.fr is unreachable from the runner, so the DGS grille technique of 2011 could not be sourced and is not cited. **Italy**: autorizzazione is regional on top of art. 8-ter c. 2 D.Lgs. 502/1992 (required 'ove attrezzati per erogare prestazioni di chirurgia ambulatoriale'); Toscana Regolamento 61/R del 24 dicembre 2010 Allegato C is the worked example and the quotable one, with 'Le prestazioni effettuate devono essere registrate con completezza ed accuratezza e corredate dalle generalita riferite all'utente', the inventario delle apparecchiature, the documento sui guasti and the documented modalita di compilazione, conservazione, archiviazione dei documenti. **Portugal**: Portaria n.o 99/2024/1 de 13 de marco, in force the day after publication, revoking Portarias 268/2010 and 167-A/2014; licensing by mera comunicacao previa under art. 4.o n.o 4 a) DL 127/2014; art. 7.o splits processos clinicos from the five-year set, art. 8.o is the always-accessible documentation, art. 10.o n.o 5 allows one direcao clinica per clinic except single-professional consultorios. The ERS publishes its actual fiscalizacao form, Mod.133_02 (2024), which is the post's spine, and the SRER certidao must be afixada in a public place. **English copy is anchored on England and says so**: CQC registration under the Health and Social Care Act 2008 and SI 2014/2936, regs 12 and 17 the most breached, reg 17(2)(c) the contemporaneous-record duty, and the provider information request answered in five working days (statement of purpose, staff by role and hours, complaints with actions). CQC's dental guidance pages carry an under-review notice after the 2024 change of approach, and the post states that. Retrieval notes for the next run: cqc.org.uk, salute.gov.it and regione.lombardia.it refuse a plain fetch or 404, and CQC needs a browser user agent; sante.gouv.fr returns an empty reply entirely. PDFs from lzk-bw, zaek-berlin, comunidad.madrid, ers.pt and diariodarepublica need local text extraction, pdfminer.six in a venv, because the fetch tool returns raw PDF. |

Polish slug `umowa-powierzenia-przetwarzania-danych-w-gabinecie-stomatologicznym`. Not country-locked, because **art. 28 RGPD is the same floor everywhere the site publishes**, but each post cites its own authority on top: verified this run, the AEPD publishes both a model clause set for processor contracts (`aepd.es/documento/clausulas-contratos-encargado-tratamiento.pdf`) and an FAQ on the contract's required content, and the Landeszahnärztekammer Baden-Württemberg publishes a whole *Kapitel 2 - Auftragsverarbeitung* for practices; CNIL, the Garante, UODO and the CNPD are the counterparts and are established by the run that writes those copies. **Three published rows are adjacent and this one must not repeat them**: `rgpd-clinica-dental` is the general obligation, `preguntas-antes-de-firmar-software-dental` is the commercial due diligence, and `self-hosting-con-tu-informatico` is the SLA with the integrator. This row is the one contract, clause by clause: the object and duration, the instruction-only limit, confidentiality, the security measures annex, **the sub-processor list and the right to object to a new one**, assistance with patient rights requests, audit rights, and what happens to the data at the end of the contract. The search results in Spanish are consultancies and one competitor's own blog, which is exactly the gap. Two things make it useful rather than a template reprint and both are ours to say honestly: an AI or analytics clause that licenses patient data for model training is a processor term a practice can refuse, and it is published in the open by at least one vendor already in Queue A (see the Practice-Web row in A3), so the post can describe the shape of such a clause without naming anyone; and self-hosting does not remove the contract, it moves it, because the backup, the remote support session and the hosting provider are each a processor even when the database sits in the clinic. State what a practice actually has to hold, and say plainly it is not legal advice |

Establish the out-of-hours arrangement from the national chamber or council in every language, because it differs. **Three published rows are adjacent and this one must not repeat any**: `lista-espera-clinica-dental` fills a hole that already opened while this row protects one before it is needed, `organizar-agenda-clinica-dental` is the shape of the day across several sillones, and `automatizar-recepcion-clinica` is the front desk in general. Link all three. **Also not this post**: the medical emergency in the chair (síncope, anafilaxia, the emergency kit) is a different subject with different sources, and the post says so in a line rather than half-covering it. What it is: how many buffer slots a practice holds and where in the day they sit, what the person answering the phone may decide alone and what has to reach a dentist, **what is recorded from that call whether or not the patient ever arrives**, how an urgent patient is fitted in without pushing over the appointments behind them, and what the practice can measure afterwards (how many buffers went unused, how many urgencias became a treatment plan). Clinical triage content is sourced from a professional body or a peer-reviewed source with the date consulted, never from a vendor's blog, and the post states plainly that it is not a clinical protocol. `schedule-day.png` is in the screenshots directory and is the natural first image |

German is the better half of the post and has real primary sources: the Bundeszahnärztekammer and several Landeszahnärztekammern publish position papers on the **Ausfallhonorar**, and they condition it — a Bestellpraxis with the slot reserved for that patient alone, explicit prior notice that is not buried in small print, no fault on the patient's side, the slot not refilled, and the loss calculated concretely and evidenced rather than charged as a flat fee. **Whether anything can be charged at all is national and conditioned**, so each language sources its own basis (dental chamber, professional body or consumer law) with the date consulted, and says plainly it is not legal advice. | Pacientes de otro país: presupuesto, consentimiento e historia clínica en un idioma que entiendan | `pacientes-extranjeros-clinica-dental` / `treating-patients-from-abroad-dental` / `patients-etrangers-cabinet-dentaire` / `pacientes-estrangeiros-clinica-dentaria` / `auslaendische-patienten-zahnarztpraxis` / `pazienti-stranieri-studio-dentistico` | pacientes extranjeros clínica dental · Added 1 October 2026, intent verified this run in Spanish and Polish. Written from both ends, because one post serves two different readers: `es`, `pt`, `pl` and `it` are destination markets where this is routine work, and `de`, `fr` and `en` are origin markets whose reader is the patient's own dentist picking up the aftercare. **The one thing this post exists to correct**: the European Health Insurance Card does not pay for planned private dental treatment. The card covers unplanned necessary care during a temporary stay, while planned cross-border treatment runs through Directive 2011/24/EU reimbursement or prior authorisation instead, so a clinic that treats the card as a payment method has mis-sold the visit. Source that from official pages only, the EU's own *Your Europe* planned-healthcare pages, each country's national contact point (`sanidad.gob.es/pnc` for Spain) and the national social-security page for the card itself. The consent half is national and is where the software matters: consent has to be in terms the patient actually understands, which for a foreign-language patient means the form, the medical questionnaire, the quote and the post-operative instructions all exist in that language, and the record has to show which version was signed. Then the part everyone forgets, the handover, what the patient takes home so their own dentist can continue, and in what format. Distinct from `paciente-pide-su-historia-clinica`, which is the records request, and from `teleodontologia`, which is the remote consultation. Not legal advice, and say so |
