Polish slug `wniosek-o-dokumentacje-medyczna-od-podmiotu-trzeciego`. Distinct from three rows that already exist and the post should say which is which: `paciente-pide-su-historia-clinica` is the patient's own access request, `derivacion-pacientes-clinica-dental` is a referral the clinic itself initiates, and `traspaso-clinica-dental-historias` is the sale of the practice. This row is the request that arrives from someone who is not the patient: a court, a private insurer, the patient's lawyer, another dentist, a labour-risk mutua, the heirs of a patient who has died. **Multi-locale but sourced nationally in each, the `rgpd-clinica-dental` pattern, never translated**, because the two markets checked this run disagree on the one line a clinic thinks it knows. Spanish professional guidance is to hand over a **copy** and keep the original in the archive, to put imaging on separate media where an original is needed for a second opinion, and to flag privacy-sensitive photographs to the court in a sealed envelope. German law does not end there: § 630g BGB gives the patient the access right with the **first copy free of charge**, a private insurer gets nothing until the patient has signed a Schweigepflichtentbindung, and in a liability trial the ZPO can require the **originals** to be produced to the court or to the expert it appoints. So "always a copy, never the original" is correct in Spain and wrong in a German courtroom, and a post that states it flatly is wrong in at least one locale. Source Spain from the BOE text of Ley 41/2002 (art. 16 on uses of the record, art. 18 on access, **art. 18.3 on the professional's anotaciones subjetivas and art. 18.4 on a deceased patient**) and the AEPD for the data-protection half; source Germany from § 630g at `gesetze-im-internet.de` and the Landeszahnärztekammer merkblätter, which publish this as practice guidance. **Never from a negligence firm's blog**: they rank for this query, they are written for the other side, and several state the Spanish rule as if it were universal. What the software has to do is the half the reader can act on: export a **defined subset** rather than the whole record, redact third-party data and the subjective annotations the law excludes, and log the disclosure itself (who asked, on what legal basis, what was sent, on what date, who authorised it) so that the clinic can show a year later what left the building, which is what `auditoria-accesos-historia-clinica` covers for internal access. **Two things not to do: never publish one list of who may ask across all seven locales, and never let the post read as coaching a refusal of a court order or a charge for a copy that national law makes free.** Not legal advice, and say so |
| El paciente deja el tratamiento a medias: qué se anota, qué se cierra y qué se cobra | `paciente-abandona-tratamiento-dental` / `patient-abandons-dental-treatment` / `patient-abandonne-son-traitement-dentaire` / `paciente-abandona-o-tratamento-dentario` / `behandlungsabbruch-zahnarztpraxis` / `paziente-abbandona-le-cure-odontoiatriche` | paciente abandona tratamiento dental · Added 5 October 2026, intent verified this run in Spanish and German. Polish slug `pacjent-przerywa-leczenie-stomatologiczne`. Distinct from four rows that already exist, and the difference is the money and the half-finished tooth: `seguimiento-presupuestos-dentales` is a quote never accepted, `reducir-ausencias-citas-dental` and `politica-cancelacion-citas-dental` are one missed appointment, and `reactivar-pacientes-inactivos` is marketing to a patient who simply drifted. This row is a plan that **started**, was partly paid and then stopped: a tooth prepared with no crown seated, an orthodontic case mid-alignment, a lab case already manufactured. The post has three parts and **only the first is the same in every market**. The clinical-record part, verified in Spanish this run: an abandonment is documented to the same standard as an alta voluntaria, which means date and time, the clinical situation, the treatment proposed, **the risks of stopping**, the alternatives offered, the patient's express refusal, an assessment of their capacity, and evidence that the clinic offered the appointments and the patient did not attend. Source that from the BOE text of Ley 41/2002 (art. 2.4 on refusal, art. 21 on alta voluntaria), not from an insurer's blog, and note the discharge report the patient should leave with. The billing part is **national and must never be translated**. Germany has explicit machinery for it and the German post has to use it: the KZVen publish guidance on **Teilleistungen** for Zahnersatz, the HKP must be marked as containing them with the reason for the abandonment recorded, the **laboratory has to be told promptly** so work in progress can be stopped, material and lab costs already incurred are billed in full, and the Festzuschüsse are paid at a percentage of the finding according to how far the work got. Spain has no equivalent tariff instrument, so the Spanish post argues from the consent document and the contract instead, and says so plainly rather than inventing a rule. **Two things not to do: never carry a percentage, a fraction or a "you may keep this much" line into a locale whose own sources do not publish it, and do not let the post turn into debt collection**, because the abandonment note is a clinical record entry before it is an invoice; the recovery half belongs to `control-deuda-pacientes`. What the software has to support: suspend a plan without deleting the stages already done, keep the completed stages billable and the rest not, keep a deposit visible as money that is not yet income (`anticipos-y-pagos-a-cuenta-dental`), timestamp every contact attempt, and leave the record reachable for the retention period in `conservar-historia-clinica-dental-plazos`. Not legal advice, and say so |
| Publicar el antes y el después: por qué el consentimiento del tratamiento no sirve | `fotos-antes-y-despues-pacientes-consentimiento` / `before-after-patient-photos-consent-dental` / `photos-avant-apres-patients-consentement-dentaire` / `fotos-antes-e-depois-de-pacientes-consentimento` / `vorher-nachher-fotos-patienten-einwilligung` / `foto-prima-e-dopo-pazienti-consenso` | fotos antes y después pacientes consentimiento · Added 5 October 2026, intent verified this run in Spanish and Italian. Polish slug `zdjecia-przed-i-po-zgoda-pacjenta`. Distinct from three rows that each stop just short of it: `fotografia-clinica-dental` is taking and organising the images, `consentimiento-informado-digital-dental` is consent to be treated, and `marketing-local-clinica-dental` is promotion in general. This row answers the question those three leave open, which is whether **this** image may be published and where. The one thing the post exists to establish is the same everywhere: consent to be treated, and even consent to be photographed clinically, is **not** consent to publish. Publication consent is a separate written permission, tied to a purpose, naming the channels, limited in time and revocable. **Sourced nationally and never translated**, because the two markets checked this run do not give the same answer and the gap is the whole value of the row. In Spain the defect is **scope, not absence**: the AEPD fined a clinic 10.000 € in procedure **PS/00130/2024** for putting before and after images on Facebook and Instagram when the consent on file covered medical or teaching use only, and its reasoning is that authorisation for scientific or clinical use does not authorise a commercial profile. Italy adds a second and harder layer **on top of** consent: the before/after format and patient testimonials are themselves restricted as pubblicità sanitaria, so in Italy a post with a perfectly valid consent can still be prohibited, and the Garante has fined 20.000 € and 5.000 € in image cases, the second for a research ePoster rather than an advert. Source Spain from the AEPD's own resolution and its guidance, Italy from the Garante's own provvedimenti plus the CAO and FNOMCeO deontological material, and the other locales from their own supervisory authority before writing a word. **Never from a dental marketing agency's guide**, which is what ranks for this query and which is written to sell the practice the post is questioning. **Three things not to do: do not write it as "get a signature and you are covered", which is exactly what the Italian half disproves; do not reuse the Spanish fine as though it stated the rule in the other six locales; and do not suggest that cropping, blurring or omitting the name makes the image anonymous**, because a clinical image is health data precisely in that it reveals the person was treated. What the software has to do, and this is the half a reader can act on today: hold the publication consent as its own versioned document separate from the treatment consent, record purpose and channels on it, attach it to the **specific image** rather than to the patient, and make a withdrawal actually propagate, which means the record has to know where the image was published in the first place. Not legal advice, and say so |
| Cámaras en la clínica: dónde se pueden poner y dónde desde luego no | `videovigilancia-clinica-dental` / `cctv-dental-practice` / `videosurveillance-cabinet-dentaire` / `videovigilancia-clinica-dentaria` / `videoueberwachung-zahnarztpraxis` / `videosorveglianza-studio-dentistico` | videovigilancia clínica dental · Added 6 October 2026, intent verified this run in Spanish and Polish. Polish slug `monitoring-w-gabinecie-stomatologicznym`. Distinct from the three rows it sits next to and the post should say so: `rgpd-clinica-dental` is the patient record, `auditoria-accesos-historia-clinica` is who opened the file, and `permisos-acceso-personal-clinica` is what each user may see. This row is the camera on the wall, which is a **separate processing with its own legal basis, its own signage and its own retention**, and which most clinics install for theft and for staff disputes without ever writing it into the registro de actividades de tratamiento. **Sourced nationally and never translated**, because the two markets checked this run reach the same practical answer through completely different machinery. Spain has an enforcement record and no dental-specific statute: in **EXP202305156** the AEPD sanctioned a dental clinic, AXARQUIA VELEZ DENTAL, S.L., over eleven cameras — two in recepción, **one inside each of the five gabinetes/quirófanos**, one in the almacén, one in the laboratorio, one in the pasillo and one in the despacho — for breach of **art. 5.1.c) RGPD (minimización)**, typified under art. 83.5, with a 5.000 € fine for the gabinete cameras settled at 3.000 € under the voluntary-payment reduction. Three details in that file are what make the post useful rather than scary, so do not drop them: the recordings carried **no audio** and were kept **seven days**, and the clinic's stated purposes were patient safety and staff supervision — in other words a clinic doing most things right still lost on the camera's **location**, because a gabinete is where a patient is undressed, sedated or reclined for an hour. Poland reaches the same place from the opposite direction: **art. 23a of the ustawa o działalności leczniczej** lets the head of a podmiot leczniczy set monitoring in the regulamin organizacyjny, but its third limb, the one covering rooms where świadczenia zdrowotne are actually given, is written **only for szpitale, zakłady opiekuńczo-lecznicze, pielęgnacyjno-opiekuńcze, rehabilitacji leczniczej and hospicja**, so a gabinet stomatologiczny has no basis under it at all and is left with the first limb, pomieszczenia **ogólnodostępne**, plus a maximum retention of **three months**. State that as the finding it is: in Poland the question is not whether the camera is proportionate, it is whether the statute gives the gabinet any basis at all. Live primary material to use rather than commentary: the RPO's standing case **VII.501.161.2023** and the Ministry of Health's reply of **13 May 2025** on `bip.brpo.gov.pl`, which is where the Polish argument currently sits. **Two things not to do.** Do not write the Spanish fine as though it banned cameras in a clinic, because it did not: it was decided on minimisation and on where the camera pointed. And do not reuse either country's answer in the other five locales — Germany runs through § 4 BDSG and the DSK's Orientierungshilfe, Italy adds **art. 4 dello Statuto dei lavoratori**, which makes staff monitoring a matter for a union agreement or the Ispettorato before the GDPR is even reached, and France through the CNIL — so each post is sourced from its own authority. The AEPD resolution was read this run from a third-party mirror (`expertorrhh.lefebvre.es`); **fetch it from `aepd.es` before quoting it**. The product paragraph is small and honest, which is the right size here: the footage is health data about who attended, it is not in the clinical record and our audit trail does not cover it, so what the record can do is hold the camera system in the registro de actividades de tratamiento alongside everything else. **Written 6 October 2026, es/en/fr/pt/de/it/pl, and the row needed three corrections.** **The AEPD resolution could not be sourced and is not in the published posts.** `aepd.es` serves its HTML and its `/documento/*.pdf` guides normally, but resolution PDFs are named by procedure number (`ps-00130-2024.pdf`), not by expediente, so `EXP202305156` has no guessable filename; `/documento/exp202305156.pdf` returns HTTP 500 or 503, the resolutions search rejects every text-query parameter tried (`?q=`, `?search_api_fulltext=`) with HTTP 403, and no `aepd.es` result surfaces for the entity name. A later run that learns the PS number can add it; until then do not reuse the eleven-camera description from any mirror. **What replaced it is stronger and should be kept:** the AEPD "Guía sobre el uso de videocámaras para seguridad y otras finalidades", **February 2025**, whose §4.4 *Sanidad y centros de asistencia* admits image capture for care purposes only in cases such as *"la monitorización de pacientes en las unidades de vigilancia intensiva, o la telemedicina"* under art. 9.2.c) or 9.2.h) RGPD, which is how the gabinete is ruled out without quoting a sanction; §2.2.3 on minimisation and *máscaras de privacidad*; §2.3.9 fixing deletion at one month; §2.3.10 on the right of access by certified statement. Plus the fact sheet `fichas-videovigilancia-6-camaras-control-laboral.pdf`: *"En ningún caso se ubicarán en zonas de vestuarios, baños y espacios de descanso de los trabajadores"* and *"No se registrarán conversaciones privadas"*. **Careful with one trap in that guide:** the line *"espacios protegidos por el derecho a la intimidad como baños, vestuarios"* sits in the section on **centros educativos**, not healthcare, so it is not a rule about clinics and the published post does not use it. Second correction: **the Polish finding in this row was right but incomplete.** Art. 23a has a pkt 2 the row did not mention, covering rooms where świadczenia are given *"jeżeli wynika to z przepisów odrębnych"*, and for dentistry no such przepisy exist, so a gabinet is left with pkt 1 alone. Two obligations worth more than the pkt 3 argument and verified from the consolidated text (Dz.U. 2025 poz. 450, `api.sejm.gov.pl/eli/acts/DU/2025/450/text.pdf`, readable with `pdftotext`): **art. 23 ust. 2** removes a praktyka zawodowa's exemption from having a regulamin organizacyjny as soon as it runs monitoring, and **art. 24 ust. 2** requires the art. 23a ust. 1 information to be published *"w miejscu udzielania świadczeń oraz na stronie internetowej"*, so a sticker on the door is not enough. The live Polish material is better than the RPO page alone: the **UODO President's letter to the Minister of Health of 27 January 2025, DOL.413.13.2024** (`uodo.gov.pl/pl/file/5682`) states that art. 23a ust. 1 *"nie jest zatem jasna"* and that the reference to general data-protection rules *"z całą pewnością nie jest wystarczające"*. Third: **four of the seven locales landed on a different answer than the row anticipated and each is the headline of its own post.** Germany is the strictest: DSK Kurzpapier Nr. 15 puts *ärztliche Behandlungs- und Warteräume* in the list of places where surveillance is *"in der Regel nicht zu erwarten und ... nicht akzeptiert"*, so the **waiting room** goes too, Sanitärbereiche are *"ausnahmslos"* out, the deletion guide is **48 hours** and not a month, and the Sächsische Datenschutz- und Transparenzbeauftragte makes it a question of the clock, not just the room (not permissible *"während der gewöhnlichen Praxisöffnungszeiten"* for freely accessible areas). Portugal is next: **CNPD lists zonas de espera among the places a camera may not cover**, with 30 days plus deletion within 48 hours after, and sound barred unless the premises are closed. Italy has an administrative gate nobody else has: **art. 4 l. 300/1970 needs an accordo sindacale or an Ispettorato authorisation before installation**, and the Garante expressly carries that condition into its own guidance. The UK is the outlier in the other direction: **the ICO sets no retention figure at all**, *"it is the purpose of your processing that should determine your retention period"*, so never port the Spanish month or the Polish three months into the English post. §4 BDSG could not be read this run (`gesetze-im-internet.de` returned HTTP 503 to every attempt) and the German post does not need it; `isap.sejm.gov.pl` is behind a CAPTCHA and `diariodarepublica.pt` still returns no readable text, so use `api.sejm.gov.pl` and the CNPD pages instead. Files: `blog/{es,en,fr,pt,de,it,pl}/` under the slugs in this row plus `monitoring-w-gabinecie-stomatologicznym` in `pl/`. |
| Cobrar en efectivo: el límite, el fraccionamiento y lo que el software tiene que dejar escrito | `pago-en-efectivo-clinica-dental` / `cash-payments-dental-practice` / `paiement-en-especes-cabinet-dentaire` / `pagamentos-em-dinheiro-clinica-dentaria` / `barzahlung-zahnarztpraxis` / `pagamenti-in-contanti-studio-dentistico` | límite pago en efectivo clínica dental · Added 6 October 2026, intent verified this run in Spanish and Polish. Polish slug `platnosci-gotowka-w-gabinecie-stomatologicznym`. Distinct from four rows that already exist, and the difference is the **legal ceiling** rather than the bookkeeping: `caja-diaria-clinica-dental` is the daily close, `tpv-datafono-clinica-dental` is card reconciliation, `control-deuda-pacientes` is instalments as a receivable and `anticipos-y-pagos-a-cuenta-dental` is money that is not yet income. This row is the question underneath all four, and it is sharp in dentistry precisely because a treatment plan of 3.000 to 6.000 € paid monthly in cash is ordinary: **a plan paid in instalments is one operation, not twelve**. **Country-specific numbers, sourced nationally, never translated** — and this row is the clearest case in the file of two markets giving **opposite** answers. Spain, verified from the BOE this run: **art. 7 de la Ley 7/2012 in its wording from Ley 11/2021** caps cash at **1.000 €** where either party acts as empresario o profesional, **10.000 €** where the payer is an individual proving no fiscal domicile in Spain, and the fractioning rule is explicit, "se sumarán los importes de todas las operaciones o pagos en que se haya podido fraccionar la entrega de bienes o la prestación de servicios". Three consequences that are the whole post for a Spanish reader: the infraction is **grave** with a proportional fine of **25 %** of the base, **both payer and receiver are liable jointly**, so the clinic is a sujeto infractor and not a witness, and the justificantes must be kept **five years**. Poland is the mirror image: **art. 19 of the Prawo przedsiębiorców** requires a payment account only when the other party is **another przedsiębiorca** and the transaction exceeds **15.000 zł**, so a patient paying in cash is not capped at all, and the Polish post is about documentation and the kasa fiskalna rather than about a ceiling. That contrast is the reason this row exists and the reason it must never be one post translated seven ways. **Figures to establish from each country's own source before writing a number, none of them verified this run**: Italy is the one to be most careful with, because the threshold has moved repeatedly and a search returns both 3.000 € and 5.000 € on the same day — take it from **art. 49 del d.lgs. 231/2007** as currently in force and not from a fiscal blog; then Portugal (Lei 92/2017 and the Lei Geral Tributária, and note `diariodarepublica.pt` returned no readable text to a plain fetch this run), France (Code monétaire et financier, with the standing warning in this file that Légifrance answers 403), and Germany, which has **no general cash ceiling** and instead an identification duty under the GwG, so the German post answers a different question and must not imply a limit that does not exist. **Two things not to do.** Do not describe splitting a plan into sub-limit cash payments as a workaround, in any locale: in Spain it is the exact conduct the aggregation rule catches, and the Italian provision has its own frazionamento artificioso doctrine. And do not give a figure without its date and its article, because this is the row most likely to go stale. The product paragraph earns its place here more than in most rows: what the software has to do is record the **method** on every payment line, keep the treatment plan and its instalments linked to one operation so the total is visible rather than twelve unrelated receipts, and produce the five-year justificante trail on demand. Not legal, fiscal or accounting advice, and say so |
| Enviar una radiografía o un informe: por qué el canal importa más que el consentimiento | `enviar-datos-de-pacientes-por-email-o-whatsapp` / `sending-patient-data-by-email-or-whatsapp` / `envoyer-des-donnees-patients-par-email-ou-whatsapp` / `enviar-dados-de-pacientes-por-email-ou-whatsapp` / `patientendaten-per-email-oder-whatsapp-versenden` / `inviare-dati-dei-pazienti-per-email-o-whatsapp` | enviar radiografía paciente whatsapp · Added 6 October 2026, intent verified this run in Spanish and German. Polish slug `przesylanie-danych-pacjentow-emailem-lub-whatsappem`. Distinct from four rows and the post has to open by saying which is which, because this is the row most at risk of being written as a duplicate: `sms-whatsapp-email-recordatorios` and `recordatorios-cita-whatsapp-dental` are **reminders**, which carry no clinical content and are a marketing-consent question; `paciente-pide-su-historia-clinica` is the **right of access**, which settles what you must hand over and says almost nothing about by what means; and `portal-del-paciente-dental` is the alternative this row ends up recommending. What this row answers is the everyday one nobody answers for a clinic: the orthodontist asks for the panorámica, the lab asks for the photos, the patient asks for their informe "por WhatsApp" — and the consent is not the hard part, **the channel is**. **Sourced nationally and never translated**, and the two markets checked this run disagree in a way worth building the post on. Spain's own authority is narrow and permissive: AEPD **FAQ-1638** answers only the email question and answers it with a condition, "En un centro sanitario debe evitarse enviar información sanitaria mediante correos electrónicos o redes públicas abiertas, salvo que los datos se hayan cifrado" — encryption is what makes it acceptable, and the FAQ does not address messengers at all. Germany is both stricter and far more specific: the **Landeszahnärztekammer Baden-Württemberg's Merkblatt "Versand von Patientendaten" (8/2020)** works from **Art. 32 DSGVO plus § 203 StGB**, allows transmission to a third party only on consent, a statutory duty or the dentist's own legitimate interest, says the Landesdatenschutzbehörden advise **against fax generally**, and on email requires the **attachment** encrypted at **AES-256 or better** with the password sent by another channel and — the detail worth the whole post — **no patient name in the subject line or body, because those are never encrypted**. On messengers it is flat: "Die meisten Messenger-Dienste, wie z.B. WhatsApp sind zur Übermittlung von Patientendaten aus datenschutzrechtlicher Sicht ungeeignet." It also names the low-tech answer as fully compliant, handing the patient the referral and the radiograph on CD or USB in the practice, which is the kind of concrete alternative this post needs and which no vendor blog will give. **Two sourcing cautions, both established this run.** The LZK document carries its own disclaimer that it is "nicht durch die Aufsichtsbehörden geprüft", so cite it as a **chamber recommendation and not as a supervisory authority's position**; and the COEM's data-protection document for Madrid clinics (`coem.org.es/media/news/pdf/NRPD.pdf`) is pre-RGPD guidance from 2018 and mentions neither WhatsApp nor email transmission, so it is **not** the Spanish source for this row and a later run need not fetch it again. For the remaining locales source the national authority directly — the Garante for Italy, the CNIL for France, the CNPD for Portugal and UODO for Poland — and do **not** lean on the widely repeated claim that end-to-end encryption settles the matter, because what the German and Spanish positions both turn on is metadata, the device, the backup and the processor relationship, not the transport. **Two things not to do**: do not write "get consent and send it", which is the advice this row exists to replace, and do not present a blurred or name-stripped radiograph as anonymous. The product mention is the natural close rather than a bolt-on, because the honest answer to the whole question is to stop sending files: a patient portal where the document is fetched over an authenticated session leaves an access log, needs no password out of band, and does not put a radiograph in a chat backup. Not legal advice, and say so |
| Amalgama: la prohibición de 2025, la excepción que hay que justificar y los residuos que no desaparecen | `amalgama-dental-prohibicion-residuos` / `dental-amalgam-ban-waste-records` / `amalgame-dentaire-interdiction-dechets` / `amalgama-dentaria-proibicao-residuos` / `amalgam-verbot-abfall-zahnarztpraxis` / `amalgama-dentale-divieto-rifiuti` | amalgama dental prohibición 2025 · Added 7 October 2026, intent verified this run in Spanish and German. Polish slug `amalgamat-zakaz-i-odpady-w-gabinecie-stomatologicznym`. **This row is current law, not history, and it is the only row in Queue B whose central fact changed while the blog was being written.** Distinct from the two rows nearest it: `registro-implantes-trazabilidad` is the lot number of something implanted and `trazabilidad-esterilizacion-dental` is the autoclave cycle. This row is a **material the clinic may no longer choose freely, plus a waste stream that outlives the ban**, and the second half is the part every vendor blog gets wrong. **The EU layer is verified this run from two official sources and is the same in all seven locales**, so it is the one part of this row that does not need national sourcing: **Regulation (EU) 2024/1849**, amending Regulation (EU) 2017/852 on mercury, means that **from 1 January 2025 dental amalgam "should not be used for dental treatment in the Union, except when deemed strictly necessary by the dental practitioner based on the specific medical needs of the patient"** (European Parliament Legislative Observatory summary, `oeil.europarl.europa.eu`, procedure document 1786205). Export of dental amalgam is prohibited **from 1 January 2025** and the manufacturing and import ban applies **from 1 July 2026**. There is a **temporary derogation until 30 June 2026** for "Member States in which dental amalgam is the only publicly reimbursed material at a rate of at least 90 % under national law, and in which such reimbursement is not yet possible for mercury-free alternatives as of 1 January 2025", with those states providing reasoned explanations; the Commission's own page (`environment.ec.europa.eu/topics/chemicals/mercury_en`) describes the same derogation as covering "use, manufacture, and import" for states needing more time. The Commission reviews the use exemptions **by 31 December 2029**. **Establish per market, before writing, whether that derogation was invoked and whether it has now lapsed**, because a post that tells a German or Polish reader amalgam is simply banned may be wrong for part of 2025 and 2026 and will certainly be wrong about why. **The second half of the post is the one worth writing and it is what nobody writes**: the separator and waste duties in **art. 10 of Regulation (EU) 2017/852 are triggered by removing old amalgam fillings, not only by placing new ones**, so they survive the use ban entirely and will outlive it by decades. Read this run from `legislation.gov.uk/eur/2017/852/article/10`, which serves the article text cleanly: dental amalgam only in **pre-dosed encapsulated form** since 1 January 2019 with bulk mercury prohibited; facilities "in which dental amalgam is used or dental amalgam fillings or teeth containing such fillings are removed" must have **amalgam separators**; separators put into service from **1 January 2018** must retain **at least 95 %** of amalgam particles and **from 1 January 2021 all separators in use** must meet that level; separators maintained per the manufacturer's instructions; and amalgam waste "including amalgam residues, particles and fillings, and teeth" must be "handled and collected by an authorised waste management establishment", never released "directly or indirectly ... into the environment under any circumstances". Note also the **pre-existing restriction since 1 July 2018** on deciduous teeth, children under 15 and pregnant or breastfeeding patients, again subject to strict medical necessity, because readers conflate it with the 2025 ban. **Two sourcing facts established this run.** `eur-lex.europa.eu` **answers HTTP 202 with an empty body** to `curl` and to a plain fetch, on the HTML, the PDF and the `/eli/` endpoints alike, so the regulation could not be read from the Official Journal this run; `oeil.europarl.europa.eu` and `environment.ec.europa.eu` are the official EU substitutes that did work, and a later run should still quote the articles from EUR-Lex when it is reachable. And `legislation.gov.uk` serves **retained** EU law, so it is sound for the text of art. 10 as adopted but must not be used for anything post-Brexit or for the 2024 amendment: the English post needs the ICO-style caution this file already carries, since the UK is outside Regulation 2024/1849 and Northern Ireland is a special case under the Windsor Framework. **The German material is real, dental and clinic-side, and none of it is primary**: the trade press and the chambers (`zm-online.de`, `zwp-online.info`, `iww.de`, and the LZK BW Praxishandbuch `phb.lzk-bw.de`) describe the separator as mandatory since 1989, replaced every six to twelve months, inspected every five years, with the waste under **Abfallschlüssel 18 01 10\*** and the measures documented in the Hygieneplan per the RKI recommendation, disposal by a certified firm only. **Re-source every one of those from a German authority before writing a number** (the Abfallverzeichnis-Verordnung for the waste key, the Abwasserverordnung for the separator, the Land chamber for the inspection interval), and cite the chamber documents as chamber recommendations, the caution this file already records for LZK BW. **Two things not to do.** Do not write the post as though the separator stopped mattering in 2025, which is the single most likely error and the opposite of what art. 10 says. And do not give clinical guidance on what to do with a patient's existing amalgam fillings: whether to leave or replace a sound restoration is a clinical decision and the post's job stops at the record. **The product paragraph is unusually well earned here and should stay small**: the medical-need exception is now something the clinic has to be able to **prove per treatment**, which makes it a field on the treatment line and a note in the clinical record rather than a policy on a wall, and the separator's maintenance and collection certificates are documents with dates that something has to hold. Not legal, clinical or waste-compliance advice, and say so |
| Publicidad de la clínica dental: qué se puede decir y qué desde luego no | `publicidad-clinica-dental-normativa` / `dental-practice-advertising-rules` / `publicite-cabinet-dentaire-regles` / `publicidade-clinica-dentaria-regras` / `werbung-zahnarztpraxis-regeln` / `pubblicita-studio-dentistico-regole` | publicidad clínica dental normativa · Added 7 October 2026, intent verified this run in Spanish and Italian. Polish slug `reklama-gabinetu-stomatologicznego`. Distinct from the three marketing rows already published and the post must open by saying so: `marketing-local-clinica-dental` is how to be found, `resenas-google-clinica-dental` and `responder-resenas-negativas-dental` are reputation. This row is the **legal ceiling on the claim itself**, and it is the row most likely to stop a clinic doing something it was about to do. **Sourced nationally and never translated**, and the two markets checked this run do not merely differ in detail, they **disagree about whether promotion is permitted at all**, which is the strongest such contrast in this file after the cash row. **Italy is verified verbatim this run** from the FNOMCeO letter on health advertising (`portale.fnomceo.it/wp-content/uploads/2022/10/LETTERA-SU-PUBBLICITA-SANITARIA.pdf`, dated 6 October 2022), which quotes **art. 1, comma 525 della Legge 30 dicembre 2018, n. 145**: informative communications by private care structures and registered professionals may contain only the information referred to in art. 2 of d.l. 223/2006 "funzionali a garantire la sicurezza dei trattamenti sanitari, escluso qualsiasi elemento di carattere promozionale o suggestivo, nel rispetto della libera e consapevole determinazione del paziente, a tutela della salute pubblica, della dignità della persona e del suo diritto" to correct health information. **That is a prohibition on promotion as such, not a truthfulness standard**, and it is the whole Italian post. Two further details from the same document, both worth keeping: **art. 1, comma 536** makes the territorial professional orders act **disciplinarily** against registered professionals or companies and **report the violation to AGCOM**, so the enforcer is the Ordine and not a consumer agency; and the Codice deontologico requires information to be "veritiera, corretta e funzionale all'oggetto dell'informazione, mai equivoca, ingannevole e denigratoria", permitting **comparative** health advertising only where there are "indicatori clinici misurabili, certi e condivisi dalla comunità scientifica". **Spain is the opposite shape**: promotion is allowed and what is regulated is truthfulness, price completeness and who may appear. Verified this run from the COEM "Decálogo sobre publicidad de tratamientos en clínicas dentales en la Comunidad de Madrid" (`coem.org.es/pdf/DECALOGO_PUBLICIDAD_COEM.pdf`, PDF metadata dated **23 July 2025**, so unlike the COEM data-protection document this file rejected it is current), whose value is that **every rule carries a footnote to a named statute**, which is what makes it usable rather than just quotable. The nationally applicable ones, and the ones the Spanish post should be built on: **art. 3.e) Ley 34/1988 General de Publicidad** (engañosa, which is where "implantes gratis", "desde X euros" and a "garantía de por vida" on a clinical act all land); **art. 20.1.c) del TRLGDCU (RDL 1/2007)** requiring the **total price of the complete treatment**, so "implante x euros" without the crown and "ortodoncia desde x euros" without a final figure are the named violations; **art. 80.7 del RDL 1/2014**, which bars public advertising of health products intended for exclusive professional use, meaning **implant, aligner, veneer and whitening brands may not be advertised to patients**; **art. 38.8 del RD 1591/2009**, which prohibits in advertising of techniques tied to specific health products "cualquier mención que haga referencia a personas que puedan, debido a su notoriedad, incitar a su utilización", the celebrity rule; **art. 9 y 9.2.e) de la Ley 16/2011 de contratos de crédito al consumo** for financing, where "sin intereses" does not excuse omitting the TAE and the total payable; and the sharpest of them, **la disposición adicional segunda de la Ley 44/2003 de ordenación de las profesiones sanitarias**, under which **Spain recognises no dental specialities, so no clinic or dentist may advertise as "especialista"** in any area of dentistry. That last one is the finding most Spanish readers will not know and it should be the callout. **One trap in that source, and it is the reason this row says "sourced nationally" twice**: four of the decálogo's ten points are footnoted to **Ley 11/1998 de Protección de los Consumidores de la Comunidad de Madrid** (arts. 13.3, 14.1, 14.3, 14.4), which is **autonomic**. The small-print rule, the "rebajas" rule requiring the previous and the reduced price, and the deferred-payment disclosures are **Madrid law and must not be stated as Spanish law**; check the equivalent autonomic consumer statute or say plainly that the rule varies by comunidad autónoma. **For the remaining locales source the national regime directly and expect it to be a different post again**: Germany runs on the **Heilmittelwerbegesetz** plus the Land chamber's Berufsordnung, France on the **Code de la santé publique** as liberalised after the 2020 Conseil d'État decisions and the Ordre's own guidance, Portugal on the **ERS** and the Ordem dos Médicos Dentistas, and Poland on the advertising limits for medical activity in the **ustawa o działalności leczniczej**, which changed in recent years and must be read current. **Two things not to do.** Do not port the Italian "no promotional element" rule into any other locale, because outside Italy it is simply wrong and it would tell a clinic it may not advertise at all. And do not write a price example as compliant: give the rule and let the clinic apply it, because the examples are exactly where an autonomic rule hides. The product mention is small and is about the **quote**, not the advert: a published price is only defensible if the clinic can show what the complete treatment included on the day it was quoted, which is the treatment plan and its versions rather than a figure on a poster. Not legal advice, and say so . **Written in all seven locales on 7 October 2026, and three sourcing corrections from that run belong here.** The decálogo's footnote 4 cites "Real Decreto Legislativo 1/2014", which does not exist: the brand prohibition is **art. 80.7 del RDL 1/2015**, verified verbatim at the BOE that run. **RD 1591/2009 was derogated in part by RD 192/2023**, but its derogation note keeps **arts. 38 to 40 (publicidad) in force** until specific legislation develops, so the celebrity rule stands and a run should say why. And the Spanish "no especialista" finding is stronger with two sources than one: the DA 2ª of Ley 44/2003 reserves specialist denominations, and **anexo I of RD 183/2008** shows no specialty is reached from the título de Odontología, with Estomatología extinguished as a medical specialty. Portugal's art. 3.º principles are **"transparência, fidedignidade e licitude", "objetividade" and "rigor científico"**, three and not five, read from the INFARMED compiled text because `diariodarepublica.pt` serves a JavaScript shell to a fetch; `ers.pt` and `legislation`-style PDFs needed `pdftotext` rather than a fetch prompt. The Polish layer nobody writes about is the **ustawa o wyrobach medycznych**: art. 58 ust. 1 pkt 1 applies the device-advertising chapter to advertising a service performed with a device, which catches implants and aligners. `isap.sejm.gov.pl` answers a CAPTCHA, and the **Sejm ELI API** (`api.sejm.gov.pl/eli/acts/DU/<year>/<pos>/text/U/...`) serves consolidated texts cleanly. `gesetze-im-internet.de` and `boe.es` both refuse `curl` without a browser user agent; BOE pages are too long for one fetch and need offsets |
| Registro de jornada del equipo: qué hay que guardar, cuánto tiempo y quién lo puede pedir | `registro-jornada-clinica-dental` / `staff-working-time-records-dental-practice` / `enregistrement-temps-travail-cabinet-dentaire` / `registo-de-horario-de-trabalho-clinica-dentaria` / `arbeitszeiterfassung-zahnarztpraxis` / `registrazione-orario-di-lavoro-studio-dentistico` | registro de jornada clínica dental · Added 7 October 2026, intent verified this run in Spanish and German, clinic-side in both. Polish slug `ewidencja-czasu-pracy-w-gabinecie-stomatologicznym`. Distinct from `turnos-equipo-clinica`, which is **who covers which chair**, and from `agenda-del-higienista-dental`, which is clinical capacity. This row is the **employment-law record underneath both**, and it is the one an inspector asks for. It belongs in Queue B rather than being waved off as generic HR for a reason worth stating in the post: a dental practice is the awkward case, with part-time hygienists, a receptionist on a split shift, associates who may not be employees at all and trainees who are, and the single most common mistake is assuming the practice software already does this. **It does not, and the post should say so plainly** rather than implying the PMS is the answer. **Sourced nationally and never translated**, and the two markets checked this run reach opposite machinery from the same European starting point, which is what makes this row worth seven posts. **Spain is verified verbatim this run from the BOE** (consolidated Estatuto de los Trabajadores, `boe.es/buscar/act.php?id=BOE-A-2015-11430`, **art. 34.9** as introduced by RDL 8/2019): "La empresa garantizará el registro diario de jornada, que deberá incluir el horario concreto de inicio y finalización de la jornada de trabajo de cada persona trabajadora." The three operative facts for a Spanish reader, all from the same apartado: records are kept **four years**, they stay "a disposición de las personas trabajadoras, de sus representantes legales y de la Inspección de Trabajo y Seguridad Social", and the **organisation and documentation** of the register is set by **convenio colectivo o acuerdo de empresa** or, failing that, by the employer after **consulting the workers' representatives**, which is the step clinics skip. **Germany is the mirror image: the duty exists and the statute does not.** The Bundesarbeitsgericht held on **13 September 2022, 1 ABR 22/21**, that employers must record employees' working time, deriving it from **§ 3 Abs. 2 Nr. 1 ArbSchG** read in the light of the CJEU's judgment of **14 May 2019 in C-55/18, CCOO v Deutsche Bank SAE** (Grand Chamber, ECLI:EU:C:2019:402, verified this run), because the German legislator had not implemented it; worth noting in both posts that **that case came from Spain**, which is why Spain legislated within months and Germany still has not; the decision binds **all employers regardless of whether a works council exists** and leaves the **form** (electronic or written) to the legislator, with the works council having co-determination over the design in the meantime. **That gap is the German post**: there is a duty, there is no prescribed system, and a practice asking "what do I have to buy" has no legal answer yet. **The ArbZG's own record-keeping and retention rule (§ 16) could not be read this run**, because `gesetze-im-internet.de` answered **HTTP 503** to every attempt, the same failure this file records for the videovigilancia row, so the German post must establish it before stating any retention period. The dental trade press carries it properly and clinic-side (`zm-online.de`, `zwp-online.info`, `iww.de`), which is how the intent was verified. **One sourcing caution established this run**: the Bundesarbeitsgericht press page for that decision **returns HTTP 404** at the obvious slug, so the holding above came from legal and dental secondary sources and the German post must cite the **decision itself** (search the BAG's own decision database by the docket number) rather than any of them, and must state whether an ArbZG amendment has since been enacted, because that is the fact most likely to have changed since this row was written. **Establish per market before writing a number**: Italy's libro unico del lavoro and the CCNL for studi professionali, France's obligations under the Code du travail with the standing warning in this file that Légifrance answers 403, Portugal's registo de tempos de trabalho and its five-year retention under the Código do Trabalho, and Poland's **ewidencja czasu pracy** under art. 149 Kodeksu pracy with its own retention rule. Every one of those is a different number and a different keeper of the record. **Two things not to do.** Do not present a time clock as a data-protection free lunch: a biometric fingerprint reader at the staff door is special-category processing, the AEPD and several European authorities have taken a hard line on it, and the post should point at `rgpd-clinica-dental` rather than re-litigate it. And do not advise on whether an associate dentist is an employee: that is the question `liquidacion-doctores-colaboradores` exists for and it is national, contested and not something a blog settles. The product paragraph is the honest one and it is a **no**: the clinical software is not a time-and-attendance system, what it can do is tell you which practitioner was attributed which treatment and when, which is useful for `liquidacion-doctores-colaboradores` and is **not** a jornada register, and saying that plainly is worth more than pretending otherwise. Not legal or employment advice, and say so |
| La historia clínica de un paciente que ha muerto: quién puede pedirla y qué se entrega | `historia-clinica-paciente-fallecido` / `deceased-patient-dental-records` / `dossier-dentaire-patient-decede` / `processo-clinico-de-paciente-falecido` / `patientenakte-verstorbener-patient` / `cartella-clinica-paziente-deceduto` | historia clínica paciente fallecido · Added 8 October 2026, intent verified this run in Spanish and German, clinic-side in both. Polish slug `dokumentacja-medyczna-zmarlego-pacjenta`. **Distinct from the three records rows nearest it, and the post should say which it is not**: `paciente-pide-su-historia-clinica` is the living patient exercising their own right, `tercero-pide-la-historia-clinica-dental` is a court, an insurer or an employer, and `conservar-historia-clinica-dental-plazos` is how long the file has to exist at all. This row is the case where **the person with the right is dead and the people asking are not the patient**, which is the one request a receptionist has no script for. **Sourced nationally and never translated**, and the two markets checked this run are not a difference of detail: Spain names a category of requester and Germany names an *interest* that has to be asserted. **Spain is verified verbatim this run from the BOE** and the post has to carry both statutes, because they do not say the same thing. **Art. 18.4 de la Ley 41/2002** (`boe.es/buscar/act.php?id=BOE-A-2002-22188`): centres and individually practising facultativos "sólo facilitarán el acceso a la historia clínica de los pacientes fallecidos a las personas vinculadas a él" por razones familiares o de hecho, "salvo que el fallecido lo hubiese prohibido expresamente y así se acredite"; where a third party asks because of a risk to their own health the access is limited to the pertinent data; and "No se facilitará información que afecte a la intimidad del fallecido ni a las anotaciones subjetivas de los profesionales" nor anything prejudicing third parties. **Art. 3 de la LOPDGDD (Ley Orgánica 3/2018)** (`boe.es/buscar/act.php?id=BOE-A-2018-16673`) covers the same death from the data-protection side, and it contains the carve-out that makes this row worth writing: the people vinculadas por razones familiares o de hecho **and the herederos** may ask, the deceased's express prohibition blocks them, **but that prohibition "no impide que los herederos accedan a los datos de carácter patrimonial del causante"**. So in Spain a prohibition that closes the clinical record does **not** close the ledger, and a clinic that applies one rule to both gets one of them wrong. Art. 3 also recognises the persons or institutions the deceased expressly designated, and names the legal representatives or the Ministerio Fiscal for a deceased minor. **Germany is the mirror shape and is verified this run from `dejure.org/gesetze/BGB/630g.html`**: under **§ 630g Abs. 3 BGB** the inspection rights pass to the **Erben** only to pursue their **vermögensrechtliche Interessen**, and to the **nächste Angehörige** only where they assert **immaterielle Interessen**, and in both cases only "soweit" the patient's express or presumed will does not oppose it. **That is a test about why the person is asking, not about who they are**, which is the opposite of the Spanish category test, and the German post's whole job is that the practice has to ask a question Spanish law does not let it ask. Two further German facts that travel and need their own sourcing before being stated: the Schweigepflicht under **§ 203 StGB** survives the patient (the standard phrase in the chamber material is "über den Tod hinaus"), and **originals are not handed over** at all, only inspection at the place the record is kept plus copies against cost reimbursement. The German clinic-side material is the dental chambers themselves, which is how the intent was verified and which is the right register for the post: `lzk-bw.de` *Einsicht in Behandlungsunterlagen*, `zkn.de` *Einsichtnahme in Behandlungsunterlagen*, `lzkth.de` *Patientendokumentation, Schweigepflicht und Datenschutz*, plus `aerzteblatt.de` and `iww.de` on the heirs' claim. **Cite them as chamber guidance, never as law**, the caution this file already records for LZK BW. **Establish per market before writing a single procedural step**, because this is a row where the machinery differs even where the answer does not: Italy's **art. 2-terdecies del Codice privacy** (and whether the deceased restricted it in writing), France under the standing warning in this file that Légifrance answers 403 to a plain fetch and to a browser user agent alike, so it has to come through the Ordre's own documents, Portugal's Lei 12/2005 and the access regime for "informação de saúde" of a deceased person, and Poland's **art. 26 ust. 2 ustawy o prawach pacjenta**, whose osoba bliska mechanism was amended more than once and must be read current (the `api.sejm.gov.pl/eli/acts/DU/<year>/<pos>/text.html` route this file records serves the text as published, not consolidated, so an amended article needs the consolidated endpoint). **Three things not to do.** Do not tell a clinic to ask for an Erbschein, a certificado de últimas voluntades or any other specific document as though the statute named it: none of the texts above prescribes the proof, practice varies, and the post should say what has to be established rather than which paper establishes it. Do not hand over the anotaciones subjetivas: Spain excludes them twice over, in art. 18.3 as against the living patient and again in art. 18.4 as against the family, and that is the detail most likely to be got wrong by someone photocopying a file under pressure. And do not treat "the family asked" as a reason to skip the identity work, because an inheritance dispute is the single most common reason this request arrives. **The product paragraph is small and it is about the record, not the request**: what a system can actually do here is hold the deceased's express prohibition as something visible on the file rather than remembered by whoever took the call, keep the anotaciones subjetivas separable from the rest so a copy can be produced without them, and log who asked, what was released and on what basis, which is the same access log `auditoria-accesos-historia-clinica` already covers. Not legal advice, and say so |
| La declaración de conformidad de la prótesis: qué tiene que darte el laboratorio y qué guardas tú | `declaracion-conformidad-protesis-dental` / `custom-made-dental-device-declaration` / `declaration-conformite-prothese-dentaire` / `declaracao-conformidade-protese-dentaria` / `konformitaetserklaerung-zahnersatz` / `dichiarazione-conformita-protesi-dentaria` | declaración de conformidad prótesis dental · Added 8 October 2026, intent verified this run in Spanish and German, clinic-side in both. Polish slug `deklaracja-zgodnosci-wyrobu-na-zamowienie-w-gabinecie-stomatologicznym`. **Distinct from the two rows it sits between, and the post opens by drawing the line**: `enviar-casos-laboratorio-dental` is the case going *out* and the information it carries, `registro-implantes-trazabilidad` is the lot number of something implanted. This row is **the piece of paper that comes back with the work**, the one document in the whole prosthetic chain that an inspector asks for by name and that most clinics believe is the laboratory's problem alone. **The legal frame is European and the duties are national, which is the unusual shape of this row**: every locale is working from the same **Anexo XIII / Annex XIII of Regulation (EU) 2017/745 (MDR)** on productos a medida, and then each market adds its own keeper, its own retention period and its own recipient. **EUR-Lex could not be read this run**: `eur-lex.europa.eu` answered the same HTTP 202 with an empty body that the 7 October entry records, on the HTML view as well, so **Annex XIII itself is unread here and must be quoted from EUR-Lex by the run that writes this**, not from any of the sources below. That matters more than usual, because the two best secondary descriptions of it disagree with each other. **Spain: the obvious source is stale and must not be used.** The AEMPS page on prótesis dentales (`aemps.gob.es/productos-sanitarios/productos-sanitarios/protesisdentales-mayo10/`) is the top result and is dated **19 May 2010**: it describes the declaración de conformidad under **anexo VIII del RD 1591/2009** and predates the MDR entirely. The current Spanish regulation is **Real Decreto 192/2023, de 21 de marzo** (`boe.es/buscar/act.php?id=BOE-A-2023-7416`), verified this run, and it is a different document: **art. 10** governs fabricación de productos a medida and requires a **licencia previa de funcionamiento** from the comunidad autónoma, **art. 10.3 and art. 21** require the fabricante established in Spain to register in the **Registro de Responsables de la puesta en el mercado de productos a medida** through the AEMPS sede electrónica, **art. 18.1** excludes productos a medida from the ordinary commercialisation registry, **art. 24.5.a)** makes them prescription-only so mail-order and online sale are prohibited, and the retention periods appear in **art. 8.d) and art. 23.4**: the documentation file is kept **at least ten years**, **fifteen for implantables**, counted from the last unit placed on the market. **Read RD 192/2023 before writing and check what it leaves of RD 1591/2009**, because the publicidad row above already records that its derogation is partial, so "derogated" is not a safe word about any particular article. **Germany publishes the sharpest fact in the row and the trade press states it wrongly.** **§ 87 Abs. 1a SGB V**, verified this run at `dejure.org/gesetze/SGB_V/87.html`, does **not** say the declaration is handed to the patient: what it requires is that the **Rechnungslegung** be accompanied by a copy of the Laborrechnung and by an "Erklärung nach Anhang XIII Abschnitt 1 der Verordnung (EU) 745/2017". So in the German statutory system the document travels **with the invoice**, and the Absatz's other content is the kostenfreier **Heil- und Kostenplan** before treatment with Befund, Regelversorgung and planned care, checked by the Krankenkasse, including **Angaben zum Herstellungsort**. The widely repeated "§ 87 Abs. 1a requires you to hand it to the patient" is not what the text says, and **a post that repeats it is wrong about the one provision it cites**. The **fifteen-year retention** quoted everywhere in the German material traces to a Landeszahnärztekammer document (`lzkth.de` EU-MDR *Sonderanfertigungen*, plus `bdizedi.org` *Medizinprodukte in der Zahnarztpraxis* and `iww.de`), not to a statute: state it as chamber guidance or source it from the MDR itself. Two more things those chamber documents publish that are worth the post if they survive checking: **Sonderanfertigungen are fixed and removable Zahnersatz and Schienen, including CAD/CAM work, while Wiederherstellungsmaßnahmen are not**, because a repair is not placed on the market for the first time; and where a Praxislabor and a Gewerbelabor each do part of the work, the declaration passes to whichever finishes it. **The clinic-as-manufacturer case is the half that makes this useful to someone who never installs anything**: a practice with a CAD/CAM mill or its own Praxislabor **is the fabricante** for that work, with the licence, registration and declaration duties that follow, and that is the sentence a chairside-milling practice has never been told. Establish it per market rather than assuming it transfers. **Establish per market before writing**: Italy's prescrizione firmata and the Ministero della Salute's regime for dispositivi su misura, France's rule that the declaration is handed to the patient at the end of the prosthetic act and kept in the dossier (it appeared this run only in an Assemblée nationale question PDF, which is not a regulator, so source it from the ANSM or the Ordre), Portugal's INFARMED, and Poland's **ustawa o wyrobach medycznych**, which the publicidad row above already establishes is reachable through the Sejm ELI API. **Three things not to do.** Do not call it a CE mark: custom-made devices carry no CE marking and saying they do is the error that makes the rest untrustworthy. Do not state a retention period without naming whose rule it is, since ten, fifteen and five all appear in the sources above and they are answering different questions. And do not write it as a laboratory post: the reader is the clinic, and the clinic's questions are what it must demand, where it must file it, how long it keeps it and what changes if it mills in-house. **The product paragraph is well earned and should stay one short passage**: the declaration is a dated document that belongs to one patient and one piece of work, which makes it an attachment on the treatment line with the lab, the date and the material beside it, retrievable at an inspection without opening a filing cabinet, and that is the same mechanism `registro-implantes-trazabilidad` describes for a lot number. Not legal or regulatory advice, and say so |
| El paciente que no paga: del aviso al juzgado, y lo que no puedes hacer con sus datos | `reclamar-impago-tratamiento-dental` / `unpaid-dental-treatment-debt-recovery` / `recouvrement-impayes-cabinet-dentaire` / `cobrar-tratamento-dentario-nao-pago` / `offene-honorare-zahnarztpraxis` / `recupero-crediti-studio-dentistico` | reclamar impago tratamiento dental · Added 8 October 2026, intent verified this run in Spanish and German, clinic-side in both. Polish slug `windykacja-naleznosci-w-gabinecie-stomatologicznym`. **Distinct from the two money rows already published, and the post must link them rather than repeat them**: `control-deuda-pacientes` is the ledger, knowing who owes what and chasing it inside the clinic, and `financiacion-tratamientos-dentales` is a third party lending the patient the money up front. This row starts where those stop: **the patient is not going to pay, and the next step leaves the building**. That is a different post with a different reader, the one deciding whether to write it off, send it to an agency or go to court. **Sourced nationally and never translated**, and the two markets checked this run differ in the most useful way available: the same decision runs through a **court procedure** in one and through a **data-protection gate** in the other. **Spain is verified verbatim this run from the consolidated LEC** and the mechanism is the **proceso monitorio**. **Art. 812.1 LEC**: it is available to whoever claims payment of a "deuda dineraria de cualquier importe, líquida, determinada, vencida y exigible", so **there is no ceiling**, which is the first thing readers get wrong, and the debt may be evidenced either by documents "firmados por el deudor" (whatever their form or medium, physical or electronic) or by "facturas, albaranes de entrega, certificaciones" and other documents "aun unilateralmente creados por el acreedor" that habitually document debts in that kind of relationship. **Art. 813** puts competence exclusively in the Juzgado de Primera Instancia of the debtor's domicile. **Art. 815.1** gives the debtor **twenty days** to pay or to file a reasoned escrito de oposición, and **art. 816.1** is why the procedure is worth the trouble: if the debtor neither pays nor appears, the decreto ends the monitorio and the creditor may instar el despacho de ejecución "bastando para ello con la mera solicitud". **Art. 818.1**: an opposition sends the whole thing to the ordinary juicio. **The sharpest Spanish finding, and the one that turns this into a records post, is art. 815.3**: if the documentation shows "que la cantidad reclamada no es correcta" the court may propose a requerimiento for a lower amount; and because a dental treatment is a contract "entre un empresario o profesional y un consumidor o usuario", the judge screens the clauses founding the claim for **abusividad before the requerimiento is even issued**. **So the clinic's own paperwork is examined first and the claim is capped by what it proves**, which is exactly how the reported dental monitorios have failed: claiming the budgeted treatment instead of the executed one. Say that plainly, since it is advice that costs the reader nothing and saves the case. **Germany runs through § 286 BGB and then into a problem Spain does not have.** Verified this run at `dejure.org/gesetze/BGB/286.html`: **§ 286 Abs. 1** puts the debtor in Verzug on a Mahnung after Fälligkeit (a Klage or a **Mahnbescheid** counting as equivalent), and **§ 286 Abs. 3** adds Verzug at the latest where the debtor does not pay "innerhalb von 30 Tagen nach Fälligkeit und Zugang einer Rechnung oder gleichwertigen Zahlungsaufstellung", **but against a Verbraucher only if "auf diese Folgen in der Rechnung oder Zahlungsaufstellung besonders hingewiesen worden ist"**. **That makes a line on the invoice a precondition for the thirty-day rule**, which is a software fact and not a legal nicety: a practice whose template omits it has no automatic Verzug. **The second half of the German post is the one nobody writes and it is the reason this row is not generic business advice**: handing an unpaid patient file to an Inkassobüro or a privatzahnärztliche Verrechnungsstelle is a disclosure of treatment data under **§ 203 StGB** and the DSGVO, not an ordinary outsourcing decision. The trade press that carries the dunning advice properly and clinic-side (`zm-online.de`, `zwp-online.info`, `iww.de`, `aerzteblatt.de`) was explicit about the Mahnwesen and **silent on the Schweigepflicht**, which is precisely the gap: **establish the consent or the § 203 Abs. 3 route from a chamber or an authority before writing a word of it**, and do not infer it. The same material also records a Landgericht Berlin decision allowing Inkassokosten as recoverable damage after three unanswered Mahnungen: **cite the decision itself by docket number or leave it out**. **One trap that applies in every locale and belongs in every copy**: putting a patient into a credit-reference file is its own regulated act, not a step in dunning. In Spain that is the sistema de información crediticia regime in **art. 20 LOPDGDD** with its own conditions, and the post should point at `rgpd-clinica-dental` rather than re-litigate it. **Establish per market before writing any step or deadline**: Italy's decreto ingiuntivo and the prescrizione period, France's injonction de payer with the standing Légifrance 403 caution in this file, Portugal's injunção and the Balcão Nacional de Injunções, and Poland's postępowanie upominawcze with the e-sąd, each of which has its own threshold, its own fee and its own paperwork. **Three things not to do.** Do not write anything that reads as advice to withhold care or records over money: the right to the historia clínica does not depend on a paid invoice, and `paciente-pide-su-historia-clinica` already covers it. Do not publish a dunning letter template, because the one jurisdiction-specific sentence in it is the part that matters and a template gets copied across borders. And do not suggest sending anything by WhatsApp: `enviar-datos-de-pacientes-por-email-o-whatsapp` is the row for that and the answer there is not yes. **The product paragraph is honest and it is about proof, not pressure**: what a system contributes is the chain the monitorio actually examines, the signed presupuesto with its versions, which lines were executed and when, the invoices and part payments against them, and the dated record of each reminder sent, so the amount claimed is the amount documented. It does not collect anything, and saying so is worth more than implying it does. Not legal advice, and say so |
| Residuos sanitarios en la clínica dental: contrato, almacenamiento y los papeles que hay que guardar | `residuos-sanitarios-clinica-dental` / `clinical-waste-dental-practice` / `dasri-cabinet-dentaire` / `residuos-hospitalares-clinica-dentaria` / `praxisabfall-entsorgung-zahnarztpraxis` / `rifiuti-sanitari-studio-dentistico` | residuos biosanitarios clínica dental · Added 8 October 2026, intent verified this run in German and French (and checked in Spanish), clinic-side in both. Polish slug `odpady-medyczne-w-gabinecie-stomatologicznym`. **Distinct from `amalgama-dental-prohibicion-residuos`, which is the mercury stream only, and from `inspeccion-sanitaria-clinica-dental`, which names the waste containers and the last collection note in one checklist bullet**: this row is the whole non-amalgam stream (sharps, infectious, chemical), the contract with an authorised collector, on-site storage limits and the paper trail, which is the part a clinic loses. German trade press (zwp-online) is clear that responsibility cannot be delegated and the disposal receipts form the Nachweis register; French guidance (MACSF guide updated 2025, ARS conventions) gives the convention plus bon de prise en charge under 5 kg/month or CERFA 11352 bordereau above it, all kept **three years**. Sources the writer must use: DE KrWG, NachwV and the LAGA Mitteilung 18 Vollzugshilfe; FR Code de la santé publique arts. R1335-1 ff. and the two arrêtés of 7 September 1999 (Légifrance); ES Ley 7/2022 de residuos (BOE) **plus the comunidad autónoma's own sanitary-waste decree, because the storage limits quoted online (72 hours, one week refrigerated) are autonomic, not Spanish** — say which comunidad a figure comes from or do not give it |
| Sospecha de malos tratos en la consulta: qué obliga a comunicar, a quién y qué se anota | `sospecha-malos-tratos-clinica-dental` / `suspected-abuse-dental-practice-reporting` / `signalement-maltraitance-cabinet-dentaire` / `suspeita-de-maus-tratos-clinica-dentaria` / `kindeswohlgefaehrdung-zahnarztpraxis` / `sospetto-maltrattamento-studio-dentistico` | dentista sospecha maltrato comunicar · Added 8 October 2026, intent verified this run in Spanish, German and French, clinic-side in all three. Polish slug `podejrzenie-przemocy-wobec-pacjenta-w-gabinecie-stomatologicznym`. **Distinct from `pacientes-menores-tutores` (who consents for a child) and `tercero-pide-la-historia-clinica-dental` (someone asks the clinic for the file)**: here the clinic is the one that has to speak first, against its own duty of secrecy, and the record of what was seen is the evidence. Spain: the Consejo General de Dentistas approved a guía de actuación ante sospecha de malos tratos (Redacción Médica, 10 April 2023); the writer must source the duty from **art. 16 LO 8/2021 (LOPIVI)** and **art. 262 LECrim** on the BOE. Germany: **§ 4 KKG** (consultation with an insoweit erfahrene Fachkraft using pseudonymised data, then Jugendamt), served properly by zm-online and zwp-online (January 2026). France: **art. 226-14 Code pénal** releases the dentist from secrecy and a minor's agreement is not needed; ONCD guidance. The three markets differ on *duty* versus *permission* to report, which is the post. Never give legal advice and say so |

Germany: **§ 71 MPDG and the MPAMIV** — the zm-online and Kammer pages that rank still cite the **MPSV, repealed in 2021**, which is a trap to name. France: matériovigilance to the ANSM, CSP arts. Polish slug `nota-prawna-strony-gabinetu-stomatologicznego`. **Distinct from `publicidad-clinica-dental-normativa`, which is what the clinic may claim; this is who it must say it is**: the identification block a regulated profession owes on its own website (chamber, title and where it was granted, registration number, professional rules, supervisory authority). Germany is the strongest market for the query (LZK Baden-Württemberg "Pflichtangaben Internet", BLZK, zm-online); cite **§ 5 DDG**, which replaced § 5 TMG in May 2024, so any source still quoting the TMG is out of date. France: LCEN art. 6 plus the ONCD's charter for practitioners' websites and the identification rules it cites from the CSP. Spain: **art. 10 LSSI** (colegio profesional, número de colegiado, título) from the BOE. Keep cookies and marketing out of it |

R5212-25 ff.**, inventory plus a per-device register kept five years after the device leaves service (ONCD documents, ASN inspection letters). **Spain has no single equivalent statute found this run**; the writer must state what the comunidad's autorización checklist asks for and not import the German or French duty |
| Formación continuada obligatoria del dentista: puntos, plazos y quién lo comprueba | `formacion-continuada-obligatoria-dentista` / `dentist-cpd-requirements` / `certification-periodique-chirurgien-dentiste` / `formacao-continua-obrigatoria-medico-dentista` / `fortbildungspflicht-zahnarzt` / `ecm-obbligatori-odontoiatra` | formación continuada obligatoria dentista · Added 8 October 2026, intent verified this run in German and French, clinic-side in both. Polish slug `obowiazek-doskonalenia-zawodowego-lekarza-dentysty`. **Country-shaped, and the post opens by saying so**: Germany ties it to payment (**§ 95d SGB V**, 125 points in five years, fee cut of 10 % then 25 %, KZBV Regelung and KZV Land Brandenburg FAQ, zwp-online March 2026), France to licensing (**ordonnance n° 2021-961 of 19 July 2021**, certification périodique for chirurgiens-dentistes from 1 January 2023, DPC counted towards it, MACSF), and Spain has no general mandatory scheme the writer may assert without a BOE source. **Distinct from `formacion-equipo-software-dental`**, which is training staff on a new program; this is the practitioner's own licensing obligation and, for a clinic owner, the associates' too. The software surface is a per-practitioner log with a window and an expiry date, nothing more, and the post must not inflate it |
| Cuando el dentista falta: sustitutos, quién factura y qué tiene que quedar en la historia | `dentista-sustituto-clinica-dental` / `locum-dentist-cover` / `remplacement-chirurgien-dentiste` / `medico-dentista-substituto` / `praxisvertretung-zahnarzt` / `sostituto-odontoiatra-studio` | dentista sustituto clínica · Added 8 October 2026, intent verified this run in German and French, clinic-side in both (Spanish returned job ads only, so Spanish is not counted). Polish slug `zastepstwo-lekarza-dentysty`. **Distinct from `turnos-equipo-clinica`**, which covers the rota and names a stand-in for each post, and from `liquidacion-doctores-colaboradores`, which is the associate's monthly pay: this row is **the treating dentist's absence**, where the replacement's own legal standing, the billing identity and the record entries are the questions. Germany verified this run on `buzer.de`: **§ 32 Abs. 1 Zahnärzte-ZV** allows Vertretung for illness, holiday or Fortbildung "innerhalb von zwölf Monaten bis zur Dauer von drei Monaten", **notification to the KZV beyond one week**, and up to **twelve months** around a Vertragszahnärztin's Entbindung; KZV forms (`kzvlb.de`) and `iww.de` confirm no retroactive approval and that unapproved Vertretung cannot be billed. France: the Ordre publishes the **contrat de remplacement** model and requires a written contract and informing the conseil départemental (`ordre-chirurgiens-dentistes.fr`); the replaced practitioner must stop all activity during the remplacement. Establish Spain (no equivalent figure; it is an employment or mercantile contract), Italy, Portugal and Poland per market. The software angle is real and narrow: every entry and every invoice line has to carry **who actually treated**, which is the same attribution `permisos-acceso-personal-clinica` and `liquidacion-doctores-colaboradores` depend on. Not legal advice |
| La evaluación de riesgos laborales de la clínica: qué documento tiene que existir y cada cuánto se revisa | `evaluacion-riesgos-laborales-clinica-dental` / `dental-practice-risk-assessment` / `document-unique-cabinet-dentaire` / `avaliacao-de-riscos-clinica-dentaria` / `gefaehrdungsbeurteilung-zahnarztpraxis` / `valutazione-rischi-studio-dentistico` | evaluación de riesgos clínica dental · Added 8 October 2026, intent verified this run in German and French, clinic-side in both (Spanish returned academic theses and generic SPA material, so Spanish is not counted). Polish slug `ocena-ryzyka-zawodowego-gabinet-stomatologiczny`. **No existing row or post carries it** (grep for evaluación de riesgos, 31/1995 and Gefährdungsbeurteilung returned nothing); it is the occupational-safety document every employing practice must hold, distinct from `registro-jornada-clinica-dental` (working time) and from the radiological rows (which protect the patient and the exposed worker under a separate regime). **The EU frame is common** (Directive 89/391/EEC; EUR-Lex is gated, see the 7 October note) **and each market has its own document**: Spain, **art. 16.2 Ley 31/1995** (evaluación inicial de riesgos and planificación preventiva) and **art. 23** (documentation the employer keeps), read this run from the BOE; Germany, **§ 5 ArbSchG** (Beurteilung der Arbeitsbedingungen) and **§ 6** (documentation), read this run on `dejure.org`, with the BuS-Dienst Kammermodell that lets a trained Zahnarzt do it himself (`zkn.de`, `lzk-bw.de`); France, the **DUERP** mandatory from the first salarié, with the ADF/ONCD grille for cabinets dentaires (Légifrance 403: source through the Ordre). Do not write the risk list for the reader; say what the document must contain and who reviews it. Product paragraph: none worth a section, and the post should say so; link `trazabilidad-esterilizacion-dental` for the one overlap. Not legal advice |
| Una empleada embarazada en la clínica: qué tareas deja, quién lo decide y qué se documenta | `empleada-embarazada-clinica-dental` / `pregnant-employee-dental-practice` / `assistante-dentaire-enceinte` / `trabalhadora-gravida-clinica-dentaria` / `schwangere-mitarbeiterin-zahnarztpraxis` / `dipendente-in-gravidanza-studio-dentistico` | auxiliar dental embarazada · Added 8 October 2026, intent verified this run in German and French, clinic-side in both. Polish slug `pracownica-w-ciazy-gabinet-stomatologiczny`. **Distinct from the risk-assessment row above**, which is the standing document: this is the **event**, the day an assistant or hygienist says she is pregnant, and a dental practice is the hard case (sharps, X-ray, aerosols, amalgam, mercury, standing work). The existing `embaraz` mentions in published posts are all about the pregnant **patient**. Germany verified this run on `dejure.org`: **§ 10 MuSchG** requires the employer to assess every activity "nach Art, Ausmaß und Dauer" for a pregnant or nursing woman, to set the protective measures "unverzüglich" once told, and allows only those activities "für die er die erforderlichen Schutzmaßnahmen getroffen hat"; the chambers publish dental-specific guides (`lzk-bw.de` *Leitfaden schwangere Mitarbeiterin*, `zkn.de`, `zaek-sa.de` checklist, Zahnärztekammer Nordrhein), including that a betriebliches Beschäftigungsverbot is reimbursed to the employer (U2) — **cite the statute, not the chamber, for that**. Spain verified this run from the BOE: **art. 26 Ley 31/1995** (adaptación, puesto compatible, then suspensión por riesgo durante el embarazo). France: ASN inspection letters and dental-employer material on removing the assistant from ionising radiation; source the Code du travail through the Ordre. Not legal advice, and never medical advice |
| Un pinchazo con una aguja usada: qué se hace en la primera hora y qué tiene que quedar registrado | `pinchazo-accidental-clinica-dental` / `needlestick-injury-dental-practice` / `accident-exposition-sang-cabinet-dentaire` / `acidente-picada-agulha-clinica-dentaria` / `nadelstichverletzung-zahnarztpraxis` / `puntura-accidentale-studio-dentistico` | pinchazo accidental clínica dental · Added 8 October 2026, intent verified this run in Spanish and German, clinic-side in both (Spanish: the UCM Facultad de Odontología clinic protocol and university dental-clinic procedures; German: `phb.lzk-bw.de` *VA Nadelstichverletzung*, `zm-online.de`, Forum Verlag). Polish slug `zaklucie-igla-gabinet-stomatologiczny`. **Nothing in the published posts carries it** (grep for pinchazo, punzante, accidente). It is the staff-safety incident, not a clinical post: the reader is the employer who has to have the procedure posted before it happens. **The frame is genuinely common across all seven markets**: **Directive 2010/32/EU** (sharps framework agreement; EUR-Lex gated this run). Spain verified this run from the BOE: **Orden ESS/1451/2013** (`BOE-A-2013-8381`) applies to "todos los centros, establecimientos y servicios, del ámbito sanitario y hospitalario, tanto del sector público como del sector privado"; **art. 9** obliges the worker to "notificar inmediatamente cualquier accidente o incidente", **art. 10** requires immediate care including profilaxis postexposición, investigation of causes and registration, under RD 664/1997. Germany: D-Arzt and the BGW accident report, from the chamber QM handbook. **Do not give a prophylaxis protocol**; say who decides and how fast. Product paragraph: the source patient's identity and the time of the procedure are what the occupational doctor will ask for, and the appointment record is where they are |
