| Cuando un dentista deja la clínica: sus pacientes, su agenda y el pacto de no competencia | `dentista-se-va-de-la-clinica-pacientes` / `associate-dentist-leaves-practice` / `depart-collaborateur-cabinet-dentaire` / `dentista-sai-da-clinica` / `wettbewerbsverbot-angestellter-zahnarzt` / `dentista-lascia-lo-studio` | dentista se va de la clínica pacientes · Added 8 October 2026, intent verified this run in Spanish and German, clinic-side in both (Spanish: TSJ social-chamber judgments on dental clinics via `iberley.es`; German: `iww.de` *Konkurrenzschutz: Wettbewerbsverbot in Angestelltenverträgen*, `zm-online.de`, `zwp-online.info`). Polish slug `odejscie-lekarza-dentysty-z-gabinetu`. **Distinct from `liquidacion-doctores-colaboradores`** (pay while they work) and from `traspaso-clinica-dental-historias` (the whole practice changing hands): this is one practitioner leaving, and the three questions the owner has the next morning: can they take patients, who tells the patients with appointments, and is the non-compete worth anything. Spain verified this run from the consolidated ET PDF: **art. 21.2 ET**, a post-contractual pacto de no competencia "no podrá tener una duración superior a dos años para los técnicos y de seis meses para los demás trabajadores" and is valid only with "un efectivo interés industrial o comercial" and "una compensación económica adecuada"; **it applies only to employees**, and many associates are autónomos, which is the first thing to establish. Germany verified this run on `dejure.org`: **§ 74 HGB**, written form and a Karenzentschädigung of at least half the last contractual remuneration, with § 74a limiting it to two years (state that from the text). **The patient is free to follow**, and the records stay with the clinic as responsable: link `traspaso-clinica-dental-historias` and `paciente-pide-su-historia-clinica`, never suggest withholding a copy. Not legal advice |
| Sueldos del equipo de la clínica: convenio, pluses y variable sin que la nómina se vuelva un lío | `salarios-equipo-clinica-dental` / `dental-team-pay-and-bonuses` / `salaires-convention-collective-cabinet-dentaire` / `salarios-equipa-clinica-dentaria` / `gehalt-zfa-praemiensystem` / `retribuzione-personale-studio-dentistico` | salario auxiliar clínica dental convenio · Added 8 October 2026, intent verified this run in German and French, clinic-side (employer-side) in both; Spanish returned translated US material and is not counted. Polish slug `wynagrodzenia-personelu-gabinet-stomatologiczny`. **Distinct from `liquidacion-doctores-colaboradores`**, which is the associate dentist's percentage: this row is **the salaried team**, assistants, hygienists, reception, and the variable on top. France is the cleanest anchor: the **Convention collective nationale des cabinets dentaires, IDCC 1619**, with its prime d'ancienneté and **prime de secrétariat**, and minimum salaries reset by an **accord of 12 February 2026** (seen this run on `legisocial.fr` and `payfit.com`, both secondary: **read the accord and the IDCC text on Légifrance or the Journal officiel through a route that is not 403**). Germany: the AAZ/vmf Gehaltstarifvertrag for ZFA where the practice is bound by it, and the bonus/Provision models documented employer-side in `zm-online.de` *Wie man seine ZFA belohnen kann*, `iww.de` and `zwp-online.info`. Spain: convenios are provincial or sectoral, so **establish which convenio applies before writing any figure**. **Do not publish salary numbers that are not dated and sourced**. Product paragraph: a production- or recall-based bonus needs per-person attribution of treatments, which is the same data `liquidacion-doctores-colaboradores` uses; the software does not run payroll and must say so |

| La web de la clínica y la ley de accesibilidad: cuándo obliga y qué cambia si se reserva online | `accesibilidad-web-clinica-dental` / `website-accessibility-dental-practice` / `accessibilite-site-cabinet-dentaire` / `acessibilidade-site-clinica-dentaria` / `barrierefreie-website-zahnarztpraxis` / `accessibilita-sito-studio-dentistico` | accesibilidad web clínica dental · Added 8 October 2026, intent verified this run in German and French, clinic-side in both (German: zm-online "Mehr als nur Farbe und große Schrift" and "Barrierefreiheit meets Customer Journey", zwp-online "Digitale Barrierefreiheit in der Zahnarztpraxis" and its 2024 warning on Abmahnungen, Forvis Mazars on the BFSG for practices; French: law-firm and DGCCRF-enforcement pieces on the 28 June 2025 obligation applied to cabinets). L. 412-13 (loi n° 2023-171 art. 16, décret n° 2023-931, arts. D. 412-57 and D. 412-60) via francenum.gouv.fr and Arcom, because Légifrance returns 403. **Written: 24 October 2026, all seven languages.** |
| Atender en residencias de mayores: el convenio, la visita y la historia clínica fuera de la clínica | `odontologia-en-residencias-de-mayores` / `dental-care-in-care-homes` / `soins-dentaires-en-ehpad` / `medicina-dentaria-em-lares-de-idosos` / `zahnarzt-pflegeheim-kooperationsvertrag` / `odontoiatria-nelle-rsa` | odontología en residencias de mayores · Added 8 October 2026, intent verified this run in German and French, clinic-side in both (German: zwp-online and zm-online on Kooperationsverträge and aufsuchende Betreuung, KZBV and KZV circulars; French: the ONCD EHPADENT and DOMIDENT documents and ARS pages on organising dental care in EHPAD). | El agua del sillón y la legionela: qué plan tiene que tener la clínica y cada cuánto se controla | `legionela-clinica-dental` / `dental-unit-waterlines-legionella` / `legionelle-unit-dentaire` / `legionella-clinica-dentaria` / `wasserhygiene-zahnarztpraxis` / `legionella-studio-odontoiatrico` | legionela clínica dental · Added 25 October 2026, intent verified this run in Spanish and German and clinic-side in both (Spanish: the COEM publishes a model Plan Sanitario frente a Legionella for dental clinics; German: zm-online and zwp-online carry practice water hygiene as a recurring compliance item). **Seven locales, and each copy cites its own national source: never translate this one.** The Spanish half is settled and was read verbatim from the BOE this run, so the writing run does not have to re-derive it: **RD 487/2022 (BOE-A-2022-10297), anexo I punto 14, lists «sistemas de agua a presión en tratamientos dentales»** among the «Relación no exhaustiva de instalaciones y equipos», pulled into scope by **art. 3.1**; **art. 7.1** lets the titular choose between a PPCL and a PSL; and **art. 9.4 requires instalaciones prioritarias to base the plan «preferiblemente» on a PSL**, where **art. 2.11 defines instalaciones prioritarias as including «centros sanitarios»** — so a dental clinic is a priority installation and the PSL is the expected plan, which is the finding the Spanish post turns on and which no vendor blog states. **Entry into force is 2 January 2023** (disposición final tercera: «el día dos de enero del año siguiente al de su publicación»), so **the «2 de enero de 2024» a dental distributor publishes is wrong** and is the trap in this row. **The German half is the better half and is where the post earns its place, but it must be verified before writing because the trade press has it backwards twice over.** First, water behind a safety device in a dental unit is **Betriebswasser and not part of the Trinkwasserinstallation**, so the TrinkwV does not govern it, which is the opposite of what most practice articles assert. Second, **the KRINKO 2006 recommendation that every German article cites has not been listed among the RKI's current recommendations since early 2021**, per the Niedersächsisches Landesgesundheitsamt (`nlga.niedersachsen.de/download/170635`), an official state health authority, which adds that it may still serve as a reference while nothing newer exists. **Settle both from the RKI and the NLGA directly, never from zwp-online or a filter manufacturer**, and note that the KBE figures in the trade press contradict each other on the denominator alone (100 KBE/ml in one article, 100 KBE/10 ml in another): quote no limit value that is not on an official page. The AWMF S2k-Leitlinie «Hygienische Anforderungen an das Wasser in zahnärztlichen Behandlungseinheiten» (DGKH/DGZMK) is the other candidate source and is itself flagged as more than five years un-updated. Software surface is real and is what earns the product mention: the flushing log, the sampling dates and the per-chair maintenance record are exactly the documentation an inspection asks to see, and `inventario-mantenimiento-equipos-clinica-dental` is the post to link rather than to repeat. **Written: 25 October 2026, all seven languages, and the row's own prediction held in both directions.** Spain was settled from the BOE as this row said, and the one thing the row did not have is the periodicity: **tabla 3 del anexo V has no row for instalaciones de uso sanitario/terapéutico**, so the dental system falls in the residual rows «otras instalaciones que puedan producir aerosolización», minimum **annual** for Legionella. Also found there: disposición transitoria quinta required every PPCL or PSL to be updated to this real decreto **before 1 July 2025**, a deadline already past, and disposición transitoria sexta gives until 1 January 2030 for the sampler's accreditation under art. 11.5. Germany: `gesetze-im-internet.de` refused § 31 TrinkwV on three attempts (503 to WebFetch twice, connection reset to curl), so the provision came from the body that enforces it, the **Gesundheitsamt der Landeshauptstadt Wiesbaden, Merkblatt Stand 11.02.2026**, which states the § 31 conditions, the Großanlage thresholds (>400 l, or >3 l pipe content, with the metre conversion), the intervals (öffentlich jährlich, gewerblich alle drei Jahre), the 100 KBE/100 ml Maßnahmenwert and the §§ 51 and 52 duties. The NLGA document delivered exactly what this row promised on KRINKO 2006. **The Betriebswasser claim in this row was not written**: the NLGA draws the line between TrinkwV investigations (Eckventile, Waschbecken) and dental unit water checks, which is sourceable, but it nowhere says the water behind a safety device is Betriebswasser, so that sentence was dropped rather than softened. **Four locales turned out to have a stronger finding than the row expected, and all four are the same shape: the duty does not attach to the practice, it attaches to a threshold.** Italy is the best of them and it is the opposite of what the trade press reports: **allegato VIII of d.lgs. 18/2023 names `odontoiatrici` in classe di priorità B**, with an azione a carattere di obbligo, the piano di autocontrollo degli impianti idrici interni with minimum control of piombo, Legionella and L. pneumophila, attuatore the GIDI; art. 9 ties it to allegato I parte D (Legionella < 1 000 UFC/l), art. 6 comma 8 sets the first assessment at **12 gennaio 2029**, and art. 23 lett. b) prices a breach of art. 5 comma 3 at **5.000 to 30.000 euro**. Read from the whole-issue GU PDF per this file's own advice; note the GU text needs a **-29 character shift** decoded before it is quotable (`/HJLRQHOOD` is `Legionella`, digits map M=0 to V=9). Poland is the trap this row did not know about: **the rozporządzenie of 7 December 2017 was repealed on 24 June 2026** and replaced by **Dz.U. 2026 poz. 748** (22 May 2026), with the units changed from jtk/100 ml to **jtk/l**, so any Polish Legionella figure from before this run is both repealed and in the wrong denominator. The duty itself turns on **art. 4j ust. 1 of the ustawa, as amended by Dz.U. 2026 poz. 605: an obiekt priorytetowy is a non-residential facility where more than 50 people a day are exposed to water-related risk**, and the health-facility limb of ust. 2 pkt 1 lit. a covers only `stacjonarne i całodobowe` providers, which an ambulatory gabinet is not; it can only be caught by lit. c, a budynek użyteczności publicznej generating a water aerosol. Penalties 100 to 10 000 zł. France: **no French text sets a Legionella value or a periodicity for a cabinet libéral**, which is the whole French post. The duty is R. 4127-204 and R. 4127-269 CSP, quoted from the **ONCD fiche «Point d'eau dans la salle de soins», V.21 juin 2019 (`/download/52805`)**, because Légifrance still returns 403, for a fourth recorded run. The figures everyone cites come from the **Ministère de la Santé guide technique «L'eau dans les établissements de santé», 2005**, written for hospitals, via the Hygiènes 2025 review by Baudet and Lizon hosted on `paca.ars.sante.fr`, which also says the annual periodicity only «devrait être retenue dans les prochaines recommandations» of the ADF. Portugal: Lei 52/2018 art. 2.º n.º 1 **alínea c) (redes prediais) certainly covers the clinic**, triggering art. 3.º n.º 3's programa de manutenção e limpeza with registo atualizado (coima 1500 to 20 000 euro for a pessoa coletiva), and none of the three exclusions in n.º 3 reaches an establishment of public access. **Whether the dental unit itself falls under alínea b) was left open in the post rather than decided**, because no official source consulted answers it; the post says so and sends the reader to the autoridade de saúde. `data.dre.pt` with `/pdf` served both the Lei and Portaria 25/2021 (29 January 2021, not 12 January), and both are two-column: `pdftotext` **without** `-layout`. English carries two markets and the contrast is the post: **CDC ≤ 500 CFU/mL** against the EPA drinking water standard, against **HTM 01-05's expected TVC of 100 to 200 cfu/ml at 22 °C**, plus HTM 01-05's two-minute flush, end-of-day drain-down from L8, bottles dried and stored inverted, Type A air gap and its refusal to recommend in-house test kits. `cqc.org.uk` returns 403 to WebFetch and was not used; `england.nhs.uk` serves the HTM PDF normally |
| El colaborador que factura: cuándo deja de ser autónomo y pasa a ser una relación laboral | — / — / `collaborateur-liberal-requalification-dentaire` / — / `scheinselbststaendigkeit-zahnarztpraxis` / — | scheinselbstständigkeit zahnarztpraxis · collaborateur libéral requalification · Added 25 October 2026. **DE and FR only, and that is deliberate**: the Spanish check returned press, union and judgment coverage rather than a clinic asking how to structure the relationship, and the Italian check returned practice start-up and VAT guides with nothing on the subordination question, so neither market met the clinic-side test. **The two markets that did meet it answer with completely different tests, which is the whole post and the reason it is two posts and not one translated.** Germany asks whether the person is weisungsgebunden and eingegliedert under **§ 7 Abs. 1 SGB IV**, and the route is the Statusfeststellungsverfahren at the DRV Clearingstelle under **§ 7a SGB IV**, read from `gesetze-im-internet.de` this run (it answered on the second attempt for `/sgb_4/__7a.html`; `/sgb_5/__136a.html` failed once and answered on retry, so retry that host rather than giving up on it). **The mechanic that makes the German post actionable is § 7a Abs. 5**: where the status application is filed **within one month of taking up the activity** and the DRV then finds employment, the day the decision is announced counts as the start of the employment relationship, provided the person agrees and held comparable health and pension cover in the interim, and the Gesamtsozialversicherungsbeitrag only falls due once that decision is unanfechtbar. That is a deadline a practice can still meet, as against the retroactive liability the trade press leads with. **Note § 7a Abs. 7: Abs. 2 Satz 2 and 3, Abs. 4a to 4c and Abs. 6 Satz 2 expire on 30 June 2027**, so check the provision again the run this is written rather than reusing this note. Clinic-side intent is confirmed by zm-online's own 2026 piece «Die Ersparnis von heute ist die Nachzahlung von morgen», which names Praxisvertreter and external billing staff as the typical cases. **France does not use the subordination test at all for this, and getting that wrong would make the French post a mistranslation of the German one.** The ONCD's own *Les contrats de collaboration · Note d'information* (fetched this run from `ordre-chirurgiens-dentistes.fr/download/52724`; it defeats a plain fetch and yields to `pdftotext -layout`, the mechanic this file already records) states that requalification follows where the collaborator is unable **«soit de se constituer une clientèle personnelle de droit»**, meaning the contract contains clauses preventing it, **«soit de se constituer une clientèle personnelle de fait»**, meaning they are materially prevented from doing so. The same note sets out the written-contract requirement on pain of nullity and the rétrocession d'honoraires. **Verify the Ordre filing duty and its one-month deadline (art. L. 4113-9 CSP) from the ONCD rather than Légifrance, which this file records as returning 403 to everything**, and check the outcome of **Cass. soc., 21 September 2022, n° 21-14.171** before citing it: the search surfaced the moyen and not the holding. The software surface is unusually direct and belongs in the French post: whether the practice's agenda and records let a collaborator hold a clientèle personnelle at all is a question the PMS answers, and the ONCD model contract's own clause on a patient who consults the collaborator exclusively for more than N months is a report, not a legal opinion |
| La garantía de una prótesis: qué estás obligado a rehacer gratis y qué no deberías prometer en el presupuesto | `garantia-protesis-dental` / — / `garantie-prothese-dentaire` / — / `gewaehrleistung-zahnersatz` / — | garantía prótesis dental · gewährleistung zahnersatz · garantie prothèse dentaire · Added 25 October 2026. **ES, FR and DE only**: those are the three markets whose rule was established this run, and a locale is added to this row only once its own rule has been verified, because the answer comes from a different legal instrument in each and there is nothing to translate. **The German rule is settled and was read verbatim from `gesetze-im-internet.de` this run** (it answered on the second attempt): **§ 136a Abs. 4 Satz 3 SGB V**, «Der Zahnarzt übernimmt für Füllungen und die Versorgung mit Zahnersatz eine zweijährige Gewähr», with Satz 4 requiring identical and partial repeats of fillings and the renewal or restoration of Zahnersatz including crowns to be done **kostenfrei** in that period, exceptions set by the **KZBV and the GKV-Spitzenverband**, and longer periods available by agreement and — expressly — advertisable to patients. **Two things in that same paragraph are what the German post is for.** The statute says «**§ 195 des Bürgerlichen Gesetzbuchs bleibt unberührt**», so the two-year Gewähr and the three-year Verjährung are different things that sit side by side, which is the origin of the «drei Jahre» confusion a ZWP seminar Q&A had to correct. And the two years is a **GKV rule in SGB V**, not a general consumer guarantee, so a privately paid restoration is not automatically inside it. **France answers with neither a period nor a statute but with the moyens/résultat split, and the split is contested**: the obligation de résultat for the conception and confection of the prosthesis runs from Cass. 15 November 1988 through 23 November 2004 (published) and 9 December 2010, but the MACSF flags **an arrêt of 20 March 2013** asserting an obligation de moyens for a care act that includes delivering an appareillage. **Settle that from Judilibre before writing and do not state the French rule from the insurer's summary**; if the 2013 position cannot be pinned down, the post says the question is unsettled and gives both lines with their dates, which is still better than what the sector publishes. **Spain is the locale with the trap and the one to write most carefully.** The top results are consumer-side and contradict each other outright: the OCU and Consumoteca say three years for the prosthesis as a producto sanitario under consumer law, a patient-facing dental site says two, and five-year commercial guarantees on crowns are described as normal practice. **None of that is a source**: settle it from the TRLGDCU text and from **RD 192/2023** directly, and carry across the distinction Consumoteca at least frames correctly, that the material and the treatment are not the same thing because the practitioner owes an obligación de medios and not de resultado. `declaracion-conformidad-protesis-dental` already covers the conformity declaration and RD 192/2023's ten-year and fifteen-year retention, so **link it and do not re-derive it**; the duplication grep was run this run and «garantía» appears in no published post in this sense. The product mention writes itself and must stay at the end: the guarantee clock starts at insertion, so what the clinic needs is the insertion date, the lab, the batch and the conformity declaration retrievable per piece years later |
| El seguro de responsabilidad civil de la clínica: quién está obligado, por cuánto y qué no cubre la póliza del colegio | `seguro-responsabilidad-civil-clinica-dental` / — / — / — / `berufshaftpflicht-zahnarztpraxis` / — / `ubezpieczenie-oc-gabinet-stomatologiczny` | seguro responsabilidad civil clínica dental obligatorio · berufshaftpflicht zahnarztpraxis pflicht · obowiązkowe OC gabinet stomatologiczny · Added 26 October 2026. **ES, DE and PL, and the three markets answer the same question in three different shapes**, which is the reason to write it three times rather than once. Intent verified this run and clinic-side in Germany (zm-online «Berufshaftpflicht: Mindestsumme wird Pflicht», zwp-online, and the Zahnärztekammer Nordrhein's own Haftpflicht briefing for new members) and in Poland (the Łódź okręgowa izba lekarska publishes on OC for lekarze dentyści, and rp.pl runs «bez ubezpieczenia od odpowiedzialności cywilnej nie ma praktyki lekarskiej»). **Spain is included for the correction, not for the intent**, which came back consumer-side: the duty is art. 46 de la Ley 44/2003 (LOPS), «Cobertura de responsabilidad», read verbatim from the BOE this run, and the Spanish field miscites it — Legalitas attributes the text to «la Ley de Sociedades Profesionales» while quoting art. 46 almost word for word. Three things art. 46 actually says and the field does not: the professional **and** «las personas jurídicas o entidades de titularidad privada que presten cualquier clase de servicios sanitarios» are each obliged, so the sociedad titular has a duty of its own; there is **no national minimum sum**, because «las comunidades autónomas (...) determinarán las condiciones esenciales del aseguramiento», which is why no two Spanish sources agree on a figure and why the post must not invent one; and the colegios «podrán adoptar las medidas necesarias para facilitar a sus colegiados el cumplimiento de esta obligación», which is facilitating it, not discharging it for the clinic. Germany is the opposite case and the figure is in the statute: § 95e Abs. 2 SGB V sets a **Mindestversicherungssumme of 3 Mio € per Versicherungsfall** and forbids limiting the annual aggregate below twice that, so the 3 Mio € the trade press carries from brokers is right and now sourced. § 95e names the Vertragsarzt; **§ 72 Abs. 1 Satz 2 SGB V is the bridge** («Soweit sich die Vorschriften dieses Kapitels auf Ärzte beziehen, gelten sie entsprechend für Zahnärzte»), both read verbatim, so do not write the German copy as if § 95e mentioned dentists. The mechanics are the part a practice can act on: Abs. 1 Satz 3 lets a policy taken out for a Landesrecht or Standesrecht duty count if it meets Abs. 2, Abs. 3 requires the Versicherungsbescheinigung nach § 113 Abs. 2 VVG to the Zulassungsausschuss **including on an application for the Genehmigung einer Anstellung**, which is the employing practice's moment, plus an unverzüglich duty to report lapse or change, and Abs. 4 makes the sanction **Ruhen der Zulassung** with immediate effect. Poland has both a deadline and a figure: art. 25 ust. 2 ustawy o działalności leczniczej, read verbatim from the Dziennik Ustaw, says «obowiązek ubezpieczenia powstaje najpóźniej w dniu poprzedzającym dzień rozpoczęcia wykonywania działalności leczniczej», and ust. 3 gives the kierownik **7 days** from signing to file the documents with the register authority. The sum is **75 000 € per zdarzenie and 350 000 € aggregate**, from § 3 ust. 1 pkt 2 (podmiot leczniczy providing ambulatoryjne świadczenia under art. 8 pkt 1 lit. b) and pkt 3 (indywidualna praktyka lekarska), read from the consolidated rozporządzenie, Dz.U. 2025 poz. 272. **The 100 000 €/500 000 € figure circulating alongside it is the hospital row of the same table** (pkt 1, art. 8 pkt 1 lit. a), not the dental one, which is where the contradiction in the Polish results comes from. § 3 ust. 3 converts the euro sums at the NBP average rate, so the Polish copy states a euro-denominated sum and says so rather than printing a fixed złoty figure. **Written 26 October 2026, and the row needed two corrections and gained one figure.** First, **the Polish 7-day deadline is gone**: art. 25 ust. 3, which this row cites for the kierownik filing the documents within 7 days, reads «(uchylony)» in the current tekst jednolity (Dz. U. z 2026 r. poz. 156, newer than the 2025 poz. 450 an earlier run used). The operative duty is the general one, **art. 107 ust. 1, 14 days** from the day a change in registered data arises, with a kara pieniężna up to ten times the minimum wage under ust. 2 and, for praktyki zawodowe lekarzy, the okręgowa rada lekarska passing that resolution under ust. 3. Neither art. 100 ust. 1 nor art. 101 ust. 1 still lists the insurance document among the application data, and **art. 105 ust. 2a makes an entry change that only presents the insurance document free of charge**, which is the practice-facing fact the post is built on. Second, **the German figure is two figures**: § 95e Abs. 5 Satz 2 extends Abs. 1, 3 and 4 to MVZ and to Vertragsärzte and Berufsausübungsgemeinschaften **mit angestellten Ärzten**, and Satz 3 sets their Mindestversicherungssumme at **5 Mio € per Versicherungsfall** with the annual aggregate not limitable below three times that. With the § 72 bridge that is the employing dental practice, which is most of the readership, and the BZÄK Kommentar names only the 3 Mio €, so the published post gives it as the reading of the two provisions and sends the reader to their own Kammer. Three more things the row did not know, all from the **BZÄK Kommentar zur Musterberufsordnung, 5. Auflage, November 2024**, § 4: the Berufsordnung sets **no** minimum sum at all and the Kommentar says so by contrast with § 95e; an **angestellter Zahnarzt is not covered by the Praxisinhaber's policy** where he is personally (deliktisch) liable, so that is two policies for one treatment and it is the sharpest thing in the German post; and missing cover can reach **Widerruf der Approbation** (VG München, U. v. 11.08.2017, M 16 K 16.398). For Spain the row's «no national minimum» held, and the honest second norm is **art. 11.3 Ley 2/2007**, which obliges a sociedad profesional separately, with art. 11.2's solidary liability as the reason it matters: that is a better correction than naming a blog, because both norms are real and the one usually quoted as «la Ley de Sociedades Profesionales» is art. 46 LOPS text. Written without `translationKey`, per the scheinselbststaendigkeit row: three statutes, three posts, not translations |
| Los permisos antes del primer paciente: autorización sanitaria, registro y lo que el ayuntamiento no te da | `autorizacion-sanitaria-clinica-dental` / — / — / — / — / — / `wpis-do-rejestru-gabinet-stomatologiczny` | autorización sanitaria clínica dental requisitos · wpis do rejestru gabinet stomatologiczny · Added 26 October 2026. **ES and PL**, and in both the correction is the same: the thing a new clinic thinks is the permit is not the permit. Intent verified this run and clinic-side in both. Spain returned official trámite pages rather than blogs (comunidad.madrid's autorización de centros, euskadi.eus's dental-specific Autorización Sanitaria de Clínicas Dentales, a Junta de Andalucía PDF written for dental authorisation specifically, and a Castilla-La Mancha draft orden on the requisitos técnico-sanitarios of clínicas dentales), which is a field held by the regulators themselves. Poland returned the strongest clinic-side evidence available anywhere in this file: **voivodeship inspection protocols of named dental practices**, published by the Poznań, Kielce and Mazowieckie offices, which show both what is checked and what practices are found in breach of. The Spanish hook is national even though the procedure is autonomic: **art. 29.1 de la Ley 14/1986 General de Sanidad** requires «autorización administrativa previa para su instalación y funcionamiento, así como para las modificaciones que respecto de su estructura y régimen inicial puedan establecerse», and **RD 1277/2003** builds on it. Read from the BOE this run, RD 1277/2003 gives the post three things: art. 2.e) defines the autorización sanitaria as the resolution that enables a centre «para su instalación, su funcionamiento, la modificación de sus actividades sanitarias o, en su caso, su cierre», so there are four distinct administrative acts and not one licence; a dental clinic is a named centre type, **C.2.5.1 Clínicas dentales**, with **U.44 Odontología/Estomatología** as its unit, which is the classification every autonomic form asks for; and art. 2.g) makes the registro the record of those authorisations rather than a separate application. Three things worth saying because clinics get them wrong: the authorisation is **previa**, so it is sought before installing and not before opening; a change of structure or of the régimen inicial needs its own authorisation, which is what adding a gabinete or a new unit is; and the **licencia de apertura municipal is a different permit from a different administration** and holding it proves nothing about the sanitary one. Poland's correction is sharper and is a genuine split in the field: **art. 106 ust. 1 ustawy o działalności leczniczej**, read verbatim, makes the register keeper **the wojewoda for a podmiot leczniczy** (pkt 1) and **the okręgowa rada lekarska for a praktyka zawodowa lekarza** (pkt 2). Both answers found this run are therefore right, for different legal forms, and poradnikprzedsiebiorcy conflated them into one. Say it the way it decides something: the legal form chosen on day one decides which register the practice enters and therefore who inspects it later. The inspection protocols also cite a Minister of Health rozporządzenie on the data entered in the register and a **14-day deadline to report changes**, with a practice found in breach of exactly that; both come from the protocols rather than from the rozporządzenie, so **read the rozporządzenie itself before the post states the deadline**. Mexico is the obvious third market (aviso de funcionamiento and the NOM-016 premises rules) and is not in this row because neither its intent nor its provision was checked this run |
| Contratar a un dentista con título de otro país: qué puede hacer antes de tener el título reconocido | `contratar-dentista-titulo-extranjero` / — / — / — / `auslaendischer-zahnarzt-approbation-anstellen` / — / — | contratar dentista título extranjero homologación · ausländischer zahnarzt approbation anstellen · Added 26 October 2026. **ES and DE.** Intent verified this run and clinic-side in Germany, where the question is asked and answered from the employer's side: service.berlin.de and hamburg.de both publish the Approbation procedure as a service a practice's candidate goes through, the Ärztekammer Nordrhein publishes a berufliche-Anerkennung briefing for akademische Heilberufe, and the Ärztezeitung covers how slowly Thüringen grants them. **Spain's intent is the candidate's rather than the employer's** and the row says so: the official page is ciencia.gob.es, which is where the applicant goes, and the clinic's half of it is the same question asked by whoever handles hiring. The German side is where the field is simply wrong. **§ 13 ZHG, read verbatim this run, does not do what the commercial blogs say it does**: the befristete Erlaubnis zur vorübergehenden Ausübung der Zahnheilkunde is expressly **not granted** to an applicant holding an EU, EEA or Swiss dental qualification (Abs. 1 Satz 2), except on the special-interest route of Abs. 1a. The blogs present it as the generic waiting room for any foreign degree; it is the third-country instrument, and the EU case does not need it because recognition is automatic. Two more facts from the same paragraph that decide what a practice can actually offer: the Erlaubnis may be granted or extended only up to **a total of two years** of dental activity (Abs. 2), beyond that only in a besonderer Einzelfall or for reasons of dental care provision (Abs. 3), and it **may be restricted to specific activities and to specific Beschäftigungsstellen** (Abs. 2), which means the permission can be tied to the practice that holds the post. § 2 Abs. 1 ZHG, also read verbatim, is the other half: Approbation requires a dentistry degree of **at least 5 000 hours over at least five years** plus the German zahnärztliche Prüfung and the German-language requirement, with EU, EEA and Swiss Ausbildungsnachweise listed in the Anlage counting automatically in place of Nr. 4. Spain's figures come from ciencia.gob.es and the ANECA Resolución of 31 July 2024: **five years of study and 540 hours of clinical training**, the competences and modules of **Orden CIN/2136/2008**, Spanish at **B2** unless the applicant is a national of a Spanish-speaking state or did 75 % of the degree's teaching hours in Spanish, Directiva 2005/36/CE for direct recognition of EU, EEA and Swiss titles, and the application filed **only** through the Ministry's sede electrónica. **That page cites no Real Decreto**, so do not attribute the Spanish procedure to one without reading it first; cite the ANECA resolution and the orden, which is what the Ministry itself cites. The one fact that makes the post worth reading for an owner is the same in both markets and is easy to state and hard to find: in Germany dental activity without Approbation is allowed **neither in one's own practice nor as an employee**, so the hiring date is the recognition date and not the contract date, and the only bridge is the § 13 route with the limits above **Written 27 October 2026, ES and DE, and four corrections to this row. (1) The ANECA reference is stale: the current criteria are the Resolución signed 20 April 2026 (`Criterios_ANECA_Dentista_abr2026.pdf`), not the one of 31 July 2024. (2) Its apartado Primero sets a second threshold this row never carried, **300 ECTS or 3.480 horas presenciales** alongside the five years, plus the 540 clinical hours. (3) The 'cites no Real Decreto' warning holds for the dentista page only: the parent `validate/homologacion.html` cites **RD 889/2022**, which supplies the two facts the post is built on, art. 5.1 (no retroactivity, effects from the credencial) and art. 18.3 (six months, negative silence). (4) The ES half's clinic-side line is **art. 4.2 of Ley 44/2003**, which requires the title for practice 'por cuenta propia o ajena' and is the exact Spanish counterpart of the German point this row already makes. On the DE side everything this row states was confirmed verbatim from the ZHG; the post adds § 2 Abs. 2 Satz 8 (a rechtsmittelfähiger Bescheid on the wesentliche Unterschiede within **four months** of the file being complete, two under § 81a AufenthG), the Eignungsprüfung/Kenntnisprüfung split, § 13 Abs. 4 (unfinished training) and the Berlin figures (210 / 430 / 150 euros, ca. 3-4 months, B2 plus a C1 Fachsprachentest at the Zahnärztekammer Berlin, and the Einstellungszusage as the document that fixes which Land is competent)** |
| Lo que la inspección de consumo mira en la clínica: hojas de reclamaciones y el precio expuesto | `hojas-de-reclamaciones-clinica-dental` / — / — / — / — / — / — | hojas de reclamaciones clínica dental · cartel precios clínica dental obligatorio · Added 27 October 2026. **Country-locked ES, and it is not the row above about la inspección sanitaria**: a different administration, a different inspector and different norms. Consumo is the one that turns up about the waiting room, and the obligations are autonomic, which is the whole difficulty. Intent verified this run and regulator-side, which is stronger than clinic-side here: the field is held by the administrations themselves. The **Ayuntamiento de Madrid publishes its dental-clinic inspection protocol** (`09 PR_ClinicasDentales2019(v.2).pdf`, read with `pdftotext` this run because WebFetch returns only the compressed streams), and it is the best single source in this row because it is literally the checklist. It states the check verbatim, «Verificar la existencia de las hojas de reclamaciones que correspondan», and it quotes **art. 14.2 de la Ley 11/1998 de Madrid** word for word for the price obligation: «Los precios de los servicios serán expuestos al público en los establecimientos donde se presten u oferten, mediante la exhibición de carteles perfectamente visibles y legibles», with «la relación de los servicios ofertados, el precio de cada uno de» ellos. **The cartel de precios is therefore sourced, but as autonomic law, not national** — do not write it as a Spain-wide rule, which is the mistake waiting to be made. Art. 14.3 of the same law adds the financing disclosure, «el precio total de adquisición bajo esta fórmula de pago» alongside «el precio total de adquisición al contado», which is the row that matters in a clinic selling implants on credit and is a different obligation from the tarifario row above. The protocol's own normativa list is the post's spine: **RDL 1/2007 (TRLGDCU)**, Ley 3/2014, Ley 11/1998, **Ley 16/2011 de contratos de crédito al consumo**, Ley 44/2003 art. 46, RD 1277/2003, RD 1594/1994 and **RD 1907/1996 sobre publicidad y promoción sanitaria**, plus the **Resolución de 20 de enero de 1994 de la Dirección General de Salud**, which approves the hoja de reclamación model for centros sanitarios in Madrid and is the reason the Madrid form is not the generic consumo one. **Andalucía is the second market and answers differently**, from consumoresponde.es, the Junta's own consumer service: the clinic must hold the official **Libro de Hojas de Quejas y Reclamaciones** of the Junta, have it available on request, **announce it with a cartel in a visible place**, and answer within **10 días hábiles**. Two autonomías, two instruments, one post that has to say so instead of picking one. The **Defensor del Pueblo** resolution on informing dental patients of their consumer rights, accepted by the Comunidad de Madrid in 2019, is the evidence that this is enforced and not theoretical. What the post must not do is state a figure or a form for an autonomía it has not read: give the two verified ones, name the instrument in each, and send the reader to their own consejería. The software half is small and honest — the price list, the presupuesto and the financed total are what the clinic has to be able to print on the day — so keep it to one mention and do not inflate it. **Written: 27 October 2026, `es` only.** Five corrections from the writing run. **The art. 14.2 quote in this row was cut short and the missing half matters**: the sentence ends «mediante la exhibición de carteles perfectamente visibles y legibles **o en el lugar donde efectivamente se presten, a través de un soporte escrito**», so a written support at the point of service is an alternative to the poster and the obligation is softer than the quote implied. Art. 14.3 has **four** items, not the two named here: a) precio total aplazado, b) precio total al contado, c) número total de plazos, d) periodicidad. **The Madrid route to the hojas is sanitary, not consumo**: art. 21.e) del Decreto 51/2006 obliges «hojas de reclamaciones y sugerencias ... según los modelos establecidos por la Consejería de Sanidad y Consumo, así como carteles anunciadores de su existencia», the model comes from the Resolución de 20 de enero de 1994, and the municipal protocol then accepts the consumo hojas as a valid substitute when the sanitary ones are missing, which is the practical answer the row lacked. **Andalucía was sourced from the BOJA decree itself, not consumoresponde**: Decreto 472/2019, BOJA 104 of 3 June 2019, where art. 2.d) is the decisive provision (the duty reaches those whom sector rules oblige to hold analogous forms, so a sanitary channel exempts nothing), art. 5.2 gives the cartel its Anexo III model, minimum DIN-A4 and 0,7 cm letters, art. 10.1 the ten días hábiles and the escrito razonado, and art. 11.1 that lacking the sheets neither blocks the complaint nor suspends the duty to answer. **There is a national floor this row did not name**: art. 21.3 del RDL 1/2007 requires an answer «en el plazo máximo de quince días», and art. 21.2 requires a clave identificativa and a written justificante, which is the one genuinely software-shaped obligation in the post. **The find worth reusing is the campaign evaluation, not the protocol**: the Ayuntamiento de Madrid also publishes `09  EV_ClinicasDentales2018.pdf`, which gives the compliance rate of every one of the 38 checklist items (676 inspections, 371 establishments, 84,9 % with an irregularity, and the price list exhibited by only 35,3 %). Published failure rates from the inspecting administration are rare and they carry the post |
| Qué puede hacer un higienista y qué no: el reparto de tareas en el gabinete | `que-puede-hacer-un-higienista-bucodental` / — / — / — / `delegation-zahnarztpraxis-zfa-zmp` / — / — | qué puede hacer un higienista bucodental · delegation zahnarztpraxis zfa zmp · Added 27 October 2026. **ES and DE**, and the two markets answer the same question with the same structure, a closed statutory list plus a reserved core, which is why it is worth writing twice rather than once. Both provisions were read verbatim this run. Spain: **RD 1594/1994**, consolidated text from the BOE, and it is more specific than the field suggests. **Art. 11.2 is an enumerated list** of what the higienista may do — aplicar flúor tópico, colocar y retirar hilos retractores, colocar selladores de fisuras con técnica no invasiva, pulir obturaciones retirando el exceso, colocar y retirar el dique de goma, y eliminar cálculos y tinciones con raspado y pulido — and **art. 11.3 draws the line**, excluding la prescripción de prótesis o tratamientos, la dosificación y prescripción de medicamentos, la administración de anestésicos y los procedimientos operatorios o restauradores, with the work done «como ayudantes y colaboradores» of the dentist. Art. 11.1 and art. 10.1 carry the salud pública half, including recoger datos sobre el estado de la cavidad oral and realizar exámenes de salud bucodental comunitarios. **Art. 3 is the line the post should open on** and the one owners miss: a dental clinic must be «organizadas, gestionadas y atendidas directa y personalmente por un Odontólogo o un Estomatólogo», which is a limit on the clinic's organisation and not only on the chairside task. Arts. 5 to 8 cover el protésico, including the five-year record-keeping duty and the presupuesto previo, worth one paragraph because the task split with the laboratorio is the same question. Germany is the better-documented of the two. **§ 1 Abs. 5 und 6 ZHG** is the enabling provision, and the **BZÄK Delegationsrahmen für Zahnmedizinische Fachangestellte** (Novelliert 16 September 2009, geprüft, version of **Juli 2021**) is the operative document: fetched HTTP 200 from the Zahnärztekammer Nordrhein this run and read with `pdftotext`, so this is a chamber source and not a legal publisher, per the rule above. It gives the post what Spain's decree does not spell out, **the conditions** rather than just the list: the Leistung must be delegationsfähig and must not require höchstpersönliches Handeln, the Mitarbeiterin must be qualified, and the Zahnarzt must personally satisfy himself of that qualification, order the specific Leistung (Anordnung), give the fachliche Weisung, supervise (Aufsicht), and remain «in gleicher Weise persönlich verantwortlich» — plus the one a practice can act on tomorrow, that the Einsatzrahmen is to be set individually per employee and documented **«möglichst schriftlich»**, and that the dentist must be reachable throughout for Rückfragen, Korrekturen oder Komplikationen. The reserved core is explicit and matches Spain's almost item for item: Untersuchung, Diagnosestellung und Aufklärung, Therapieplanung, Entscheidung über sämtliche Behandlungsmaßnahmen, invasive Eingriffe, Injektionen and sämtliche operativen Eingriffe. The qualification ladder (ZFA/ZAH as the floor under the BBiG, then ZMV, ZMP, ZMF and DH widening what may be delegated) is the German-specific part with no Spanish equivalent, and the Delegationsrahmen is **a guideline, not law**, so say so. Two warnings. The Delegationsrahmen's own Rechtsgrundlagen list names the länderspezifische Heilberufsgesetze and the Berufsordnungen der Zahnärztekammern alongside the ZHG, so the German post sends the reader to their own Kammer rather than implying one national answer; and **the subgingival question is the contested one in the German field** — the trade press argues about delegating subgingivale Kürettage to ZMP or DH — so the post states what the Delegationsrahmen and the ZHG say and does not resolve a dispute the sources do not resolve. The software half is genuinely relevant for once and still goes at the end: who may record what, and the per-employee Einsatzrahmen being a written document someone has to keep, which connects to the user-permissions row above. **Not a translation of each other and no `translationKey`** |
| Dentista pode ser MEI? O CNPJ da clínica e o que o Fator R decide | `dentista-pode-ser-mei` in `blog/pt-br/` | dentista pode ser mei · cnpj dentista · **country-locked: BR.** Added 27 October 2026. **This row replaced a Spanish «autónomo o sociedad» target that failed its own intent check this run**, and the replacement is recorded because the failure is the useful part: searches for the Spanish legal-form question returned Brazilian results almost exclusively (jornalcontabil, companyhero, agilize, meucontadoronline, contabilidadezen on the «jornada financeira dentista PJ») plus one UK trade piece, and no Spanish source comparing autónomo with SL. The intent is Brazilian, so the row is Brazilian. Primary source read this run: **LC 123/2006, consolidated text on Planalto**, and it corrects the field's own explanation. **Art. 18-A § 4º does not contain a «profissão regulamentada» exclusion** — the incisos are I, activity taxed «na forma dos Anexos V ou VI desta Lei Complementar»; II, «que possua mais de um estabelecimento»; III, participation in another company as titular, sócio or administrador; and V, «constituído na forma de startup», with inciso IV (contratar empregado) **revogado pela LC 155/2016**. So do not write that the statute bars dentists as a regulated profession, which is how the accounting blogs put it: the bar runs through the **CGSN occupation list**, which art. 2º § 4º-B presupposes by requiring unanimity to remove an occupation authorised to act as MEI. **Read Resolução CGSN nº 140/2018 and its Anexo XI directly before the post states that odontologia is absent from it** — that step was not done this run and it is the post's central claim, so it is the first thing the writing run does. The ceiling is **R$ 81.000,00** of receita bruta in the previous ano-calendário (art. 18-A § 1º, and the page also shows the superseded R$ 60.000,00, so quote the current one). The part worth the post regardless of how the MEI question lands is the **Fator R**, because it decides the rate and almost nobody states it correctly: **art. 18 § 5º-B, inciso XX, «odontologia e prótese dentária»**, places the activity in **Anexo III**, and **§ 5º-M** moves exactly those incisos, XX among them, to **Anexo V** when the ratio of folha de salários to receita falls **below 28 %**, with § 5º-J giving Anexo III at 28 % or above. That is the one number a clinic can act on, it is the reason a pró-labore decision changes the tax bill, and it ties back to inciso I above, since an activity taxed under Anexo V cannot be MEI at all. Also note § 5º-I's older odontologia entry was **revogado pela LC 155/2016**, so a source citing it is out of date. Brazilian Portuguese without hedging per A5, prices in reais, and «este post não é orientação contábil» in the same place the regulatory posts put the legal disclaimer |

**The queues were extended on 3 August 2026 and again on 28 August 2026.**
The August 28 extension added 32 vendors, each verified from its own site
that day, opened the Polish section (A8) and unblocked Mexico, taking
Queue A back to roughly a month of runway at twenty-one comparisons a
week. When a queue runs dry the fallback rule in *Two queues* holds: a
morning run with no comparison left takes a topic, and an afternoon run
with no unwritten topic left takes a comparison instead.

**Both queues were empty again on 16 September 2026.** The three rows added
on 15 September were written the same day, so the 04:00 run on 16 September
found Queue A drained and Queue B complete in all seven locales, and added
the three rows above instead of writing: the retention counterpart to the
records-request row, the periodontal chart beside the odontogram, and the
Spanish receta electrónica privada. Each was verified to have search intent
that run, in more than one market where the row is not country-locked.

**A pattern is now visible and it is worth naming.** On 15 September the 04:00
run extended and the 08:00, 11:00 and 16:00 runs wrote all three of the rows it
had just added, which is exactly what happened again here: the day's first run
publishes nothing and the other three drain what it wrote. That is a stable
loop but an expensive one, because every day in it costs the 04:00 comparison
slot and the site publishes three guides instead. **The fix is a Queue A
extension, and it is the one thing this run cannot do alone**: a vendor row has
to be reached at its own site the day it is added, and A9 is empty, so the
cheap half is the head-term and `alternativa-open-source-a-X` rows that need no
vendor verification: an `alternativa-open-source-a-<incumbent>` row exists for
`es`, `en`, `fr`, `de`, `it` and `pl`, and for none of `pt`,
`pt-br` or `es-mx`, whose incumbents A5 and A2 already name. One gap found the
same day that is not a queue row at all: the install post exists in `es`, `en`, `fr`, `pt`, `de`
and `it` but not in `pl`.

**Queue B was complete in all seven locales at the 16:00 run on 4 October 2026**, checked twice: once by slug against every directory, and once by reading the
`translationKey` of every topic post, which is the stricter test because it
catches a file that exists under a different name. All 106 rows were done in
`es`, `en`, `fr`, `pt`, `de`, `it` and `pl`. So that run took a comparison
instead, per the fallback rule above, and the rotation sent it to French
(13 published comparisons, tied with German, which had no unwritten row left,
while `pl` and `es-mx` at 11 and `pt` and `pt-br` at 12 were all drained).
It wrote Effigie and retired the duplicate Weclever row. Queue A is down to
Optimydent in A1 after that, so the next extension is due.

**The 04:00 run on 17 September 2026 broke that loop, and how it did it is
the part worth keeping.** It found both queues empty for the third day
running, so instead of only extending Queue B it did the expensive half
too: seven vendor rows reached at their own sites that morning, plus the
three cheap head-term rows the paragraph above had already identified.
Queue A went from zero to ten, weighted at the markets the rotation
starves first, `es-mx` (DENTALISTA, DentalSoft, `alternativa-open-source-dentalink`),
`pt` and `pt-br` (Gestrato, NoviGest, EasyDental and two head terms),
`de` (power.dent, Austria), `it` and `pl` (FELG Dent in each). Three
things made it cheap enough to do inside one run, and the next extension
should start from them rather than rediscovering them:

- **Three of the seven were already named in this file** as candidates or
  as blocked rows, so no discovery was needed: Henry Schein One Austria,
  FELG Dent and EasyDental were each sitting in a "checked and not added"
  note waiting for exactly this.
- **A plain fetch is not a verdict on a vendor.** `tactis.pt` answers 403,
  `gestrato.pt` and `easydental.com.br` answer 503 and 403, and all three
  serve the full site to a browser user agent. EasyDental had been kept
  out since 28 August on the strength of that 403 alone. Before writing a
  vendor off as unreachable, retry it the way the tab32 and UNO rows
  already document.
- **The rest came from one search per market** for what a clinic there
  actually types, then a fetch of each candidate's own site. Two
  candidates failed that check and are recorded as not added, which is
  the point of doing it: Globalsoft's clinic product turned out not to be
  a dental one, and kiCLINIC renders everything in JavaScript and
  publishes nothing a post could cite. **The Globalsoft half of that was
  wrong and was corrected on 2 October 2026**: its dental product is
  Oralcloud, it is dental-specific with an odontograma, and it now has a
  row in A5. The check read a general healthcare page and stopped, which
  is the cost of writing a multi-vertical vendor off from one fetch.

That leaves the comparison rotation with roughly three weeks of runway
again and, more to the point, with something to take in `es-mx`, `pt`,
`pt-br` and `pl`, the four directories that had none. The Polish install
post is still missing and is still not a queue row.

**That runway lasted two days, and the arithmetic is worth writing down
rather than rediscovering.** By the 16:00 run on 19 September 2026, eight
of the ten rows added on the 17th had been written and Queue A was down to
**two** unwritten targets, power.dent in `de` and FELG Dent Italia in `it`
(Trophy Gestion stays parked and is not counted). Queue B went to zero the
same run, when `plan-tratamiento-dental-por-fases` was written in all seven
locales. Ten comparison rows are not three weeks at twenty-one comparisons
a week, they are three days: the runway of an extension is its row count
divided by three, and only a Queue A extension of roughly sixty rows buys
the month that paragraph assumed. The next run to find a queue empty should
extend by the method in the three bullets above rather than treating it as
an unusual event, because at this cadence it is the normal one.

**Both queues were empty again at the 16:00 run on 20 September 2026, and
that run is the one that proves the paragraph above.** The ten rows added
on 17 September were gone in three days: the 04:00, 08:00 and 11:00 runs
that day wrote the last three (`alternativa-open-source-simples-dental` in
`pt-br`, FELG Dent Italia in `it`, power.dent in `de`), so the afternoon
run found Queue A at zero and Queue B complete in all seven locales for
the fourth time in six days. It extended both instead of writing.

Two things that run established and the next extension should not
rediscover:

- **The cheap half of a Queue A extension is now exhausted.** All nine
  directories have a head-term row and all nine have an
  `alternativa-open-source-a-<incumbent>` row, the last three having been
  added on 17 September for `pt`, `pt-br` and `es-mx`. Every further
  Queue A row from here costs a vendor reached at its own site, so the
  "cheap way to extend" sentence below is spent and an empty-queue run
  should budget for the expensive half from the start.
- **One search per market then a fetch of each candidate's own domain
  still works, and it still rejects candidates.** Seven rows were added
  that way, weighted at the three emptiest comparison directories: `pl`
  (IQ Dental, Medyc, Gabinet Stomatologiczny), `pt-br` (Prontuário Verde,
  Dentista Organizado, Simples Agenda) and `pt` (SISINFO). Two candidates
  were rejected and are recorded above rather than silently dropped:
  DentDoc/DentFlow in Mexico, which publishes a price over an unedited
  Divi placeholder footer and no RFC, and App Clínica in Portugal, which
  verifies but sells dentistry as one specialty among several. The 503
  and 403 retry mechanic from 17 September earned its keep again:
  `medyc.pl` answers 503 to a plain fetch and serves the whole site to a
  browser user agent.

Seven rows is still barely two days of comparison runway at three
comparisons a day, and Queue B's three new rows are three afternoons.
**The arithmetic has not changed and neither has the fix**: a Queue A
extension of roughly sixty rows is what buys a month, and no single run
has yet attempted one at that size. A run that finds both queues empty
should expect to spend its whole session on vendor verification.

**Both queues were empty again at the 11:00 run on 23 September 2026, and
this time it was a morning run that found it.** The three rows added on
20 September were written on the 21st and 22nd, so Queue A was at zero for
the fifth time in nine days and Queue B was complete in all seven locales,
and the morning fallback (a comparison run takes a topic) had nothing to
take either. That run added the three Queue B rows above and wrote nothing.

Two things it established that the next empty-queue run should not
rediscover:

- **The state check is arithmetic and takes one command, so do it before
  picking anything.** Queue B completeness in Polish is not visible from
  the table, because most rows name no Polish slug: count instead. Queue B
  holds 75 rows that are not country-locked, so every directory should
  carry those 75 plus its own country-locked rows plus the install post,
  and this run every one of them did: `es` 80 (four ES-locked), `en`,
  `fr`, `de` and `it` 78 (two each), `pt` 76 (none), `pl` 76 (KSeF, and
  **no install post**, which is the only gap in the nine and is still not
  a queue row). Counting comparison slugs the same way gave es 15, en 23,
  fr 10, pt 9, de 13, it 12, pl 11, pt-br 13, es-mx 7 with every row in A1
  to A8 already written, so the language rotation had nothing to rotate to
  in any of the nine.
- **The three Queue B rows cost one search each and no vendor fetch**,
  which is the whole reason a morning run can do this and still not fix
  anything. Each was verified in two markets that run and each turned out
  to be a different question in the second market, which is the property
  worth selecting for: the German Überweisung is not the Spanish
  derivación, the Polish korekta of a paragon is not the Spanish
  rectificativa, and the German Schmerzpatient is not the Spanish urgencia.
  A row that survives that test is seven posts; a row that does not is one
  post translated six times.

**Queue A is still the one at zero and no run since 20 September has
touched it.** Three Queue B rows buy three afternoons and leave nine
morning slots with nothing to write, so the next run that finds this
state should spend itself on A rather than on B, by the method in the
three bullets from 17 September.

**The 16:00 run on 24 September 2026 did that, and it is the first
empty-queue run to spend itself entirely on Queue A.** The three rows
added on the 23rd were written by the 04:00, 08:00 and 11:00 runs that
same day, so Queue B went back to zero in under twenty-four hours,
exactly as the arithmetic above predicts, and this was the sixth empty
queue in ten days. **An afternoon run finding both queues empty adds
Queue A rows, not Queue B rows**, whatever the general fallback says: the
afternoon slot is one post a day and the morning slots are three, so B
rows buy the cheaper thing. It added **eleven verified vendor rows** —
Dentasys and MN program (`es`), XDentalCloud (`es-mx`), Oremia and Agatha
Seven (`fr`), DenPro (`pt`), claire (`de`), OpenStudio, DrawDent Desktop
and Horizon Blue (`it`), CALDIS (`pl`) — which is under four days of
comparison runway and does not change the conclusion that only a
sixty-row extension buys a month.

Four things it established that the next extension should not
rediscover:

- **Search-result market-share tables are a trap in France specifically,
  and they cost two fetches to clear.** Every French aggregator reprints
  the same table putting Owandy at 35,68 % and Visiodent at 21,61 % of
  the éditeur market, which reads as two missing leaders. Neither is a
  row: `owandy.fr` redirects to `owandy.com/fr`, which sells imaging
  hardware and Quickvision, not a PMS, and `visiodent.fr` redirects to
  `visiodent.com`, which is the publisher of **Veasy**, already queued.
  Both are recorded in A4. **The publisher's name is not the product's
  name**, and the same mistake nearly added Medware in Mexico, which is
  OdontoWare's publisher. Check this section for the product before
  adding what looks like a vendor.
- **The most valuable rows this run are the ones with a licence shape we
  do not have.** DrawDent Desktop sells a €79 perpetual Windows licence,
  Horizon Blue sells modules à la carte at €197-317/anno, Agatha Seven
  sells the software bundled with a mini PC at 1.490 € ttc, and
  OpenStudio publishes a genuinely free tier capped at 30 patients.
  Those make better posts than another cloud subscription, because
  section 6 writes itself and the honest answer is sometimes theirs.
  Weight a search toward `licenza perpetua`, `licence`, `jednorazowo`
  and `kostenlos` rather than only the head term.
- **A vendor's own page is where a withdrawal shows up, and only there.**
  MedicHub ranks first for the Italian head term on several aggregators
  and its own page says "Da agosto 2026 MedicHub non è più in vendita".
  One fetch caught it before a post existed. That is the clearest return
  on the rule this whole file is built on, and it is an argument for
  verifying at the moment a row is *added*, not only when it is written.
- **kiCLINIC is settled, not pending.** Three checks, three times
  unreadable. It is recorded in A5 as closed to general extension runs so
  a fourth run does not spend a fetch on it.

**Both queues were first empty on 15 September 2026.** Every Queue A target in A1 to A8 exists in its market's
directory (Trophy Gestion parked, Dentra removed), and every Queue B row
exists in all seven locales or in the single directory a country-locked row
names. The 04:00 run that day had no comparison to take and no topic either,
so it added the three Queue B rows above instead of writing, each one a search
verified to have intent that run. **Queue A is the one still at zero**: it
needs vendor rows, and those cannot be added without reaching each vendor's
own site, so the cheap extension is the head-term and
`alternativa-open-source-a-X` rows that need no vendor verification.

**Queue B was complete again on 26 September 2026**, in all seven locales and
in every directory the country-locked rows name, so the 16:00 run took a
comparison instead, exactly as the fallback rule in *Two queues* says an
afternoon run should. The rotation put French lowest with work left (twelve
published comparisons, against ten in `pt` and eight in `es-mx`, neither of
which has an unwritten target), and the one French row left was Trophy
Gestion, parked since 6 September. It read normally this run and was written.
**The parking rule earned its keep and so did the retry**: three blocked runs
were right to stop retrying every rotation, and the row was still worth one
more attempt once it was the only thing standing between a locale and its
next post. A parked row is not a removed row.

**Extending Queue A cannot be done from memory.**
Every new vendor row must be verified from the vendor's own site the day it is
added — name, domain reached, market evidence — exactly like the existing
rows were (and two of the first draft's rows still turned out to be wrong; see
*The comparison queue rotates by language*). Head-term and
`alternativa-open-source-a-X` rows need no vendor verification and are the
cheap way to extend. When either runway drops under three weeks, extend the
queue again the same way: real searches a dental clinic makes, verified to
have intent behind them this run, not invented from memory. A target that only
makes sense in one country goes in country-locked, with the country named.

**Both queues were empty again on 30 September 2026, and this time it was a
morning run that found it.** Every Queue A row in A1 to A8 exists in its
market's directory (Dentra removed, Trophy Gestion no longer parked since the
26th), and every Queue B row exists in all seven locales or in the single
directory a country-locked row names, checked row by row against the
filesystem with `pl` included rather than inferred from the commit log. The
rotation had `es-mx` lowest at six published comparisons and `pt` next at
eight, and neither has an unwritten target left, so there was nothing to skip
to and no topic to fall back on either. The run added the three Queue B rows
above and wrote nothing, and **it checked intent in two languages per row
rather than one**, which is worth keeping: it is what established that the
financing row is Factoring in Germany and a financiera in Spain, and the
cancellation row has real primary sources in German and none in Spanish. Two
of the three rows also came back with a specific thing not to repeat — the
unsourced "30 % of first visits are lost on the phone" statistic, and the
Spanish habit of publishing a cancellation fee as if it were settled law.

**Queue A is still the binding constraint and still at zero, for the third
extension in a row.** Three Queue B rows buy three afternoon runs; the three
morning runs a day will take those same rows as topic fallback and drain them
inside a day, exactly as the 23 September run recorded. The next run that
finds both queues empty should spend itself on A whatever its hour, and start
with the head-term and `alternativa-open-source-a-X` rows, which need no
vendor verification. Adding vendor rows means reaching each vendor's own site,
and that is the only way A grows.

**Both queues were empty again on 1 October 2026, and this run did the
expensive half.** The check was the same as 30 September and gave the same
answer, row by row against the filesystem with `pl` included: every Queue A row
in A1 to A8 exists in its market's directory, the only Queue A slug missing
anywhere is the struck-through `dentalpin-vs-dentra`, and every Queue B row
exists in all seven locales or in the single directory a country-locked row
names. The rotation found `es-mx` lowest at eight published comparisons and
`pt` next at ten, and neither had an unwritten target, so there was nothing to
write and nothing to fall back on. **This was the fifth consecutive extension
in which Queue A was the binding constraint**, so this run spent itself on A
first, as the paragraph above told it to, and wrote no post.

**The cheap half of Queue A is now exhausted, and that supersedes the standing
advice above.** The head-term and `alternativa-open-source-a-X` rows that need
no vendor verification exist in all nine directories: `-gratis` in every one,
and an `alternativa-open-source-a-<incumbent>` in every one since the
17 September run closed `pt`, `pt-br` and `es-mx`. There is no cheap row left
to add. **From here A grows only by reaching a vendor's own site**, so an
empty-queue run that does not do that does not extend A at all, which is what
makes the loop this file has recorded five times self-perpetuating.

Three vendor rows were added, each verified at its own domain this run and
**weighted at the two directories the rotation starves**: Luna Salud in A2 for
`es-mx`, the lowest at eight, and msTITANIUM and Dentoral in A5 for `pt`, next
at ten. So the next three comparison runs have somewhere to go, starting with
`es-mx`. Four things worth carrying forward:

- **The browser-user-agent retry paid again.** `mstitanium.com` answers HTTP
  503 to a plain fetch and serves the whole site, tariff and AT certificate
  number included, to a browser user agent. That is the fourth vendor to
  behave this way after tab32, Gestrato and NoviGest, so the retry is now the
  default and a plain fetch failure is never the verdict.
- **A single-page application is still a hard no, and now there is a second
  case.** `tuasistentedental.com` returns one 8,5 kB shell for the apex, the
  terms and the privacy policy alike. Its JSON-LD does publish a Mexican
  tariff, which is more than kiCLINIC offers, and it is still not a product
  page: recorded in A2 as a lead for a run that can render JavaScript, not as
  a row.
- **Two scope exclusions held on re-check**, which is what makes them settled
  rather than pending: App Clínica in A5 and Agenda Clínica in A2 both still
  publish no dental page and no odontograma. Re-checking them cost two fetches
  and the condition for moving either up is unchanged.
- **One lead found and deliberately not added: Optimydent**, a Spanish dental
  PMS that Doctoralia's own press pages name as an integration partner with a
  bidirectional API. Nothing about it has been verified at its own domain, so
  it is a candidate for A1 and not a row. Verify it there first. It is logged
  here because the 17 September run established that candidates already named
  in this file are what makes the next extension cheap.

The three Queue B rows added this run kept the two-languages-per-row practice:
the erasure row was checked in Spanish and German and came back with different
retention periods and a different substitute mechanism in each, which is why
it is sourced nationally rather than translated; the booking-portal row in
Spanish and German, where the brand changes but the four questions do not; the
foreign-patient row in Spanish and Polish. Each also came back with a specific
thing not to repeat, which is the pattern worth keeping: no flat retention
number across locales, no platform's own booking or no-show figures, and above
all not the widespread assumption that the European Health Insurance Card pays
for planned private dental work.

**Both queues were empty again at the 16:00 run on 2 October 2026, the seventh
time in eighteen days, and the loop ran exactly as the 1 October entry
predicted it would.** That run added three Queue A rows and three Queue B rows;
the 08:00 and 11:00 runs on the 1st and the 04:00 run on the 2nd wrote the
three comparisons, and the 16:00, 08:00 and 11:00 runs wrote the three guides.
Six rows, six runs, under thirty-six hours. The check this run made was the
same arithmetic the 23 September entry prescribes and it took one command:
Queue B holds 89 rows that are due in all seven locales and every one of them
exists in all seven (`pl` counted by `translationKey` rather than inferred,
which is the only way to see it, since the table names no Polish slug; the
Polish install post that the 17 and 23 September entries both flag as the one
gap in the nine is now there). Twelve further rows name a single directory and
all twelve exist; the remaining six are the country-locked compliance rows and
the EU AI row, which name between one and six locales each, and those are
complete too. **The only Queue A slug missing anywhere is the struck-through
`dentalpin-vs-dentra`.** **So this run wrote no post and spent
itself on Queue A**, per the standing instruction that an afternoon run finding
both queues empty adds A rows whatever the general fallback says.

**Nine vendor rows were added, each reached at its own domain this run**, and
weighted at the directories the rotation starves: `es-mx` lowest at nine
published comparisons gets three (Consultorio Dental Pro, Dentiqa, SIGA
Dental), `fr` at twelve gets three (DrSanté, Weclever Dental, Effigie), `pt` at
twelve gets Oralcloud, `pl` at eleven gets MedEQ, and `es` gets Optimydent,
which was a logged candidate rather than a discovery. That is three days of
comparison runway, not the sixty-row month this file has now asked for four
times, and the reason is in the next paragraph rather than in the method.

Five things worth carrying forward, three of them new failure modes:

- **A candidate already named in this file is still the cheapest row
  available.** Optimydent was logged on 1 October as a lead with nothing
  verified; one fetch this run turned it into a row. It also demonstrated why
  the lead was only a lead: the bidirectional API that made it interesting is
  claimed by **Doctoralia's** press page and appears nowhere on Optimydent's
  own site, so the row carries an explicit instruction not to state it as
  theirs.
- **Poland's long tail is mostly not reachable, and that is a finding rather
  than a bad run.** Six Polish candidates were checked and one survived.
  `incomdent.eu`, `www.programdlastomatologa.pl` and `mpstomatolog.pl` **have
  no DNS at all** (checked with `getent hosts`, not only through a fetch), so
  incomDENT and BinSoft's dental edition are out under the rule that a vendor
  unreachable at its own site is not a row, even though aggregators quote both
  with prices (incomDENT at "800 PLN lifetime + 200 PLN/year", mpGabinet at
  "1000-2100 PLN per workstation"). `mpgabinet.pl`, the medical sibling,
  resolves fine, which is what makes the dental domain's absence meaningful.
- **`dr100.pl` is the first case of a domain that resolves and still cannot be
  reached**, so it is a different animal from the 503/403 vendors and needs its
  own note. DNS gives 146.59.68.214 and every attempt, plain and with a browser
  user agent, dies as a connection reset mid-exchange. **Do not write it off**:
  LekSeek Polska is a real publisher and third parties quote a full złoty
  tariff (Basic 1680 zł/rok, Standard 3360, Premium 4560). It is a row for the
  first run that can reach it, and it is logged here so that run does not have
  to rediscover it.
- **An aggregator name is not a vendor, and this run caught three ways that
  bites.** `dentoro.pl` is quoted everywhere as a dental program "from 65 PLN
  net/month" and its apex is a **patient-education site about dental
  treatment**, not a product; **Konibo** is listed in French comparisons at
  "70-100 € par mois" and is an equipment and IT reseller that installs LOGOSw
  and Julie, so it is the Owandy/Visiodent trap with the arrow reversed, a
  reseller mistaken for a publisher; and **Simpliteca**, whose pricing table
  supplied most of the Polish candidate names, is a marketing agency. The
  method still works, because every one of these failed at the vendor's own
  domain, which is where it is supposed to fail.
- **Three rejections on scope, all the App Clínica pattern**, recorded so they
  are settled rather than re-checked: **Aurero** (Medily, Łódź) sells to
  medical practices of every specialty with dentistry as a use case and puts
  its price behind a calculator; **Mendov** (Portugal) is general healthcare
  with no odontograma and no published price; **DOC.PT** publishes a clean
  tariff (19,90 / 39,90 / 59,90 € per month) and serves ten specialties with no
  odontograma, which makes it the most tempting of the three and still not a
  dental PMS. **Cegid Vendus** is invoicing and POS, not practice management.
  `dentosapp.lovable.app` is a `lovable.app` subdomain, not a vendor's own
  domain, and is out on that alone.

**The arithmetic has not moved and the binding constraint has not changed.**
Nine rows is three days. The honest reading of this run is that the expensive
half of a Queue A extension is not just expensive, it has a low yield: twenty
candidates were checked to produce nine rows, and the four markets that need
rows most are the four where the remaining candidates are thinnest. A
sixty-row extension is not nine runs of this, it is a different activity, and
the next run to find both queues empty should consider spending the session on
one market exhaustively rather than one search per market across four.

**The 16:00 run on 3 October 2026 was the first afternoon run to use the
reverse fallback, and it is worth recording that it worked.** Queue B was
complete in all seven locales plus the two variants (checked row by row
against the filesystem, not by counting), so the run took a comparison
instead, as *Two queues* says it should. The rotation sent it to `es-mx`,
which had 11 published comparisons against `pl` and `pt` on 12, and the next
unwritten row there was SIGA Dental. Two things that make the next such run
cheaper: the per-row check is worth scripting rather than eyeballing, because
Queue B's table has no Polish column and `pl` has to be verified by count
(91 topic files against 91 due rows) rather than by slug; and the row's own
"verified" note was four days old and already wrong in three places, so a
queue row is a pointer to a vendor, never a cache of its facts.

**Both queues were empty again at the 08:00 run on 5 October 2026, the eighth
time in twenty days, and this is the first entry written by a morning run that
found nothing to compare.** The check was scripted this time rather than
eyeballed, which the 3 October entry asked for, and it is worth keeping: one
pass over the Queue A sections resolving each row's market directory, and one
pass over the Queue B table resolving the six named slugs plus `pl` by
`translationKey`, since the table still has no Polish column. The answer was
unambiguous. Every Queue A row in A1 to A8 exists in its own market's
directory, the only slugs the script reported missing were the struck-through
`dentalpin-vs-dentra`, the two English head terms in A1 that sit in `en/`, the
Portuguese head term that sits in `pt/`, and five backticked technical tokens
inside prose (`utf-8`, `www`, `onmouseover` and friends) that a slug regex
cannot tell from a target. Every Queue B row exists in all seven locales, or in
the single directory a country-locked row names. The rotation found `es-mx`
lowest at ten published comparisons, then `pl`, `pt` and `pt-br` at eleven, and
**not one of those four sections had an unwritten target**, nor did any of the
remaining five, so the fallback in *Two queues* sent this run to Queue B and it
was complete too.

**Scripting the check is now the recorded method, and the two passes belong in
the scratchpad of every empty-queue run.** It cost two short scripts and
replaced the row-by-row reading that the 1 and 3 October runs both did by hand,
and it caught something eyeballing would have reported as a gap: the two rows
the Queue B script flagged, the Italian STS row and the Spanish
`historia-clinica-electronica-dental`, are both country-locked and both
complete, and only looked incomplete because the script adds `pl` to every row
by default. **A flagged row is a question, not a finding**, and both answers
were in the row's own third column.

Three Queue B rows were added, each verified for intent this run and each
checked in two markets, which is the practice the 1 October entry established
and the reason all three are marked sourced-nationally rather than translated.
Every one of the three came back with the same shape of finding, a line a
clinic believes is universal and is not:

- **The third-party records request.** Spanish guidance says hand over a copy
  and keep the original; German law makes the first copy free under
  § 630g BGB, requires a Schweigepflichtentbindung before a private insurer
  sees anything, and can compel the **originals** to a court or its appointed
  expert. The flat sentence "always a copy, never the original" is wrong in one
  of the two markets checked.
