- **The abandoned treatment.** The documentation standard travels (it is the
  alta voluntaria standard, and Spain publishes it in Ley 41/2002), the
  **billing does not**: Germany has KZV Teilleistungen, a markable HKP, a duty
  to stop the lab and Festzuschüsse paid by how far the work got, and Spain has
  no equivalent instrument at all. Any percentage in this post is national.
- **The before and after photo.** Spain's AEPD fine (PS/00130/2024, 10.000 €)
  is about the **scope** of a consent that existed, while Italy restricts the
  before/after format itself as pubblicità sanitaria, so a valid consent is not
  the end of the question there. "Get a signature and you are covered" is the
  exact advice this row exists to contradict.

One candidate was checked and **deliberately not added**, and it is logged so
the next extension does not pay for it twice: **patient membership or
subscription plans**. The English-language intent is real and there is a whole
vendor category behind it (DentalHQ, BoomCloud, Subscribili and others sell
little else), but the Spanish check came back dominated by consumer insurance
comparison and affiliate pages rather than by clinics wanting to run a plan,
with `recepcionista.com` among the results, and this file bars it. A row that
ranks in one locale and nowhere else fails the standard in *Which languages a
post gets written in*: if you cannot name the query it would rank for, do not
write it. It comes back if a future run can name clinic-side intent for it in a
second market, and it is probably an `en`-first row rather than a seven-locale
one.

**Queue A is still the binding constraint and has now been at zero for seven
consecutive extensions.** This run did not extend it, because the operative
instruction for an empty-queue run is three Queue B rows, and because the
standing advice in this file is already specific about why A is expensive: the
cheap half is exhausted, every new row means reaching a vendor's own site, and
the 2 October run spent twenty candidate checks to produce nine rows. Three
Queue B rows buy three runs, and the three morning runs a day will take them as
topic fallback and drain them within a day, which is the loop this file has now
recorded eight times. **The thing that actually breaks it is a Queue A
extension of roughly sixty rows, spent on one market exhaustively rather than
one search per market**, and the four markets that need it are `es-mx`, `pl`,
`pt` and `pt-br`, in that order, which is the rotation's own order and has not
changed in a week.

**The 04:00 run on 6 October 2026 wrote the before/after row in all seven locales, and
three things in that row needed correcting against the sources.** Queue A was at zero
again, so the morning run took Queue B per the fallback, and the row it took was the last
of the three added on 5 October. With it written, **both queues are empty from the next
run onwards**: Queue A has no unwritten row outside the struck-through
`dentalpin-vs-dentra`, and Queue B now exists in all seven locales for every row. The next
run does not need to re-derive that, it needs to extend A.

Four findings worth carrying forward, because the row was confident about things that
turned out to be different:

- **The AEPD case is not a dental clinic, and saying so matters.** PS/00130/2024
  (EXP202403915) sanctions the owner of a **medicina estética** practice who published
  another doctor's surgical before/after on his clinic's Facebook and Instagram. The 10.000 €
  is two fines of 5.000 €, one under art. 6.1 RGPD and one under art. 9. What transfers to
  dentistry is the reasoning, not the facts, and the post says which is which. The quotable
  part is the consent's own wording, apartado 6, "con fines médicos científicos o
  educativos", which the AEPD holds valid "solo para 'fines de avances en la educación
  médica', no para fines de publicidad comercial".
- **France, not Italy, holds the hardest line, and the row did not know it.** The ONCD's
  own *Recommandations et explicitations* (version 19.06.2026, under décret n° 2020-1658 of
  22/12/2020) state that professional secrecy "reste un principe à valeur absolue dont le
  patient ne peut délier le praticien", and that a patient's face "doit être flouté même si
  le patient souhaite donner une autorisation expresse de diffusion de son image". That is a
  jurisdiction where consent is explicitly **not** sufficient, which is a stronger version of
  the point the row was built on. Légifrance answers 403 to a plain fetch and to a browser
  user agent alike, so French statute text has to come through the Ordre's own documents or
  not at all: do not cite a LEGIARTI or JORFTEXT id that has not been fetched.
- **Germany runs the other way, and the honest post says so.** § 11 Abs. 1 S. 3 Nr. 1 HWG
  bans before/after only for the § 1 Abs. 1 Nr. 2 c "operative plastisch-chirurgische
  Eingriffe ... ohne medizinische Notwendigkeit", and the BZÄK's own MBO commentary states
  that since the 2012 HWG reform "Gleiches gilt für die sog. Vorher-Nachher-Darstellungen",
  i.e. no longer forbidden. So the German post cannot be a translation of the Italian one:
  what binds there is Art. 9 DSGVO, § 203 StGB and § 21 MBO, and § 7 Abs. 2 MBO expressly
  lets the patient release the practitioner from confidentiality, the opposite of France.
- **Poland is the mirror case: the ethics loosened, the statute did not.** The new KEL
  (annex to uchwała nr 5 of 18 May 2024, in force 1 January 2025) dropped the blanket
  advertising ban and art. 71 now permits "informacja o oferowanych usługach", while art. 14
  of the ustawa o działalności leczniczej still says "Treść i forma tych informacji nie mogą
  mieć cech reklamy". Any Polish post that cites only the KEL gets this backwards.

One thing in the row was **not** written, deliberately: the Garante's 5.000 € research-ePoster
case. The 20.000 € one is solid (provvedimento n. 769 of 12 December 2024, docweb 10095836,
arts. 5 and 9 GDPR plus art. 2-septies comma 8 Codice privacy), but the ePoster case could not
be reached at the Garante's own site this run, only in press coverage, so it was dropped rather
than softened. The Italian post stands on the 769 provvedimento plus art. 1 comma 525 of legge
145/2018, whose text is quoted verbatim in the FNOMCeO Commissione Albo Odontoiatri letter,
because Normattiva and the Gazzetta Ufficiale both serve article text only through JavaScript.

Two mechanics worth reusing: **`api.sejm.gov.pl/eli/acts/DU/<year>/<pos>/text.html` serves
Polish consolidated statute text as fetchable HTML**, which is the cheapest primary source
found so far for any locale; and **a product paragraph has to be checked against what other
posts already claim**, because the first draft of these seven invented "versioned consent
documents with a purpose field", which nothing in the repo supports. What the published posts
do claim is consents in the patient record with author and date, a timeline showing when the
patient was informed and when they signed, and images attached under the same permissions and
access log as the rest of the record.

**Both queues were empty at the 08:00 run on 6 October 2026, the ninth time in
twenty-one days, and this is the first run to find them empty with no
derivation left to do.** The 04:00 run four hours earlier wrote the last of the
three rows added on 5 October and said so in its own entry, so this run
re-ran the two scripted passes only to confirm the handover rather than to
discover anything, and they confirmed it: every Queue A row in A1 to A8 exists
in its own market's directory, the rotation's four hungriest directories
(`es-mx` at ten published comparisons, then `pl`, `pt` and `pt-br` at eleven)
have no unwritten target between them, and every Queue B row exists in all
seven locales or in the single directory a country-locked row names. **Queue A
has now been at zero for eight consecutive extensions.**

Two notes on the scripted check, which is cheap enough now that it should stay
the method:

- **Counting `dentalpin-vs-` prefixes undercounts `fr` by one.** The French
  Gesden comparison is `dentalpin-face-a-gesden`, not `dentalpin-vs-gesden`, so
  a prefix count reads `fr` as twelve when it is thirteen. It did not change
  the rotation this run, since `es-mx` was lowest either way, but a run that
  ties on twelve will pick the wrong language.
- **`pl` still has to be derived, and the arithmetic now has a check digit.**
  Queue B holds 110 rows, seventeen of them country-locked, so 93 are due in
  `pl` plus the one PL-locked row, which is 94; `pl` holds 107 files of which
  thirteen are Queue A (eleven comparisons plus `darmowy-program-dla-gabinetu-
  stomatologicznego` and `alternatywa-open-source-dla-estomed`), leaving exactly
  94. When those two numbers agree, `pl` is complete without opening a file.

Three Queue B rows were added, each with intent verified this run and each
checked in two markets, and all three came back with the same shape of finding
that the 5 October entry recorded: a sentence clinics believe is universal and
is not. What is different about this set is that two of the three markets pairs
disagree **structurally** rather than numerically, which is the strongest
argument this file has yet produced for sourcing nationally.

- **The camera on the wall.** Spain has no dental-specific statute and a real
  enforcement record: AEPD `EXP202305156` fined a dental clinic over eleven
  cameras, one of them inside each of five gabinetes, on **art. 5.1.c) RGPD**
  minimisation alone — the recordings had no audio and were kept seven days,
  so a clinic doing most things right still lost on **where the camera
  pointed**. Poland arrives at the same place from the opposite direction:
  **art. 23a of the ustawa o działalności leczniczej** writes its
  treatment-room limb only for szpitale, ZOL, ZPO, rehabilitation facilities
  and hospicja, so a gabinet stomatologiczny has no basis under it at all and
  is left with pomieszczenia ogólnodostępne and a three-month cap. "Is the
  camera proportionate" and "does the statute let you have one" are different
  questions and the row says which market asks which.
- **Cash at the front desk.** This is the file's clearest case of two markets
  giving **opposite** answers. Spain caps cash at **1.000 €** where either
  party is an empresario o profesional (art. 7 Ley 7/2012 in its Ley 11/2021
  wording, verified from the BOE this run), aggregates everything a plan was
  fractioned into, fines **25 %** of the base and makes **payer and receiver
  jointly liable**, so the clinic is a sujeto infractor rather than a witness.
  Poland's **art. 19 Prawo przedsiębiorców** bites only between two
  przedsiębiorcy above **15.000 zł**, so a patient may pay any amount in cash
  and the Polish post is about documentation, not a ceiling. A treatment plan
  paid monthly in cash is the ordinary dental case and in Spain it is one
  operation, not twelve.
- **The channel, not the consent.** Spain's own authority is narrow and
  permissive — AEPD **FAQ-1638** addresses email only and resolves it with a
  condition, encryption — while the **LZK BW Merkblatt "Versand von
  Patientendaten" (8/2020)** is flat that most messengers including WhatsApp
  are unsuitable, requires the **attachment** encrypted at AES-256 with the
  password out of band, and adds the detail that carries the post: **the
  subject line and body are never encrypted, so no patient name goes in
  either**. It also names the fully compliant low-tech answer, handing the
  patient the referral and radiograph on CD or USB at the desk.

Three sourcing facts established this run and worth not paying for twice:

- **`api.sejm.gov.pl/eli/acts/DU/<year>/<pos>/text.html` worked again and is
  now two for two**, serving `Prawo przedsiębiorców` (2018/646) cleanly. Its
  limit is that it serves the **text as published**, not the consolidated one,
  which is why art. 23a could not be read out of `2011/654`: it was added by a
  later amendment. For an article added after enactment, search first and read
  the statute only to confirm.
- **The COEM data-protection document (`coem.org.es/media/news/pdf/NRPD.pdf`)
  is pre-RGPD guidance from 2018 and mentions neither WhatsApp nor email
  transmission.** It looks like the obvious Spanish professional source for the
  messaging row and it is not one. Recorded so the next run does not fetch it.
- **`sede.agenciatributaria.gob.es/Sede/iva/limitacion-pagos-efectivo.html`
  404s** and `diariodarepublica.pt` returned no readable text to a plain fetch.
  The BOE consolidated view works but needs `offset` past 100.000 characters to
  reach art. 7, which is the mechanic to reuse for any long BOE text.

One candidate was checked and **deliberately not added**, on the same test that
rejected membership plans on 5 October: **patient complaint handling**, the
hoja de reclamaciones and Beschwerdemanagement question. German clinic-side
intent is real and is in fact mandated, since the G-BA's
Qualitätsmanagement-Richtlinie requires a Beschwerdemanagement in every
practice, and the trade press (zm-online, iww, zwp-online) serves it properly.
The Spanish check did not hold up: it returned consumer-side and news results
about the Dentix and iDental collapses plus law firms selling defence to
clinics, which is patient intent and lawyer intent rather than a clinic asking
how to run the process. It comes back if a future run can name clinic-side
intent in a second market, and like membership plans it is probably a one- or
two-locale row rather than a seven-locale one.

**The constraint this file has now recorded nine times is not the Queue B
extension, and this run did not fix it.** Three Queue B rows are three runs,
and with three comparison runs a day taking topic targets as fallback they are
gone inside a day. Queue A is empty, which means **the comparison cadence has
stopped**, and no amount of Queue B extension restarts it: twenty-one of the
twenty-eight weekly targets are comparisons and there is nothing left to
compare. The operative instruction for a morning run that finds both queues
empty is three Queue B rows, so that is what this run did, but the thing that
actually needs to happen is unchanged from the 2 October entry and is now
overdue: **a Queue A extension of roughly sixty rows, spent on one market
exhaustively rather than one search per market**, in the rotation's own order,
`es-mx`, `pl`, `pt`, `pt-br`. The 2 October run's yield figure is the planning
number to use: twenty candidate checks produced nine rows, so sixty rows is a
session of its own and not the tail end of a content run.

**Both queues were empty again on 7 October 2026**, and this is the ninth
consecutive extension to record it. The 08:00 run confirmed it the same way
the 6 October run did, by deriving both queues from the filesystem rather
than trusting this file: Queue A is **130 live targets across A1-A8 and 130
published**, counting per directory (`es` 18, `es-mx` 12, `en` 20, `fr` 14,
`pt` 11, `pt-br` 13, `de` 14, `it` 15, `pl` 13), with the two struck rows in
A4 excluded; Queue B is **113 rows and every one complete in every directory
it was due in**. The only row that reads as incomplete under a naive check is
`reglamento-ia-clinica-dental`, whose English cell is `—`, so English is not
due and the row is done. Three rows added, each verified for search intent
this run and each checked in two markets.

- **Amalgam, and the thing every vendor blog gets wrong.** The EU layer is
  settled and verified from two official sources: **Regulation (EU) 2024/1849**
  amending 2017/852 means that from **1 January 2025** dental amalgam "should
  not be used for dental treatment in the Union, except when deemed strictly
  necessary by the dental practitioner based on the specific medical needs of
  the patient", with export banned from the same date, manufacture and import
  from **1 July 2026**, a derogation to **30 June 2026** for Member States
  where amalgam is the only material publicly reimbursed at **90 %** or more
  and mercury-free reimbursement was not yet possible on 1 January 2025, and a
  Commission review of the use exemptions by **31 December 2029**. The half of
  the row worth writing is the other one: **art. 10 of Regulation (EU) 2017/852
  binds facilities where amalgam fillings or teeth containing them are
  *removed*, not only where amalgam is placed**, so the separator, the 95 %
  retention level and the authorised-collector duty survive the use ban and
  will outlive it by decades. A post that treats 2025 as the end of the
  subject is wrong in the direction a clinic will act on.
- **Advertising, where two markets disagree about whether promotion is
  allowed at all.** Italy's **art. 1 comma 525 l. 145/2018**, verified verbatim
  from the FNOMCeO letter of 6 October 2022, permits only information
  "funzionali a garantire la sicurezza dei trattamenti sanitari, escluso
  qualsiasi elemento di carattere promozionale o suggestivo", enforced by the
  Ordine disciplinarily under comma 536 with referral to AGCOM. Spain allows
  promotion and regulates its content instead, and the COEM decálogo of
  **23 July 2025** is unusually useful because every point is footnoted to a
  named statute: Ley 34/1988 art. 3.e), TRLGDCU art. 20.1.c) for the total
  price of a complete treatment, RDL 1/2014 art. 80.7 barring brand advertising
  of professional-only health products to patients, RD 1591/2009 art. 38.8 for
  the celebrity prohibition, Ley 16/2011 for financing, and **Ley 44/2003
  disposición adicional segunda**, under which Spain recognises no dental
  specialities so nobody may advertise as "especialista". **The trap in that
  source is recorded in the row**: four of its ten points rest on **Ley 11/1998
  of the Comunidad de Madrid** and are autonomic, not Spanish, law.
- **The jornada register, duty without a statute.** Spain's **art. 34.9 ET**,
  read verbatim from the BOE consolidated text, is explicit: a daily register
  with the concrete start and end time per worker, kept **four years**,
  available to the worker, their representatives and the Inspección de Trabajo,
  and organised by convenio or acuerdo de empresa or else by the employer after
  consulting the representatives. Germany arrives from the same CJEU judgment
  (**C-55/18, CCOO v Deutsche Bank SAE**, Grand Chamber, 14 May 2019) at the
  opposite place: the **BAG, 13 September 2022, 1 ABR 22/21** derived the duty
  from **§ 3 Abs. 2 Nr. 1 ArbSchG** because the legislator had not acted,
  binding every employer regardless of a works council while leaving the *form*
  unlegislated. That the CJEU case came out of **Spain** is the detail that
  makes the pair legible: the same judgment produced a statute with a retention
  period in one market and a court-made duty with no prescribed system in the
  other. A German practice asking what it must buy still has no legal answer,
  and that gap is the post.

Four sourcing facts established this run and worth not paying for twice:

- **`eur-lex.europa.eu` answers HTTP 202 with an empty body**, to `curl` and to
  a plain fetch alike, on the HTML view, the PDF view and the `/eli/` endpoints.
  It is a bot gate, not an outage, and it means the Official Journal was not
  readable this run. The official EU substitutes that did work are
  **`oeil.europarl.europa.eu`** (the Parliament's Legislative Observatory
  summaries) and **`environment.ec.europa.eu`**; use them to establish a fact
  and quote the articles from EUR-Lex when it is reachable.
- **`legislation.gov.uk/eur/...` serves retained EU law as readable text**,
  which is how art. 10 of 2017/852 was read this run. It is sound for the
  article as adopted and unsafe for anything after Brexit, so it must never be
  the source for the 2024 amendment or for the English post's own position.
- **A PDF that defeats a plain fetch usually yields to `pdftotext`.** Both the
  FNOMCeO letter and the COEM decálogo came back as binary noise through the
  fetch tool and extracted cleanly from the saved file, which is how the
  verbatim comma 525 text and the decálogo's statutory footnotes were obtained.
  This is now the default second attempt for any chamber or college PDF.
- **The Bundesarbeitsgericht press page 404s** at the obvious slug for the
  Arbeitszeiterfassung decision, so the holding was taken from legal and dental
  secondary sources and the German post must cite the decision itself from the
  BAG's decision database by docket number. **`gesetze-im-internet.de` answered
  HTTP 503 again**, now three runs in a row counting the videovigilancia row's
  attempt at § 4 BDSG, so § 16 ArbZG went unread and no retention figure for
  Germany is recorded here. Treat that host as unreliable and plan a second
  German source for any statute.

One candidate was checked and **deliberately not added**, on the same test that
rejected patient complaint handling on 6 October and membership plans on
5 October: **professional liability insurance for the practice**. Spanish
clinic-side intent is real and the obligation is genuine, but the German and
Italian checks returned broker and comparison-site results rather than a
clinic asking what it has to hold, and the row has no software surface at all
beyond a renewal date, which is the weakest reason this file accepts for a
seven-locale guide. It does not come back unless a run can name clinic-side
intent in a second market.

**The constraint this file has now recorded ten times is unchanged and this run
did not fix it either.** Queue A is empty for the ninth consecutive extension,
which means the comparison cadence has been stopped for over a week:
**twenty-one of the twenty-eight weekly targets are comparisons and there is
nothing left to compare**, so three of the four runs each day now fall through
to Queue B and three Queue B rows are consumed in a single day. Adding Queue B
rows is the operative instruction for a morning run that finds both queues
empty, and it is also the thing that keeps the real problem invisible. The
overdue item is unchanged from the 2 October entry: **a Queue A extension of
roughly sixty rows, spent on one market exhaustively rather than one search per
market**, in the rotation's own order, `es-mx`, `pl`, `pt`, `pt-br`. The
2 October yield figure remains the planning number, twenty candidate checks for
nine rows, so sixty rows is a session of its own and cannot be the tail end of
a content run. **This needs a human decision, not another extension**: either a
dedicated Queue A research session is scheduled, or the four daily runs should
be re-pointed at Queue B explicitly so the cadence stops pretending to be three
comparisons and one guide.
**Both queues were empty at the 08:00 run on 8 October 2026, the tenth consecutive
extension to record it, and the second morning comparison run in four days to find
nothing to compare.** The check was the scripted one the 5 October entry made the
method, and it is now cheap enough to be worth stating as two numbers. Pass one
resolved every Queue A row in A1-A8 against its own market's directory: the only
slugs reported missing were the two struck-through rows in A4,
`dentalpin-vs-dentra` and the retired duplicate `dentalpin-vs-weclever-dental`,
plus the A5 rows, whose table carries no slug column and which were checked by
vendor name instead and are all present. Pass two resolved the Queue B table:
**116 rows, of which 98 are due in all seven locales, 12 name a single directory
and 6 are country-locked or otherwise name fewer than six, and not one was
missing a file anywhere.** The `pl` check digit the 6 October entry introduced
agreed exactly: `pl` holds 113 files, 14 of them Queue A, leaving 99 topic files
against 98 seven-locale rows plus the one PL-locked row. The rotation put `es-mx`
lowest at 11 published comparisons with `pl`, `pt` and `pt-br` on 12, and none of
those four sections had an unwritten target, so the fallback in *Two queues* sent
this run to Queue B and it was complete too.

Three Queue B rows were added, each with intent verified this run in two markets
and clinic-side in both, and each central statute read from a primary source
rather than trusted. All three came back with the shape of finding this file has
now recorded four extensions running, a sentence a clinic believes is universal
and is not, and two of them came back with something better: **a named statute
that says the opposite of what the trade press says it says.**

- **The dead patient's record.** Spain answers with a *category* of requester and
  Germany with an *interest* that has to be asserted, which is a structural
  difference and not a procedural one. Art. 18.4 Ley 41/2002 admits the personas
  vinculadas por razones familiares o de hecho unless the deceased prohibited it;
  § 630g Abs. 3 BGB admits the Erben only for vermögensrechtliche Interessen and
  the nächste Angehörige only for immaterielle ones. The finding worth the row is
  inside Spain rather than between the two: **art. 3 LOPDGDD says the deceased's
  prohibition "no impide que los herederos accedan a los datos de carácter
  patrimonial del causante"**, so the same prohibition closes the clinical record
  and leaves the ledger open, and a clinic applying one rule to both gets one of
  them wrong.
- **The conformity declaration for a prosthesis.** The EU frame is common and the
  duties are national, which is an unusual shape for this queue, and the sources
  disagree with each other in a way that is itself the post. **§ 87 Abs. 1a SGB V
  does not say the declaration is handed to the patient**: read this run, it
  requires the Rechnungslegung to carry a copy of the Laborrechnung and an
  "Erklärung nach Anhang XIII Abschnitt 1 der Verordnung (EU) 745/2017". The
  German dental press repeats the patient version, and the fifteen-year retention
  everybody quotes traces to a Landeszahnärztekammer document and not to a
  statute. On the Spanish side the top search result, the AEMPS prótesis dentales
  page, is **dated 19 May 2010 and pre-MDR**: it is a trap rather than a source,
  and the live text is RD 192/2023, whose own retention rule is ten years and
  fifteen for implantables.
- **The patient who does not pay.** The same decision runs through a court
  procedure in Spain and through a data-protection gate in Germany. The Spanish
  half turns out to be a records post: **art. 815.3 LEC** lets the court propose a
  lower requerimiento where the documentation does not support the amount, and
  because a dental treatment is a contract between an empresario o profesional and
  a consumidor the clauses are screened for abusividad **before** the requerimiento
  issues, so the clinic's own paperwork is examined first and the claim is capped
  by what it proves. The German half is the § 203 StGB question nobody writes
  about: the trade press carries the Mahnwesen properly and is **silent** on
  whether the file may go to an Inkassobüro at all, which is the gap the row
  tells the writing run to close from a chamber or an authority and never to
  infer. One detail with a software surface on top: § 286 Abs. 3 BGB gives the
  thirty-day Verzug against a consumer only where the invoice itself flagged it,
  so an invoice template decides whether the rule applies.

Four sourcing facts established this run, one of them the most useful mechanic
found for `boe.es` so far:

- **The BOE consolidated PDF plus `pdftotext` is the route for a long statute,
  and it is what finally read the LEC.** `boe.es/buscar/act.php?id=BOE-A-2000-323`
  serves a **7,5 kB navigation shell** and nothing else, with or without the `p`
  and `tn` parameters and at every offset, so the 7 October note about needing
  offsets past 100.000 characters does not apply to this one: there is no body to
  offset into. `boe.es/buscar/pdf/<year>/BOE-A-<year>-<num>-consolidado.pdf`
  returns the whole consolidated text as a PDF, and `pdftotext -layout` turned
  2 MB of it into 1,5 M characters in which arts. 812 to 818 read cleanly. **Try
  that URL pattern first for any long BOE text.**
- **`dejure.org` is the working substitute for `gesetze-im-internet.de`**, which
  this file has now recorded as HTTP 503 for three runs. `dejure.org/gesetze/BGB/630g.html`,
  `/BGB/286.html` and `/gesetze/SGB_V/87.html` all served the provisions this run.
  It is a private publisher, not an official gazette, so it is sound for reading a
  provision and establishing what to look for, and the post should still cite the
  statute rather than the site.
- **`eur-lex.europa.eu` is still gated and the 7 October finding holds.** HTTP 202
  with an empty body on the HTML view of CELEX:32017R0745. **MDR Annex XIII is
  therefore unread in this file**, which is recorded in the row itself because two
  secondary sources describe it differently and neither is safe to repeat.
- **The long-BOE problem is not uniform.** Ley 41/2002 and the LOPDGDD both served
  their article text from the ordinary `act.php` view on a plain fetch, and the
  LOPDGDD needed only the standard offset note. It is specifically the very long
  codes that come back as a shell, so test the `act.php` view before paying for a
  PDF.

One candidate was checked and **deliberately not added**, and unlike the last
three rejections this one failed on duplication rather than intent: **the 72-hour
data-breach notification**. The intent is real and clinic-side in every market,
but `ransomware-clinica-dental` and `rgpd-clinica-dental` both already carry the
seventy-two hours and the notification, seven mentions of brecha in the first and
six in the second, so a third post would cannibalise two that already rank.
**That is a cheaper check than the two-market intent test and it should run
first**: grep the published Spanish posts for the candidate's central term before
verifying intent for it anywhere. It is the first rejection in this file made on
the filesystem rather than on a search engine.

**The constraint is unchanged for the tenth time and this run did not fix it
either, because the operative instruction for an empty-queue run does not permit
it.** Queue A is empty, so the comparison cadence has now been stopped for over a
week: twenty-one of the twenty-eight weekly targets are comparisons, three of the
four daily runs fall through to Queue B, and three Queue B rows are consumed
inside a day. The 2 October yield figure is still the planning number, twenty
candidate checks for nine rows, so the overdue item cannot be the tail end of a
content run: **a Queue A extension of roughly sixty rows, spent on one market
exhaustively rather than one search per market**, in the rotation's own order,
`es-mx`, `pl`, `pt`, `pt-br`. This has been recorded in four separate entries and
asked for as a human decision in two. Until that decision is made, the honest
description of this routine is **four topic runs a day**, not three comparisons
and a guide, and the three morning runs will keep reporting a comparison queue
that has nothing in it.

**Done 8 October 2026, as the human decision asked for above:** 35 Queue A rows
(10 `es-mx`, 6 `pl`, 8 `pt`, 11 `pt-br`, every vendor verified that day on its
own site) and 26 Queue B rows (all with the duplication grep and a two-market
intent check, several of them DE + FR only where Spanish intent was thin, as each
row says). Three Queue B candidates found by two independent searches
(whitening, botox and hyaluronic acid, the equipment log) went in once. The
morning runs have comparisons again, and an empty-queue run should not be
needed for weeks.

**Both queues were empty again at the 08:00 run on 25 October 2026, the eleventh
consecutive extension to record it, and the first since the 8 October Queue A
extension of 35 rows was consumed.** That extension bought seventeen days, which
is the measured answer to the question the last four entries argued about: at
three comparison runs a day, 35 comparison targets last about two and a half
weeks. Both queues were derived from the filesystem rather than trusted, by the
scripted method the 5 October entry made standard. Queue A: every slug-bearing
row in A1-A4 and A6-A8 resolved against its own market's directory, 124 of them,
with no miss, the two struck rows in A4 excluded by the `~~` wrapper; A5 carries
no slug column and was checked by vendor name, 17 Portugal rows against
`blog/pt/` and 22 Brazil rows against `blog/pt-br/`, all present, plus the four
head terms. The rotation ran its full tie-break and found nothing: `de` and `fr`
tied lowest at 12 published comparisons each, `de` wins that tie-break and A6 is
complete (12 vendors and both head terms), `fr` likewise, then `it` at 13, `es`
at 16, `pl` at 17, `pt` at 19, `es-mx` at 20, `en` at 21 and `pt-br` at 22, every
one of them complete. Queue B: 145 rows, every one present in every directory it
was due in.

**The `translationKey` frontmatter field is the cheap way to check Polish and
this entry is recording it, because the Queue B table has no `pl` column and
three previous entries reached for a count-based check digit instead.** Every
`es` post is the origin and carries no key; every other locale's copy carries
`translationKey: <the Spanish slug>`. So mapping the key across the nine
directories answers coverage per target directly, with no arithmetic. Run that
way, exactly one row reads as incomplete, `reglamento-ia-clinica-dental` with no
`en` copy, and its English cell is `—` because neither the US nor the UK is bound
by Regulation (EU) 2024/1689, so the row is done. Use this check in place of the
`pl` file-count digit.

Three Queue B rows were added, each with the duplication grep run first against
the published Spanish posts (the 8 October entry's cheaper-first rule, which
worked: it is what confirmed «legionela» appears in no post and «garantía» in
none in the warranty sense), then a two-market clinic-side intent check, then the
central provision read from a primary source. All three came back with the shape
this file now expects, a sentence the profession repeats that the statute does
not support, and two of them came back with an official source saying the
opposite of the trade press.

- **The water in the chair.** The Spanish half was read verbatim from the BOE and
  is settled: RD 487/2022 anexo I.14 names «sistemas de agua a presión en
  tratamientos dentales», art. 2.11 makes a centro sanitario an instalación
  prioritaria, and art. 9.4 therefore expects a PSL rather than the PPCL a clinic
  would otherwise choose under art. 7.1. **The German half is where the row is
  worth writing and it inverts the trade press twice**: water behind a safety
  device in a dental unit is Betriebswasser and outside the TrinkwV, and the
  KRINKO 2006 recommendation every German article treats as binding has not been
  listed among the RKI's current recommendations since early 2021, per the
  Niedersächsisches Landesgesundheitsamt, which is a state health authority and
  not a publisher.
- **The collaborator who invoices.** Two markets, two unrelated tests, which is
  why it is a DE + FR row and not a seven-locale one. Germany turns on
  weisungsgebunden and eingegliedert under § 7 Abs. 1 SGB IV with the § 7a
  Clearingstelle route; France does not use subordination for this at all, and
  the ONCD's own note makes requalification turn on whether the collaborator can
  build a clientèle personnelle **de droit** and **de fait**. The useful mechanic
  is § 7a Abs. 5, a one-month filing window that converts retroactive liability
  into a forward-dated start, and it is the kind of deadline a practice can still
  act on.
- **The guarantee on a prosthesis.** Germany has a hard statutory figure,
  § 136a Abs. 4 Satz 3 SGB V's zweijährige Gewähr, and the same paragraph
  explains the confusion around it by saying § 195 BGB bleibt unberührt: the
  two-year Gewähr and the three-year Verjährung are different clocks. Spain has
  no equivalent and the field is held by consumer-side sources that contradict
  each other at two, three and five years, so the Spanish copy has to be built
  from the TRLGDCU and RD 192/2023 rather than from the top of the results page.

Three sourcing facts established this run:

- **`gesetze-im-internet.de` is intermittent rather than down, and retrying is
  worth it.** `/sgb_4/__7a.html` returned 200 on the first attempt in the same
  batch where `/sgb_5/__136a.html` returned nothing at all; the identical request
  for § 136a then returned 200 twice in a row moments later. Four previous
  entries in this file record the host as 503 and reach for dejure instead. **Try
  it twice before substituting a legal publisher**, because the official text is
  worth one retry.
- **`boe.es/buscar/act.php?id=<id>&tn=0` served RD 487/2022 in full at 256 kB**,
  so the 8 October finding holds that it is specifically the very long codes that
  come back as a navigation shell. Strip the tags locally and grep the result; the
  consolidated-PDF route was not needed here.
- **The ONCD PDF route works and is the France answer again.** The note on
  collaboration contracts came back as binary through the fetch tool and
  extracted cleanly with `pdftotext -layout`, confirming the 7 October mechanic
  for chamber and college PDFs. With Légifrance still at 403, the Ordre's own
  notes and model contracts remain the French primary source.

One candidate was checked and **deliberately not added**, and it failed the
two-market test from the opposite direction to the usual: **the employment status
question in Spain and Italy**. It is not rejected as a topic, it is already a row
above, but the Spanish and Italian copies of it were considered and dropped.
Spain returned redaccionmedica pieces on a TSJ Madrid judgment and a CESM call
for inspections, which is press and union intent; Italy returned partita IVA and
regime forfettario guides for a dentist setting up, with nothing on whether a
collaborator is a disguised employee. Both come back if a later run can name
clinic-side intent, and the Spanish one probably will, because the owner-side
question plainly exists even though the results page is held by news.

**The structural point is unchanged and this run could not fix it either, but it
is now measurable.** The 8 October extension added 35 Queue A rows and they were
gone in seventeen days, which means **a Queue A extension buys about a day of
runway for every two rows added**. Three morning comparison runs a day against a
catalogue that is, by this run's own count, exhausted in all nine directories, is
not a cadence that an extension of the size this file keeps asking for will fix
for long: sixty rows is under five weeks. The honest description of this routine
remains **four topic runs a day**, and the decision asked for in two previous
entries is still the one that matters — either Queue A is extended on a schedule
of its own, or the 04:00, 08:00 and 11:00 runs are re-pointed at Queue B
explicitly so that three runs a day stop opening by searching a catalogue that
has nothing left in it. A run that spends its first several minutes proving the
comparison queue is empty, for the eleventh time, is spending them on the wrong
thing.

**Both queues were empty again at the 08:00 run on 26 October 2026, the twelfth
consecutive time.** Queue A was derived from the filesystem and is complete in
all nine directories: the rotation ran, `de` came back uniquely lowest at 12
published `dentalpin-vs-` slugs, and A6 turned out to have all fourteen of its
rows written, so the count that chose it was the wrong count. **A comparison
count that greps only `dentalpin-vs-` and `alternativ` undercounts every
section that holds a head term**, which is all of them: A6's real total is 14,
not 12, once `zahnarztsoftware-kostenlos` and
`open-source-alternative-zu-dampsoft` are included. The rotation still picked
the right section, but by luck. Count a section's rows against its own
directory, not a slug prefix against a directory. Checked that way all 165
slug-bearing rows in A1-A8 exist, A9 is empty, and Queue B was complete at 148
rows in every directory each row named.

**The slug column was widened to seven positions this run**, which is recorded
above the table. It was six, written before Polish launched, and that shape
cannot express a row due in Polish and in some but not all of the other six.
The first of the three rows added below is exactly that row, so the column had
to change before it could be written honestly rather than by leaving the Polish
locale out or implying six-locale breadth.

Three Queue B rows were added, each with the duplication grep run first, intent
verified clinic-side in two markets this run, and the central provision read
from a primary source. **The cheaper-first grep paid for itself again and this
time by rejecting a candidate, not confirming one.** A fourth row was drafted on
whether a dental clinic has to issue an invoice to every patient, which looked
like a clean "the statute says the opposite" row because art. 3.1.a) of RD
1619/2012 dispenses much of art. 20 from invoicing. It is already written:
`iva-tratamientos-dentales` has a section called *Estar exento no te libera de
facturar* that reaches the same provision, names the apartados excluded from the
dispensa and cites art. 6.1.j) on the exemption reference. **Grep the published
posts for the provision, not only for the topic word**, because that row's topic
word is «IVA» and the overlap was in its body.

- **The insurance the clinic is obliged to hold.** Three markets, three shapes,
  and that is the row rather than a problem with it. Spain has the duty and no
  figure: art. 46 LOPS obliges the professional **and** the legal entity
  separately, and then hands the «condiciones esenciales del aseguramiento» to
  the comunidades autónomas, which is the verifiable reason no two Spanish
  sources agree on a sum. Germany has the figure in the statute, 3 Mio € per
  Versicherungsfall under § 95e Abs. 2 SGB V with the annual aggregate not
  limitable below twice that, and the sanction is Ruhen der Zulassung rather
  than a fine. Poland has both a figure and two deadlines, 75 000 €/350 000 €
  and the duty arising the day before activity starts with 7 days to file the
  documents. **The Spanish field miscites its own norm**: Legalitas quotes art.
  46 nearly verbatim and attributes it to the Ley de Sociedades Profesionales.
- **The permits before the first patient.** The Spanish procedure is autonomic
  and the hook is not: art. 29.1 Ley 14/1986 requires authorisation «previa»
  for instalación, funcionamiento and modificaciones, and RD 1277/2003 names
  C.2.5.1 Clínicas dentales and U.44 as the classification every autonomic form
  asks for. The Polish half resolves a real split: art. 106 ust. 1 makes the
  wojewoda the register keeper for a podmiot leczniczy and the okręgowa rada
  lekarska for a praktyka zawodowa, so both answers in the field are right for
  different legal forms and the popular guide conflated them.
- **Hiring a dentist whose degree is from somewhere else.** § 13 ZHG read
  verbatim contradicts the commercial blogs that present the befristete
  Erlaubnis as the waiting room for any foreign degree: it is expressly **not**
  granted on an EU, EEA or Swiss qualification, caps at two years in total, and
  can be tied to named Beschäftigungsstellen. The owner-facing fact both markets
  share is that there is no lawful interim: in Germany dental activity without
  Approbation is allowed neither in one's own practice nor as an employee.

Four sourcing facts, two of them corrections to earlier entries in this file:

- **`gesetze-im-internet.de` behaved exactly as the 25 October entry predicted,
  and the retry rule is now worth stating as a rule.** `/sgb_5/__95e.html` and
  `/sgb_5/__72.html` returned 200 to curl on the first attempt; `/zhg/__2.html`
  and `/zhg/__13.html` were both reset on the first attempt and both returned
  200 on the second, in the same batch. **Always retry once before concluding
  anything about this host**, and prefer curl plus local tag-stripping to
  WebFetch, which caps quotes too short to quote a provision.
- **`boe.es/buscar/act.php?id=<id>&tn=0` answered twice, at 272 kB and 173 kB.**
  Ley 44/2003 and RD 1277/2003 both came back whole. Note that the `Artículo 46`
  string matches the navigation index before it matches the article, so grep for
  the article **and** a word from its body rather than taking the first hit.
- **`eli.gov.pl/eli/DU/<year>/<pos>/ogl/pol/pdf` answered twice and is the
  Poland route**, confirming the 24 October entry. The ustawa o działalności
  leczniczej came back at 579 kB and the consolidated OC rozporządzenie at
  171 kB, both clean under `pdftotext -layout`. **Reach for the rozporządzenie
  when a Polish figure is contested**: it is what showed that the
  100 000 €/500 000 € sum circulating for dental practices is the hospital row
  of the same table.
- **`ciencia.gob.es` answers through WebFetch and is the Spanish source for
  recognition of foreign degrees.** Worth recording what it does *not* say: it
  cites the ANECA criteria, Orden CIN/2136/2008 and Directiva 2005/36/CE, and no
  Real Decreto at all, so a post should not attribute the procedure to one.

One candidate was checked and **deliberately not added**: whether a defibrillator
is compulsory in a dental clinic in Spain. It fails on the law rather than on the
intent. RD 365/2009 is the state framework and it governs the use of DESA
**«fuera del ámbito sanitario»**, which a clinic is not; the obligation to have
one, where it exists, is autonomic and only a handful of comunidades impose it,
on premises defined by public concurrence rather than by being healthcare. The
honest post is seventeen regional answers and a national provision that does not
apply, which is a worse read than it looks and not what the search intent is
reaching for. **It comes back if a state-level rule appears**, and the sibling
question that is worth a row one day is medical-emergency preparedness as a
whole, which is professional-body guidance in every market rather than statute.

**The structural point, for the twelfth time, with one new number.** Three
morning comparison runs a day now open by proving a 165-row catalogue is empty
in all nine directories, and the 8 October extension's runway measured at about
a day per two rows added. This run also found that the one cheap half of a
Queue A extension named in the 16 September entry, the head-term and
`alternativa-open-source-a-X` rows for `pt`, `pt-br` and `es-mx`, **has since
been filled**, so that suggestion is spent and there is no cheap half left: every
further Queue A row needs a vendor reached at its own site the day it is added.
The decision is unchanged and is not one a run can take on its own: either
Queue A gets an extension schedule of its own, or the 04:00, 08:00 and 11:00
runs are re-pointed at Queue B, which is what they have actually been doing for
twelve days.

## Where the routine publishes and flags

Nothing in this list stops a publish. The post goes to main like any
other; the point is that the user hears about it, because each one is a
signal beyond the post itself:

- A competitor claims something about DentalPin that needs answering.
- A target has shut down, been acquired, or changed model. Write the
  post against the current reality, and check whether an already
  published post about that vendor went stale too.
- The honest verdict is "their product is a better fit for most
  readers". Publish it anyway, and say so in the notification, because
  it is a product signal, not a content problem.
